“There is just one other point, Mr. Arbuthnot,” the Inspector went on, and he told the lawyer about the cheques, marked X. Upon these, however, the latter could throw no light.

===Chinese===
「还有最后一点，阿巴思诺特先生。」探长接着说，随即把那些标着 X 的支票讲给律师听。可这一层，后者也说不出丝毫端倪。

===Original===
“And you know of no one who had a quarrel with Sir William, or who for any reason might desire his death?”

===Chinese===
「再就是，您知道有什么人同威廉爵士结过怨，或者出于任何缘故巴望他死吗？」

===Original===
“No one. Quite the opposite. Sir William was universally liked and respected.”

===Chinese===
「没有。恰恰相反，威廉爵士是到处受人爱戴敬重的。」

===Original===
Tanner was silent for a moment, considering if he had obtained all the information he was likely to get from the solicitor. Deciding he had, he rose.

===Chinese===
坦纳沉默了片刻，掂量着从这位律师嘴里还能不能问出别的什么。断定再没有了，他便起身。

===Original===
“Well, Mr. Arbuthnot, let me express my gratitude for the way you have met me. I am sorry for having been such a nuisance.”

===Chinese===
「好了，阿巴思诺特先生，承您这般倾诚相待，请容我道一声感激。搅扰您这半日，实在抱歉。」

===Original===
“All in the day’s work, Mr. Tanner,” the solicitor returned as he shook hands and bowed his visitor out.

===Chinese===
「分内之事罢了，坦纳先生。」律师一面握手，一面躬身送客。

===Original===
Tanner left Lincoln’s Inn, and after making a call at the Yard, took the next train back to Halford.

===Chinese===
坦纳出了林肯律师学院，到苏格兰场转了一遭，便搭下一班车回哈尔福德去了。

## V · Inspector Tanner Becomes Convinced / V · 坦纳探长深信不疑

===Original===
After dinner that same evening Inspector Tanner, having lit his pipe, and selected the most comfortable armchair he could find, set himself to take stock of his position, and see just where he stood with his new case.

===Chinese===
当晚用罢晚餐，坦纳探长点燃烟斗，又拣了所能寻见的最舒服的一张扶手椅坐下，着手盘点自己的处境，看看这桩新案子里自己究竟走到了哪一步。

===Original===
He realised that the lawyer’s communication contained food for thought. Certainly a lot of the information he had gained seemed to point in a rather unmistakable way to Austin. That the latter had murdered his father he felt it hard to believe, and yet he had known men to be convicted on slighter evidence than that he already held. Absentmindedly pressing down the tobacco in his pipe, he closed his eyes, and tried to view the facts he had learnt in a proper perspective.

===Chinese===
他意识到，律师那一番话里大有值得深思之处。的确，他到手的种种信息，有不少似乎明白无误地指向奥斯汀。要说后者竟谋害了亲生父亲，他实觉难以置信；然而他见过有人被定罪，凭的证据比他手中这些还要单薄。他心不在焉地按了按斗钵里的烟丝，闭上眼睛，力图把已知的种种事实放到恰如其分的远近来看。

===Original===
Here was a son who had never been able to get on with his father, so much so that they could not live in the same house. To the father he had been a continual disappointment, and no doubt that irritation would show in the father’s manner, and could not but increase the bad blood between them. It was true they had agreed to differ, and Sir William had allowed Austin £1,000 a year, but agreeing to differ did not necessarily prevent very unpleasant feelings on both sides, and as for the money, though it seemed handsome at first sight, it was very small compared to what Sir William might have paid without missing it.

===Chinese===
瞧瞧这么一个儿子：从来与父亲处不来，闹到连一幢屋子都住不进同一扇门。在父亲眼里，他是一而再、再而三的失望；那份懊恼必定形于做父亲的言谈举止，也就免不了加深父子间的嫌隙。诚然，父子俩讲好了各执己见、互不相犯，威廉爵士也允给奥斯汀每年 1,000 英镑；可是「各执己见」未必就能挡住双方心里那股很不痛快的滋味。至于钱，乍看固然出手豪阔，可比起威廉爵士就是拿了出来也不觉得肉痛的数目，实在算不得什么。

===Original===
From what Tanner had seen of Austin and his villa, he thought the latter must be living at the rate of well on to a thousand a year. That was to say, nearly at the rate of his income. Under these circumstances he falls in love and decides to marry. The lady would have but a small dot, if any. The two of them must therefore live on what had before been enough for Austin only. What does Austin do? He sees his father the day after the engagement, probably to tell him the news, and possibly to ask for an increased allowance. As in the ordinary course of nature a large portion of Sir William’s money would soon become his in any case, this would not be an unreasonable demand. But what does Sir William answer? Tanner could only surmise, but from what the dead man had said to Mr. Arbuthnot it was probable he had not only refused the increase, but had threatened altering his will adversely to Austin if the marriage took place. If, as appeared possible from the words overheard by Innes, Sir William had said anything derogatory to the lady, Austin’s feelings, already considerably aroused, would probably reach white heat. At all events, whether the interview between father and son had or had not taken this course, it was bedrock fact that they had quarrelled about some woman.

===Chinese===
就坦纳对奥斯汀本人和他那幢小宅的观察来看，此人过活的花销怕是每年要近千镑。换句话说，几乎把进项花到了头。偏在这个当口，他恋爱了，决意成婚。那位小姐纵有嫁妆也必定微薄，两口人今后要靠先前只够奥斯汀一人花销的款项度日。那么奥斯汀怎么行事？订婚次日他便去见父亲，多半是报喜信，也可能顺带请求增加津贴。照常理，威廉爵士的财产早晚有大半要归他所有，这要求原不算过分。可威廉爵士怎么回答的？坦纳只能揣度；但据死者对阿巴思诺特先生说过的话，他多半非但回绝了增贴，还撂下话来：倘若这桩婚事当真办了，就改遗嘱，叫奥斯汀吃大亏。倘若——从英尼斯无意中听来的那几句看，不无这种可能——威廉爵士还说了什么有辱那位小姐的话，奥斯汀本已相当激愤的情绪，只怕要烧到白热。无论如何，父子那场会面有没有走到这一步且不论，他们曾为某个女人吵过一架，却是凿凿的实情。

===Original===
Tanner continued his surmises. Assuming he was correct so far, Austin would inevitably be faced with a very terrible temptation. If his father should die without altering his will, it would mean £150,000 to himself. £150,000! Quite a respectable sum of money! There would then be no question of love in a cottage with Miss Drew. He could give her all those things which men delight to give women. And the irritation of the constant unpleasantness with Sir William would be gone. The more Tanner considered the matter, the more powerful he felt this temptation would have been. Many a stronger man than Austin seemed to be had succumbed to less.

===Chinese===
坦纳继续往下揣想。倘若到此为止他的推断不差，奥斯汀就无可避免地要面对一种极其可怕的诱惑。父亲若是不改遗嘱便一命归西，对他就意味着 150,000 英镑。150,000 英镑！好一笔像样的巨款！到那时，同德鲁小姐「茅屋里的爱情」便无从谈起，男人们乐于奉献给女人的种种，他尽可以一一奉献给她；而同威廉爵士无休无止怄气斗气的那份煎熬，也一并烟消云散。这件事坦纳越琢磨，越觉得这诱惑的力量只怕大得很。多少瞧着比奥斯汀更硬气的人，栽在比这更小的诱惑上也不稀奇。

===Original===
Then coming down to details. The murderer had unquestionably known his way about Luce Manor. He had either gone to the library by the side door, and there committed the crime, carrying the body down to the boathouse, or, more probably, he had devised some scheme to make Sir William go there of his own accord. He had also taken the particular oars belonging to the boat used. All the knowledge necessary for this Austin of course possessed.

===Chinese===
再往细处看。凶手对卢斯庄园的路径无疑了如指掌。他要么是打侧门进了书房，就在那里行凶，再把尸体搬下船屋；要么——可能性更大——是设了个什么圈套，让威廉爵士自己走到那地方去。他还取走了那条船配套的那对桨。而干这一切所需要的那点知识，奥斯汀自然样样不缺。

===Original===
But more suspicious than that, Austin by his own showing had actually taken a boat down the river on that night, and the hours of leaving and returning were such that he would just have had time to reach the Luce Manor boathouse, commit the murder, and return the boat to the club. Of course, he had claimed to have an alibi for this period, but alibis could be faked, and the very readiness and apparent completeness of this seemed to Tanner slightly indicative of prearrangement.

===Chinese===
可比这更可疑的是：据奥斯汀自己供述，当晚他确曾驾船顺流而下，而离镇与回镇的钟点掐得恰好——刚好够他赶到卢斯庄园船屋、行凶杀人、再把船送回俱乐部。当然，他声称这段时间自己另有不在场证明；可不在场证明也能伪造，何况这一份来得那样现成、瞧上去又那样完密，在坦纳看来，倒隐隐透出几分预先布置的痕迹。

===Original===
There was then the question of the oars. This also had a suspicious look. At first Tanner had accepted without question the boatman’s theory that the boat and oars had become separated in the upper channel and had passed down to the falls through different arches of the road bridge. Indeed, his own inspection of the place had led him to the same conclusion. And this losing of the oars had seemed to explain adequately enough how the accident happened.

===Chinese===
然后是船桨的问题。这一点也透着蹊跷。起初，船工的那套说法坦纳照单全收：船和桨在上游河道里失散，又各自穿过干道大桥不同的桥孔，漂到了瀑布下头。说实在的，他亲自察看那地方之后，得出的也是同一个结论。而这失桨一事，似乎也把这场「意外」如何发生解释得够圆的了。

===Original===
But when it was shown that the affair was not an accident at all, the matter of the oars took on a very different complexion. Evidently it was part of the plant﻿—deliberately arranged to create the very impression it had produced. And Tanner now saw that the murderer had overreached himself. Sir William might easily have lost one oar, but he would not have let both go. And it was very unlikely, even if he had dropped both, that they would have separated far enough from the boat to have taken the other archway. Here again one might have done so, but not both. No, Tanner was driven to the conclusion that the boat must have been sent adrift close to the Luce Manor bank of the river, while the oars were taken over to the opposite side in another boat, and there thrown overboard. And Austin had had such a boat down the river that night.

===Chinese===
然而一旦查明此事压根儿不是意外，船桨问题便全然换了副面孔。它显然是整个布局中的一环——蓄意安排出来，好制造它果真制造出的那种印象。而且坦纳如今看明白了：凶手这一手玩过了头。威廉爵士失手落一支桨，大有可能；两支一齐落水，他决计不容。退一步说，纵然两支都脱了手，它们也不大可能漂离船身那么远，各自钻进另一个桥孔去。这一层也一样：一支或许办得到，两支断不能都办到。不，坦纳不得不认定的只能是：那条船是在紧挨卢斯庄园一侧的河岸边被放开漂走的，而那对桨则被人用另一条船载到对岸，抛进了水里。偏偏当晚奥斯汀就有这么一条船到过下游。

===Original===
Lastly, and most suggestive of all was Austin’s start and look of fear, when the Coroner had announced the adjournment of the inquest. It was true he had accounted for his apprehension. An investigation might bring up the question of the alibi in which, as he was unable to prove its truth, no credence might be placed, leaving him in an awkward position. But was this a satisfactory explanation? Were these consequences not too remote to be seen with such immediate clearness as to cause an actual start? Did it not involve too instantaneous a perception of the facts? The Inspector thought so.

===Chinese===
最后，也最耐人寻味的一点是：验尸官宣布验尸审讯延期时，奥斯汀猛然一惊，面露惧色。不错，他对自己的惶恐是解释过的——调查可能翻出不在场证明的问题，而他既无法证实其真伪，人家也许就无从采信，弄得他处境尴尬。可这个解释站得住么？这些后果未免太迂远了，哪能一眼望得那样分明，分明到教人当真吓一跳？这岂不太需要一种对事实的瞬间顿悟了么？探长觉得是。

===Original===
But he saw as he still smoked and pondered, that it was equally possible that his whole conclusions were erroneous. Austin unquestionably might be, as he pictured himself, the innocent victim of the real murderer’s plant.

===Chinese===
但他一边抽烟一边盘算，也看出了另一种同样可能的事：他的全部结论也许压根儿错了。奥斯汀未尝不像他自己刻画的那样，是真凶设局陷害的无辜牺牲品。

===Original===
His thoughts turned to Cosgrove Ponson. Here was another man who had an interest in Sir William’s death. And from what Mr. Arbuthnot had said, his interest might be quite as strong, or even stronger, than Austin’s. When a man about town who had been living a rather fast life got into financial difficulties, he was not usually very squeamish as to how he extricated himself. £75,000 to a man perhaps faced with ruin would be worth taking a risk for. And if Cosgrove had become hardened enough to commit the crime, he would probably not have jibbed at making the plant to throw the suspicion on Austin. Tanner concluded that if Austin’s alibi held, his next work must be the investigation of Cosgrove’s life as well as his movements on the night of the murder.

===Chinese===
他的思绪又转到科斯格罗夫·庞森身上。这是另一个能从威廉爵士之死中得好处的人。而且照阿巴思诺特先生的话来看，他的利害未必弱于奥斯汀，甚至可能更强。一个浪荡城中、日子过得相当放浪的人，一旦陷入财务困境，为求脱身，通常是不大讲究手段的。对一个也许面临破产的人，75,000 英镑值得为之冒一回险。倘若科斯格罗夫已经狠到下得去手作案，那么再设个局把嫌疑栽到奥斯汀身上，他多半也不会缩手。坦纳得出结论：如果奥斯汀的不在场证明站得住，他下一步的功夫，就得花在彻查科斯格罗夫的履历及其案发当晚的行踪上。

===Original===
His whole cogitations therefore simply brought him back to the point from which he had started. Before he did anything else, every point of Austin’s alibi must be gone into with the most meticulous care.

===Chinese===
于是他这一番冥思苦想，兜了个圈子，又把他送回了出发点。在做任何别的事情之前，奥斯汀不在场证明的每一个环节，都必须以最一丝不苟的细心查验到底。

===Original===
Accordingly, having refilled his pipe, he set himself to go over in detail the story Austin Ponson had told him of his movements on the night of the murder. He wished not only to be clear in his own mind as to the sequence of events, but to make a list of the points upon which he could reasonably hope to obtain confirmatory evidence.

===Chinese===
他重新装满烟斗，随即着手把奥斯汀·庞森讲给他听的案发当晚行踪，细细过了一遍。他不仅要让自己对事件的先后次序了然于心，还要开一张单子，列出那些他有望合理取得佐证的要点。

===Original===
Austin, according to his own statement, left the pavilion of the Halford boat club at about ten minutes before nine. He rowed to the Old Ferry, sat there for about ten minutes, until the maid arrived with the note, then walked to the Abbey and spent a few minutes searching for Miss Drew and her friends. Failing to find them, he went to the Franklyns’ house, satisfied himself it was empty, returned to the Old Ferry, and rowed back to Halford, arriving at the pavilion about quarter before eleven. There he had waked the caretaker to get the boat in. He had walked to the Drews’, stayed with them some ten minutes, going on to his own house, which he reached about twenty minutes past eleven. Now, first, as to the checking of these hours.

===Chinese===
照奥斯汀自己的陈述：九点差十分左右，他离开哈尔福德划船俱乐部的会所；划船到老渡口，在那里坐了约十分钟，直到那女仆把字条送到；随后步行到修道院，花几分钟寻找德鲁小姐和她的朋友们；没有找到，他又走到弗兰克林家的宅子，弄确实了屋里空无一人，这才回到老渡口，划船返回哈尔福德，约十一点差一刻回到俱乐部会所。在那里他唤起看门人，才把船收进去。之后他步行到德鲁家，坐了约十分钟，再回自己的住处，到家约十一点二十分。好，先来核对这些钟点。

===Original===
Tanner took a sheet of paper, and looking up his note of the times it had taken him to walk the various distances, and estimating for the rowing, he made a statement something like a railway time table. When he had finished he found that the total estimated time which the journeys should have taken worked out at practically that mentioned by Austin. So far, then, the story hung together.

===Chinese===
坦纳取过一张纸，翻出自己记下的徒步各段路程所需时间的笔记，再估出划船的用时，编成一份类似铁路时刻表的东西。编完一看，各段行程估算出的总耗时，同奥斯汀所说的几乎分毫不差。如此说来，这故事到目前为止是自洽的。

===Original===
Next with regard to outside testimony. What confirmation would it be reasonable to expect?

===Chinese===
其次是旁证。可以合理指望什么样的佐证呢？

===Original===
From the attendant at the boat club pavilion he should be able to learn if Austin did take out a boat, and if so, at what hour. Possibly other persons in the club might have been present, and could substantiate this point. The attendant would also know the hour at which he was called out on Austin’s return. The Drews would certainly have remarked the time of Austin’s call, and lastly, the latter’s own servants would be sure to remember the hour of his return. There should be ample﻿—indeed overwhelming﻿—evidence of the times at which Austin set out with his boat and returned.

===Chinese===
从俱乐部会所的看门人那里，他应当能问出奥斯汀当晚是否果真取船外出，若果真取了，又是什么时辰。俱乐部里或许还有旁人在场，能证实这一点。奥斯汀回来时看门人被唤起身，那时刻看门人也必定记得。德鲁一家肯定留意过奥斯汀来访的时刻；最后，奥斯汀自家的仆人也一定记得他回来的钟点。关于奥斯汀携船出发与归来的时刻，证据应当是充足的——简直是压倒性的。

===Original===
So far so good, but it was clear to Tanner that if he could get no further corroboration than this, the whole alibi was worthless. One point﻿—but that a vital point﻿—would remain unsubstantiated. Austin might have left and returned at the hours he stated, but where was he between them? Did he go to the Abbey and the Franklyns’? If he did, he could not have been to Luce Manor, and necessarily was innocent. How was this to be ascertained?

===Chinese===
到这一步还算顺当；但坦纳心里明白，倘若再得不到更进一层的佐证，整个不在场证明便一文不值。有一个要点——而且是性命攸关的要点——将无从证实。奥斯汀固然可以在他所说的钟点离家与回家，可这两个钟点之间，他人又在哪里？他果真去了修道院和弗兰克林家么？若是去了，他便到不了卢斯庄园，必然清白无辜。可这一层又如何查实呢？

===Original===
There were, of course, the footprints. That these were made by Austin’s shoes was beyond question. The fact that the latter were new prevented the marks showing the individuality that might otherwise have been expected, but against that, at one point on the edge of the left sole there was a slight dint﻿—caused possibly by striking a stone﻿—and this dint was faithfully reproduced in the prints. It was too much to suppose that two separate pairs of similar shoes should have similar dints.

===Chinese===
当然，还有那几枚脚印。脚印是奥斯汀那双鞋留下的，这一点无可置疑。鞋是新的，印痕上便显不出本来或许可以指望的那种个人特征；但反过来说，左鞋掌边缘有一处小小的凹痕——多半是磕在石头上磕出来的——而这凹痕，在脚印里也如实再现。要说两双各自独立的同款鞋上都带一模一样的凹痕，未免太巧了。

===Original===
But two other points were by no means beyond question. Though it was certain that Austin’s shoes had passed over that ground, how could it be proved that Austin was then wearing them? How, again, could it be known that they were made at the time stated? Could not Austin really have been at Luce Manor on that evening, and have made the tracks to the Abbey at some other time? Tanner was puzzled. He did not see how this point was to be cleared up.

===Chinese===
但另外两点却决谈不上无可置疑。奥斯汀的鞋固然走过那片地面，可怎么能证明当时穿鞋的正是奥斯汀本人？又怎么能知道脚印就是在所说的那个时辰留下的？难道奥斯汀就不能当晚真去了卢斯庄园，而在别的什么时间踩出通向修道院的那串脚印？坦纳犯了难，看不出这一层该怎么弄个水落石出。

===Original===
Next morning he began his investigation at the pavilion of the Halford boat club. It was quite a large place, situated on a kind of bay on the river, just below the town. The house was built of red brick, with heavy overhanging eaves, and a kind of piazza in front. Before the piazza and stretching a good way past it in each direction, was the wharf wall, with several broad flights of steps leading to the river. Out on the water were moored some dozen or more boats, and others were in the railed-off space surrounding the house. Tanner pushed open the gate of this railing and entered.

===Chinese===
次日早晨，他从哈尔福德划船俱乐部的会所着手查起。那是个相当大的场子，坐落在镇子下游一处河湾里。房子红砖砌成，屋檐沉沉外探，房前带一方游廊。游廊前面是码头护墙，朝两头各延伸出去好一段，其间几道宽阔的石阶下到水边。水上系着十几条或更多的船，另有一些搁在房子四周用栏杆围出的场地上。坦纳推开栏杆的门，走了进去。

===Original===
At first he could see no one about, then, as he walked round the house, he came on a youngish man in a cap and a blue jersey, who was washing out one of the boats. He hailed him, and they got into conversation in a leisurely way. Tanner praised the house and general appearance of the place, and then gradually came to the object of his call.

===Chinese===
起初他四下看不见一个人影；待绕着房子走到一侧，撞见一个戴便帽、穿蓝色针织衫的年轻人，正在冲洗一条船。他招呼一声，两人便不紧不慢地攀谈起来。坦纳称赞了那房子和整个场子的气象，随后渐渐转到此行的正题上。

===Original===
He had no trouble in obtaining the required information. The caretaker remembered Austin taking out the boat on the Wednesday night. He confirmed the latter’s statement on all points, and was quite certain of the hours he had left and returned.

===Chinese===
他要打听的情况不费吹灰之力便到了手。看门人记得奥斯汀星期三晚上取船的事，后者陈述的各点他都一一证实，对他离所与返回的钟点也一口咬定，毫不含糊。

===Original===
“Mr. Ponson was in a rare old hurry to be away,” went on the man. “The boat he wanted was out, but it was just coming round the bend there, and I told him to wait a moment and he could have it. Well, he did wait, but he was all jumping like a hen on a hot griddle to be off. ‘Hang it, Stevens,’ he says, ‘are those people going to be all night?’ But Mr. Brocklehurst, that was him who had out the boat, was sculling in quite fast. Well, sir, I fixed up the boat, and let him have it, and he rowed off like a blooming paddle steamer. I couldn’t but laugh when I watched him going down the river.”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生那阵子急着要走，可是急得少有。」那人接着往下说。「他要的那条船借出去了，不过正绕过那边的河湾回来，我叫他稍等片刻，船就是他的了。嗐，他倒真等了，可整个人跟热锅上的蚂蚁似的，蹦着跳着只盼动身。『见鬼，史蒂文斯（Stevens），』他说，『那些人要划到深更半夜么？』不过布罗克赫斯特（Brocklehurst）先生——借船的就是他——双桨摇得飞快，不一会儿就到了。得，先生，我把船拾掇停当交给他，他划起来那股冲劲，活像一艘开足了劲的明轮船。我瞧着他顺流而下，忍不住直笑。」

===Original===
“What time did you say that was?”

===Chinese===
「您说那是几点来着？」

===Original===
“It was ten minutes to nine. Mr. Brocklehurst got ashore about the quarter, and it took me about five minutes to get the boat squared up.”

===Chinese===
「九点差十分。布罗克赫斯特先生约莫一刻钟上的岸，我拾掇那条船又花了大约五分钟。」

===Original===
“And you say he rooted you out of bed when he got back?”

===Chinese===
「您是说，他回来的时候把您从床上揪起来了？」

===Original===
“Yes, sir. It was a quarter to eleven, and I had just put out the light.”

===Chinese===
「是的，先生。那是十一点差一刻，我刚熄了灯。」

===Original===
Tanner was nothing if not thorough. He went to the police station, learnt that Mr. Brocklehurst was a solicitor with an office in the main street, and promptly called on him. In five minutes he had his information. Mr. Brocklehurst had reached the pavilion about quarter before nine, for he recollected that as he had arrived at his house the town clock had struck nine.

===Chinese===
坦纳这人，别的且不论，周密是头一条。他先到警察局，打听到布罗克赫斯特先生是位律师，事务所在大街上，随即登门拜访。不出五分钟，他要的情况便到了手。布罗克赫斯特先生约在九点差一刻到达俱乐部会所——因为他记得，自己回到家门口时，镇上的大钟正敲九点。

===Original===
The inspector then turned his steps to Elm Cottage, the residence of the Drews. Apart from the question of the alibi, he was anxious to meet Miss Drew and form his own conclusions as to the part she would be likely to play in the matter.

===Chinese===
随后探长转道榆树小屋（Elm Cottage）——德鲁一家的住处。撇开不在场证明的问题不谈，他也急于见一见德鲁小姐，亲自掂量掂量她在这桩案子里可能扮演怎样的角色。

===Original===
Elm Cottage was a small detached villa, set some twenty yards back from the road, and surrounded by a tiny, but well-kept garden. Tanner sent in his card, and was taken to a low and rather dark drawing-room at the back of the house, from which however there was a fine view of the river. Everything bore traces of culture and taste, but of rather straitened means. The room wanted papering, the carpet was worn, the furniture shabby. But what there was of it was good, there was everywhere neatness and spotless cleanliness, and the otherwise somewhat drab effect was met by means of flowers in bowls and vases. If the room bore the impress of Miss Drew’s character, as Tanner suspected, it showed her a fine girl, bravely determined to make the best of things which she could not remedy.

===Chinese===
榆树小屋是一幢独门独户的小宅，退开大路约二十码，围着一座小而打理得极精致的园子。坦纳递进名片，被人引进屋后一间低矮而相当幽暗的客厅——好在从窗口望出去，河上风光颇佳。屋里处处透着教养与品味，也透着境况的拮据。四壁早该重新糊纸，地毯磨得见了旧，家具也寒素陈旧。但凡摆着的东西件件都是好的，处处整洁，纤尘不染；屋里原本有些黯淡的底色，又教一碗一碗、一瓶一瓶的鲜花给提亮了。倘若这间客厅真如坦纳所疑，印着德鲁小姐的性情，那么它显示的便是一位好姑娘：对那些无力补救的境况，勇敢地立志随遇而安、尽力经营。

===Original===
In a few moments she joined him, and as the Inspector looked at her face, he felt the character he had imagined of her was there. A low, broad forehead crowned by masses of dark hair surmounted two dark, intelligent eyes, which met his own with steady directness. Her nose was small, her lips rather full but delicately modelled, and her chin firm and well-rounded. Indeed, if anything, the lower part of her face might be considered a trifle too much developed for perfect symmetry. It gave her almost too pronounced an appearance of strength and determination, and Tanner felt she would be a person to be reckoned with. But as they talked he became convinced her power would never be used except to further what she believed to be just and right.

===Chinese===
片刻工夫，她便出来相见。探长端详她的脸，觉得心中勾画的那个她果然就在眼前。宽阔低平的前额覆着一头浓密的深色头发，额下一双深色眸子慧黠有神，迎着他的目光，沉静而坦然。鼻梁小巧，唇偏丰润而线条精致，下颌圆润坚毅。说实在的，若要挑剔，她脸的下半部或许略显发达，稍损完美的匀称；也正因如此，她面上那股刚毅果决的神气几乎过分鲜明，坦纳感到这是一个不可小觑的人物。然而随着交谈深入，他渐渐确信：她的力量，除伸张她认定正当的公道之外，绝不会另作他用。

===Original===
“I have to apologise, madame, for this intrusion,” he said courteously, “but I have been sent down by Scotland Yard to inquire into the circumstances attending the death of Sir William Ponson. Mr. Austin Ponson has told me of the hoax which was played on him on the same evening, and we discussed whether there might not possibly be some connection. He mentioned your name, and I ventured to call to ask you if you would please tell me what you know about it.”

===Chinese===
「女士，如此搅扰，恕我失礼。」他彬彬有礼地说，「我是奉苏格兰场之命，下来查证威廉·庞森爵士之死的前后情节的。奥斯汀·庞森先生已向我讲过当晚有人耍他的那场骗局，我们谈到两者之间或许不无牵连。他提到了您的名字，我便冒昧登门，想请您把所知的情形见告。」

===Original===
“Did Mr. Ponson tell you we are engaged?”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生把咱们订婚的事告诉您了？」

===Original===
“He did, Miss Drew, under promise of secrecy. He gave that as his excuse for mentioning your name, also saying he had your authority to do so.”

===Chinese===
「告诉了，德鲁小姐——那是要我保守秘密的。他以此为提及您名字的缘由，并说已征得您的同意。」

===Original===
“And what precisely do you want me to tell you?”

===Chinese===
「那么，您究竟要我说些什么呢？」

===Original===
“Anything you can about it, please. What, for example, was the first you heard of it?”

===Chinese===
「凡您知道的都请讲。比如，这事您最初是怎么听说的？」

===Original===
The girl did not reply for a moment. Then she answered with another question.

===Chinese===
姑娘沉吟了片刻没有作声，随即以另一个问题作答。

===Original===
“Just let me understand you, if you please, Mr. Inspector. Do you doubt Mr. Ponson’s story, and are you looking for confirmation?”

===Chinese===
「请恕我要先弄明白您的意思，探长先生。您是疑心庞森先生的说辞，来寻证实的么？」

===Original===
Inspector Tanner hesitated in his turn.

===Chinese===
这一回，轮到坦纳探长踌躇了。

===Original===
“I think, Miss Drew,” he said quietly, “that you would probably prefer me to tell you the exact truth.”

===Chinese===
「我想，德鲁小姐，」他平静地说，「您多半宁愿我告诉您不打折扣的实话。」

===Original===
She nodded and he went on.

===Chinese===
她点了点头，他便接着说下去。

===Original===
“The answer to your question is Yes and No. In the ordinary routine way I asked Mr. Ponson where he himself was on Wednesday night. Such a question is always asked under such circumstances, and it has no unpleasant significance. In answer to it he told me about the hoax. The story seemed to me probable, and I saw no reason for doubting it. But that did not absolve me from trying in every way I could to test its truth. You must see that I was bound to do so. And I may be allowed to say that all the inquiries I have made up to the present confirm what Mr. Ponson told me.”

===Chinese===
「您这个问题的答案是：既是也不是。照例行公事，我问了庞森先生本人星期三晚上在哪里。这种情形下总要问这样的话，并无什么不祥的含意。他在回答时向我讲起了那场骗局。这故事在我看来可信，我也看不出有什么可疑的地方。但这并不能免除我竭尽所能去验证它的真实。您一定明白，我责无旁贷。而且请容我说一句：到目前为止，我所作的一切查证，都印证了庞森先生对我说的话。」

===Original===
“But why does it matter whether or not his story is true?”

===Chinese===
「可他的话是真是假，又有什么要紧呢？」

===Original===
Tanner felt very uncomfortable. Though hardened by a life of contact with crime, he was a good fellow at heart, and he disliked intensely giving pain, especially to women. But as he looked into the steady, truthful eyes of the girl before him, he felt he could not prevaricate.

===Chinese===
坦纳觉得极不自在。他虽因常年与罪恶打交道的生涯磨硬了心肠，骨子里却是个厚道人，最不愿给人痛苦，尤其不愿给女人痛苦。可望着眼前这位姑娘那双沉静而真挚的眼睛，他觉得实在无法支吾搪塞。

===Original===
“I would rather not tell you,” he answered, “but if you insist, I will.”

===Chinese===
「我宁愿不说，」他答道，「但您若坚持，我就说。」

===Original===
“I insist.”

===Chinese===
「我坚持。」

===Original===
“Well, I am sorry to say we don’t exactly understand how Sir William died. There is a doubt that it may have been suicide or even murder. Let me make it clear that this is by no means certain, but I am bound to say that the idea has occurred to us that the murderer, if there was one, hoaxed Mr. Ponson to try to throw suspicion on him.”

===Chinese===
「唉，很遗憾，威廉爵士究竟是怎么死的，我们还不十分了然。存在一种疑虑：那可能是自杀，甚至是谋杀。请容我言明，这决计说不上定论；但我不能不说，我们心里起过一个念头：凶手——倘若真有凶手——之所以耍弄庞森先生，正是想把这嫌疑栽到他身上。」

===Original===
The girl’s face paled, but she gave no other sign of emotion.

===Chinese===
姑娘的脸白了，但旁的没有流露一丝激动。

===Original===
“I dreaded it,” she replied in a low tone, “and he dreaded it too. We talked the whole thing over on the afternoon of the day the body was found, and we couldn’t see any reason why Sir William should have gone down to the boat of his own accord. And then this hoax looked as if it had been made for just what you say. But I am at least thankful you take that view and don’t suspect Mr. Ponson of inventing the whole thing. That, I may say now, was what I really feared.”

===Chinese===
「我就怕是这个。」她低声答道，「他也怕。遗体被发现那天下午，我们把整件事前前后后商量过，实在想不出威廉爵士有什么缘由要自己走到船那边去。而这骗局，看上去恰恰就是为您所说的那个目的设下的。不过，您既持这个看法，并不疑心整件事是庞森先生编造出来的，我至少要谢天谢地。说句实话，我真正怕的正是那一个。」

===Original===
“I can only repeat that all the inquiries I have made up to the present have confirmed Mr. Ponson’s story, and I have no reason whatever to think he invented it.”

===Chinese===
「我只能再说一遍：到目前为止，我所作的一切查证都印证了庞森先生的说辞，我没有任何理由认为那是他编造的。」

===Original===
“Thank you for that at all events. Now what do you want me to do?”

===Chinese===
「无论如何，谢谢您这句话。那么您要我做些什么？」

===Original===
“To tell me at what hours Mr. Ponson came and left here on the Wednesday night, and what took place while he was here.”

===Chinese===
「请告诉我，星期三晚上庞森先生几点到您府上来、几点离开，以及他在这里时发生了什么。」

===Original===
“He came at eleven﻿—almost exactly. I looked at the clock when the ring came, for we seldom had so late a call. He showed me the two notes he had received, and asked me about them. When I explained I had not written them he told us﻿—my father was present﻿—the whole story of the hoax. As I have said, he did not stay, leaving in about ten minutes.”

===Chinese===
「他是十一点来的——几乎分秒不差。门铃响时我看了一眼钟，因为我们家很少有这么晚的来访。他把收到的那两张字条拿给我看，问我是怎么回事。等我说明不是我写的，他便给我们——当时我父亲也在座——讲了这场骗局的始末。如我方才所说，他没有久坐，约十分钟后就走了。」

===Original===
Tanner rose.

===Chinese===
坦纳站起身来。

===Original===
“Thank you, Miss Drew. That is all I want to know. I am sorry for having come on unpleasant business, and exceedingly grateful for the way you have met me.”

===Chinese===
「谢谢您，德鲁小姐。我要知道的就这些。很抱歉拿这样不愉快的事登门，也万分感激您接待我的这番气度。」

===Original===
She also rose and held out her hand.

===Chinese===
她也站起身，伸出手来。

===Original===
“I hope you will let me know how you get on,” she said.

===Chinese===
「我希望您容我知道案子办得怎么样了。」她说。

===Original===
“You may trust me,” he promised, and bowing low, took his leave.

===Chinese===
「您尽管放心。」他允诺道，深深鞠了一躬，告辞而去。

===Original===
As he walked slowly towards Austin’s villa, Tanner thought over the interview he had just had. He felt sure that the accounts he had heard of Miss Drew﻿—that she was “a fine girl” and “a real lady”﻿—were true. He believed she was the kind of girl who would marry neither for wealth nor position, and the fact that she had consented to an engagement with Austin seemed to speak well for the latter.

===Chinese===
朝奥斯汀的小宅信步走去的一路，坦纳回味着方才那番会面。他确信，先前听到的关于德鲁小姐的评语——说她是个「好姑娘」、是「真正的淑女」——都非虚言。他相信她是那种既不为钱财、也不为地位嫁人的姑娘；而她竟肯同奥斯汀订婚，这件事本身，似乎恰恰是替后者说好话的。

===Original===
His thoughts turned back to the point about the alibi which still worried him﻿—whether Austin had really been at the Abbey at the time he said. And suddenly a way in which he might test the matter occurred to him. The shoes which made the tracks Tanner had found had, so Austin had said, been bought by him in London on the Monday. He, Tanner, had got them from Austin after the inquest on Friday. If he could trace the movements of those shoes from Monday to Friday, would light not be thrown on the problem? Tanner thought it worth while trying.

===Chinese===
他的思绪又回到那件仍旧搅扰他的悬案上——奥斯汀当天那个时辰究竟在不在修道院。忽然，一个验证此事的法子浮上心头。留下那串脚印的鞋，据奥斯汀说，是星期一在伦敦买的；而他坦纳本人，则是星期五验尸审讯之后从奥斯汀手里取到的。倘若他能把那双鞋从星期一到星期五的行踪追查清楚，这道难题岂不就照进一线亮光了？坦纳觉得值得一试。

===Original===
Not far from the door of Austin Ponson’s house a police constable was moving slowly along his beat. The Inspector went over to him.

===Chinese===
离奥斯汀·庞森家宅大门不远，一名警员正沿着自己那段巡逻路线缓缓走着。探长走了过去。

===Original===
“Have you seen Mr. Ponson lately?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
「近来见过庞森先生么？」他问道。

===Original===
“Yes, sir,” answered the man, “he and Lady and Miss Ponson passed in the car about half an hour ago, going towards London.”

===Chinese===
「见过，先生，」那人答道，「他和庞森夫人、庞森小姐大约半小时前乘车过去了，往伦敦方向去的。」

===Original===
This was a fortunate chance, and relieved the Inspector of a possibly tedious wait, as he wished to make his call at the house when Austin was from home. He now rang at the door. The butler appeared.

===Chinese===
这一下碰得巧，省了探长一场也许冗长的空等——他本就打算趁奥斯汀不在家时登门。此刻他按响门铃，应门的是位管家。

===Original===
“Is Mr. Ponson at home?”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生在家么？」

===Original===
“No, sir. He went out about half an hour ago. I expect him back about six. What name shall I say?”

===Chinese===
「不在，先生。他约半小时前出去了，我想他六点前后回来。您怎么称呼？」

===Original===
“Tanner. Inspector Tanner of New Scotland Yard. I am sorry Mr. Ponson is out as I wished to ask him for a little information, but perhaps if you would be so kind you might be able to give it to me.”

===Chinese===
「坦纳。新苏格兰场的坦纳探长。庞森先生不在，我很遗憾，我本想向他打听一点情况；不过您若肯行个好，也许您也能见告。」

===Original===
The butler was obviously impressed by the occupation of his visitor, and Tanner felt sure his curiosity would also be aroused.

===Chinese===
来客的身份显然镇住了这位管家，坦纳也笃定他的好奇心必已勾了起来。

===Original===
“If you will come in I shall be glad to do what I can.”

===Chinese===
「您请进来吧，我愿意尽我所能。」

===Original===
“Now, Mr.﻿—? I didn’t hear your name?”

===Chinese===
「那么，——先生？我没听清您的名字？」

===Original===
“Lewis. John Lewis.”

===Chinese===
「刘易斯（Lewis）。约翰·刘易斯（John Lewis）。」

===Original===
“Well, Mr. Lewis, Mr. Ponson told me a curious story of a hoax that had been played on him last Wednesday night. I was down, you know, about Sir William’s death, and we got talking. You know about the hoax, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
「是这样，刘易斯先生，庞森先生向我讲了一桩稀奇事：上星期三晚上有人设局耍了他。您知道，我是为威廉爵士的案子下来的，我们聊着聊着就说到了它。这场骗局，您想必知情吧？」

===Original===
“Not a word,” the butler answered, his manner portraying keen interest.

===Chinese===
「一无所闻。」管家答道，神情里透着热切的好奇。

===Original===
“He didn’t tell me it was any secret. He got a note on Wednesday evening asking him to go out to meet some friends that night at the Abbey ruin.”

===Chinese===
「他没对我说这是机密。星期三傍晚他收到一张字条，约他当晚出去，到修道院废墟会几位朋友。」

===Original===
The butler nodded several times.

===Chinese===
管家连连点头。

===Original===
“Yes, I remember that note. I found it in the hall-door letter-box. It must have been left by private messenger, for there was no postmark on it. After Mr. Austin had read it he came asking me where I had found it. He seemed kind of puzzled about it.”

===Chinese===
「记得，我记得那张字条。是我在大门信箱里发现的。那必定是专人送来的，因为上头没有邮戳。奥斯汀先生读过之后，还来问我在哪儿发现的。他像是有点给弄糊涂了。」

===Original===
“That would be it,” Tanner agreed. “He went out to the Abbey, but when he reached it there was no one there and he had his journey for nothing. Someone had hoaxed him properly.”

===Chinese===
「正是这么回事，」坦纳附和道，「他去了修道院，可到了那儿一个人也没有，白跑了一趟。有人结结实实耍了他一场。」

===Original===
“Bless us all!” ejaculated the butler. “You don’t say?”

===Chinese===
「我的老天！」管家脱口喊道。「竟有这种事？」

===Original===
“Mr. Ponson asked me when I was here to look into the thing for him and I did. I found some footsteps there that might give a clue, but the worst of it is I don’t know whether they weren’t made by himself. Maybe, Mr. Lewis, you would help me there. I would be much obliged, I’m sure.”

===Chinese===
「我上回来时，庞森先生托我替他把这事查一查，我查了。我在那儿发现几枚脚印，也许能提供线索；糟糕的是，我不知道那会不会就是他自己踩下的。刘易斯先生，这一层您或许帮得上忙。若蒙相助，我感激不尽。」

===Original===
“Certainly, Mr. Tanner. I’d be pleased if you’d tell me how.”

===Chinese===
「当然，坦纳先生。您说怎么帮，我乐意效劳。」

===Original===
“All I want is to see the shoes Mr. Ponson wore on Wednesday night, so as I can compare them with the prints.”

===Chinese===
「我只求看一看庞森先生星期三晚上穿的那双鞋，好拿它们同脚印比对。」

===Original===
The butler’s face fell.

===Chinese===
管家的脸垮了下来。

===Original===
“That’s just the one thing I can’t do,” he answered. “They’re not here. I missed them on Friday, and told Mr. Austin. He said they were not comfortable, and he had sent them to be stretched.”

===Chinese===
「偏偏就是这个我办不到，」他答道，「鞋不在这儿了。星期五我发现鞋不见了，告诉了奥斯汀先生。他说那双鞋不合脚，送去撑一撑了。」

===Original===
“They were new, then?”

===Chinese===
「那么，鞋是新的？」

===Original===
“Yes, he got them in town on Monday.”

===Chinese===
「是新的，星期一他在城里买的。」

===Original===
“It was curious he should wear them that night if they weren’t comfortable.”

===Chinese===
「既然不合脚，那晚他还偏穿它们，倒奇怪了。」

===Original===
“I suppose he hadn’t found it out. That evening was the first time he had had them on.”

===Chinese===
「我猜他自己还没觉出来。那天晚上是他头一回上脚。」

===Original===
“I see.” Tanner nodded his head, then continued, “What time did he go out, Mr. Lewis?”

===Chinese===
「明白了。」坦纳点了点头，接着又问：「刘易斯先生，他是几点出门的？」

===Original===
“About eight or a bit after.”

===Chinese===
「八点上下，或者稍晚一点。」

===Original===
“And when did he get back?”

===Chinese===
「什么时候回来的？」

===Original===
“A little before half-past eleven. I brought him some whisky, and when I was coming out of his room the clock struck the half-hour.”

===Chinese===
「十一点半差一点。我给他送了威士忌去，正从他房里出来时，钟敲了半点。」

===Original===
“And he didn’t go out again that night?”

===Chinese===
「当夜他没有再出去吧？」

===Original===
“No, he went to bed about twelve. I heard him go up.”

===Chinese===
「没有，十二点前后他就上楼睡了。我听见他上楼去的。」

===Original===
“I see. And what was the next occasion he wore those shoes?”

===Chinese===
「明白了。那双鞋后来他还什么时候穿过？」

===Original===
“There wasn’t no next occasion. That was the only time he had them on.”

===Chinese===
「后来再没有过。他就上过那一回脚。」

===Original===
Tanner considered. This seemed to be pretty conclusive, but he was anxious to obtain even stronger evidence. After a moment he went on again.

===Chinese===
坦纳思量着。这看来已经相当板上钉钉，但他还想拿到更硬的证据。沉吟片刻，他又接着说了下去。

===Original===
“I’m afraid I’m not quite clear about the thing yet. What I want to get at is whether anyone else could have got hold of those shoes and made the tracks I saw at the Abbey.”

===Chinese===
「这事恐怕我还没弄利落。我想弄明白的是：会不会有旁人拿到过那双鞋，踩出了我在修道院看见的那串脚印。」

===Original===
“If that’s all’s worrying you, you may make your mind easy. Those shoes were in my charge from Monday evening till Mr. Ponson put them on on Wednesday after dinner. Then I brought them down to clean before I went to bed that night, and they were there till he took them on Friday. I remarked them particularly because they were new, and if anyone had touched them I would have known. Besides, there was no one about that could do it.”

===Chinese===
「您操心的若只是这一桩，那尽可以把心放进肚子里。从星期一晚上起，那双鞋就归我照管，直到星期三晚饭后庞森先生穿上它们。当晚我睡前把它们拿下去擦拭，之后它们就一直放在那儿，直到星期五他取走。因为鞋是新的，我格外留意过，谁若碰过，瞒不过我的眼睛。再说，周围也没有能做这件事的人。」

===Original===
“What state were they in when you cleaned them?”

===Chinese===
「您擦鞋的时候，它们是什么模样？」

===Original===
“Muddy﻿—very wet and muddy. I couldn’t think where Mr. Austin had been to get them into such a condition.”

===Chinese===
「满是泥——又湿又泥。我想不出奥斯汀先生去了什么地方，把鞋糟践成那样。」

===Original===
“One more question, Mr. Lewis. You tell me the shoes were not worn except on Wednesday evening. But could someone else not have worn them then? Suppose Mr. Austin went out wearing some other pair, and someone else slipped in and got hold of these and made the tracks, and then put back the shoes without your knowing?”

===Chinese===
「再问一个问题，刘易斯先生。您说那双鞋除星期三晚上外没有穿过。可当晚穿它们的，会不会是别的什么人呢？假定奥斯汀先生换穿了一双别的鞋出门，有人溜进来抓起这双去踩下脚印，再趁您不备把鞋放回原处呢？」

===Original===
The butler looked at the other with an expression of pitying scorn.

===Chinese===
管家望着对方，一脸怜悯加鄙夷的神气。

===Original===
“Why, Mr. Inspector, I’m not altogether a fool. I tell you I saw them on Mr. Austin’s feet when he was going out, and I saw them on his feet when he was coming in, so they weren’t in the house for anyone else to take. And what’s more, if that doesn’t convince you, every other pair of Mr. Austin’s boots and shoes were in the house that evening. I know because I happened to look over them to see if any wanted mending. So if anyone else had his new shoes he must have been going about himself in his socks.”

===Chinese===
「嗐，探长先生，我还不至于是个十足的傻瓜。我告诉您：奥斯汀先生出门时，那鞋就在他脚上；他回来时，鞋还在他脚上。所以那段时间鞋根本不在屋里，旁人无从拿走。再者，倘若这还说服不了您——奥斯汀先生当晚其余所有的靴子鞋子，一双不落全在屋里。这我知道，因为我恰巧翻检过一遍，看看有没有该修的。所以，那晚要是有人穿走了他的新鞋，他自己就得穿着袜子满世界跑了。」

===Original===
It was enough. This placed the affair beyond doubt, or it would if one other point were settled. Tanner rose.

===Chinese===
这就够了。事情至此已无置疑的余地——或者说，只消再把另一点敲定，便再无置疑的余地。坦纳站起身。

===Original===
“I am extremely obliged to you, Mr. Lewis, and now I must beg your pardon for having played a little trick on you. I have the shoes. Mr. Ponson gave them to me on Friday. Come with me to the hotel and have a drink, and tell me if the shoes I have are the ones you were speaking of, and that’ll be all I’ll ask you.”

===Chinese===
「实在感激不尽，刘易斯先生。如今我还得请您原谅——我方才对您使了个小小的花招。鞋在我这儿，庞森先生星期五把它们交给了我。请随我到旅馆喝上一杯，看看我手里的鞋是不是您说的那一双，此后我再无别的相求。」

===Original===
That the butler was suspicious there was more in the questions than met the eye was obvious, but he made no remark, and on seeing the shoes, he identified them unhesitatingly as Austin’s.

===Chinese===
这些问话背后另有文章，管家分明已疑心了，但他一句没说；而一见到那双鞋，便毫不迟疑地认出正是奥斯汀的。

===Original===
Tanner was pleased with the result of his inquiries. As he summed up the situation it stood as follows:

===Chinese===
坦纳对这番查访的结果颇为满意。他把局势归结如下：

===Original===
Austin had left Halford and returned to it at such hours as would have just enabled him to reach the Abbey in the interval. Therefore, if he did reach it he could not have been at Luce Manor, and if he was not at Luce Manor he was innocent. Footprints were made at the Abbey by a certain pair of shoes. Those shoes were at Austin’s house every moment of the time from their purchase till they came into Tanner’s possession, except during the particular period in question. The tracks at the Abbey must therefore have been made during this period. Further, during this period Austin himself must have been wearing the shoes, as not only had he left his house and returned to it wearing them, but he had no others to put on﻿—the remainder were all at his house. If, therefore, Austin did not himself make the tracks at the Abbey, he must have had no shoes during the time this was being done, in which case he could hardly have been at Luce Manor committing the murder. To Tanner the alibi was complete. Short of seeing Austin at the Abbey, he could expect no stronger evidence.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀离开哈尔福德又回到哈尔福德，两个钟点之间恰好够他去一趟修道院。因此，他若真到了修道院，便到不了卢斯庄园；到不了卢斯庄园，他便清白无辜。修道院的脚印是某一双鞋踩下的。那双鞋从买下到落入坦纳之手，时时刻刻都待在奥斯汀家里——唯独所说的那段特定时间除外。修道院的脚印，因此必定是在那段时间里踩下的。进一层说，在那段时间里，穿这双鞋的必定是奥斯汀本人：因为他不但穿着它们离家、又穿着它们进门，而且他无鞋可换——其余的鞋全在家里。所以，倘若修道院的脚印不是奥斯汀本人踩下的，那么脚印留下之时他必定脚上无鞋；果真如此，他也很难再跑到卢斯庄园去行凶了。在坦纳看来，这份不在场证明已告圆满。除非亲眼看见奥斯汀人在修道院，他想不出更硬的证据了。

===Original===
Even if the truth of Austin’s story were unlikely, Tanner would have felt compelled to believe it. But, as he had seen from the start, it was by no means unlikely. On the contrary the whole thing was just the kind of plant the real murderer might probably enough devise to shift suspicion from himself to Austin. That it was such a plant Tanner now felt certain.

===Chinese===
即便奥斯汀的说辞本身不像真的，坦纳也觉得不能不信了。何况正如他从头就看出的，这事绝非不像真的。恰恰相反，整件事正是真凶很可能设计出来的那种栽赃的局，好把嫌疑从自己身上挪到奥斯汀身上。它正是这样一个局——对此，坦纳如今已确信不疑。

===Original===
And if so, had it not one rather suggestive point? The man who made the plant was familiar with Austin and his affairs. Who, of those who knew the affairs of both Austin and Sir William, had an interest in the latter’s death?

===Chinese===
倘若如此，这里面岂不是有一个颇堪玩味之处么？设局之人对奥斯汀及其私事了如指掌。那么，在既知奥斯汀底细、又知威廉爵士底细的人当中，谁盼着后者死呢？

===Original===
The answer was not far to seek. One such at least was Cosgrove Ponson. He had both the knowledge and the motive. Tanner felt his next business must be with the cousin.

===Chinese===
答案不远。这样的人至少有一个：科斯格罗夫·庞森。他既有那份知识，又有那份动机。坦纳感到，下一步的功课，非落在这位堂兄弟身上不可。

===Original===
And then a more sinister idea entered the Inspector’s mind. What if there was more in the plant than a mere attempt to shift the suspicion off the murderer? What if the plan was to encompass Austin’s death as well? If Austin were convicted and executed it would make a great difference to Cosgrove apart from rendering his position safe. Tanner recalled the terms of the will. If Sir William died Cosgrove received £75,000, but if Austin also should lose his life his cousin would net another £30,000. Here was motive enough for anything.

===Chinese===
接着，一个更阴狠的念头钻进探长心里。倘若这个局里，还不止是把嫌疑从凶手身上引开这一层呢？倘若这计划连奥斯汀的性命也要一并算计呢？奥斯汀若被定罪处决，科斯格罗夫的地位固可安然无虞，好处却远不止此。坦纳记起遗嘱的条款：威廉爵士一死，科斯格罗夫得 75,000 英镑；奥斯汀若再丧了命，他这位堂兄弟还能净捞另外 30,000 英镑。这样的动机，干什么都够了。

===Original===
Tanner recollected the woman who, Austin had stated, had handed him the note at the Old Ferry. As the latter’s story must now be taken as true, this must be a real woman, and if Cosgrove were the guilty man she must be his accomplice.

===Chinese===
坦纳想起那个女人——据奥斯汀说，是她在老渡口把字条递给他的。奥斯汀的说辞如今既必须当作实情看待，这女人便必有其人；而倘若科斯格罗夫就是罪人，她必是同谋。

===Original===
Here was a line of inquiry which might lead to something. Tanner decided he would return to town by the next train, and start this new phase of the case.

===Chinese===
这是一条也许通向些什么的线索。坦纳决定搭下一班火车回城，开启案子的这个新阶段。

## VI · What Cosgrove Had to Tell / VI · 科斯格罗夫的交代

===Original===
At three o’clock that same afternoon Inspector Tanner stepped from the train at St. Pancras. He had telephoned to the Yard before leaving Halford, and, as a result, one of his men was awaiting him on the platform.

===Chinese===
当天下午三点钟，坦纳探长在圣潘克拉斯（St. Pancras）下了火车。离开哈尔福德之前他已给苏格兰场打过电话，因此站台上正有一名手下等着他。

===Original===
“Ah, Hilton,” the Inspector greeted him. “I want you to go over to Knightsbridge and look up a man for me﻿—a Mr. Cosgrove Ponson who has rooms at Number 174B. All you need find out is whether or not he is at home. I’ll follow you round in a couple of hours, and you can report to me there.”

===Chinese===
「啊，希尔顿（Hilton），」探长同他打招呼，「我要你到骑士桥去，替我查一个人——一位科斯格罗夫·庞森先生，在 174B 号有几间屋子。你只消查明他此刻在不在家。两个钟头后我随后就到，你在那儿向我报告。」

===Original===
This arranged, Tanner took a taxi and was driven to his house at Fulham.

===Chinese===
这事安排停当，坦纳坐上一辆出租汽车，回他富勒姆（Fulham）的宅子去了。

===Original===
Town was very hot. The sun poured down out of an almost brazen sky, taking the freshness from the air and turning the streets into canals of swimming heat. The narrow courts were stifling, the open spaces shone with a blinding glare. Dust was everywhere, a dry burning dust which parched the throat and made the eyeballs smart. As Tanner looked around him he recalled with regret the green lawns and shady trees of Luce Manor.

===Chinese===
伦敦城里热得很。日头从近乎黄铜色的天心直灌下来，抽干了空气里的清爽，把条条街道烤成热浪翻腾的河渠。窄巷里闷得叫人窒息，空旷处白花花的光晃得人睁不开眼。尘土无处不在——一种干灼灼的尘土，燎得喉咙冒烟、眼球发疼。坦纳环顾四周，不由得满怀怅惘地想起卢斯庄园绿茵茵的草坪和荫翳的树木。

===Original===
A couple of hours later he emerged from his house, resplendent in a silk hat and frock coat, with well-fitting gloves and a gold-headed cane. Taking another taxi, he drove to Knightsbridge. There he dismissed his vehicle, and approaching his man, Hilton, made him a slight sign. The other responded by nodding his head. Cosgrove, the Inspector understood, had gone out.

===Chinese===
两个钟头后，他从家里出来，丝质大礼帽、燕尾长礼服，一派雍容装束：手套熨帖合手，拄一根金头手杖。又雇了辆出租汽车，驶往骑士桥。到了地方他打发走车子，走近手下希尔顿，朝他略一示意。对方点头作答。探长明白了：科斯格罗夫出门了。

===Original===
Sauntering leisurely across the road, Tanner mounted the steps of the house and rang. The door was opened by a dark, clean-shaven manservant.

===Chinese===
坦纳慢悠悠踱过马路，登上那幢房子的台阶，按响门铃。开门的是个肤色黝黑、脸刮得干干净净的男仆。

===Original===
“Mr. Ponson is not at home, sir,” he said in reply to the Inspector’s inquiry, as he reached back for a salver.

===Chinese===
「庞森先生不在家，先生。」他回答探长的问话，一边回身去取托盘。

===Original===
Tanner held out a card engraved “Mr. Reginald Willoughby, The Albany.”

===Chinese===
坦纳递出一张名片，上头印着「雷金纳德·威洛比先生（Reginald Willoughby），奥尔巴尼（The Albany）」。

===Original===
“I rather wanted to see Mr. Ponson on business,” he went on. “Do you think he’ll soon be back?”

===Chinese===
「我有点生意上的事想见庞森先生，」他接着说，「您看他快回来了么？」

===Original===
“I think so, sir. He’ll almost certainly be in before seven.”

===Chinese===
「我想是快了，先生。七点以前他几乎肯定回来。」

===Original===
The Inspector glanced at his watch.

===Chinese===
探长看了看表。

===Original===
“I have half an hour to spare. I think I’ll come in and wait.”

===Chinese===
「我还能耽搁半个钟头。我想进去等等吧。」

===Original===
“Very good, sir,” the man replied, as he led the way to a large sitting-room on the first floor.

===Chinese===
「好的，先生。」那人应道，把他引到二楼一间宽敞的起居室。

===Original===
Left to himself, Tanner began by looking carefully round the room, and noting and memorising its contents. It was furnished as a library, with huge leather-covered chairs, and a large roll-top desk. The walls were lined with bookshelves, relieved here and there by a good print. The air was heavy with the scent of innumerable roses, arranged in bowls of silver and old china. Books and papers, mostly of a sporting character, were littered on chairs and occasional tables. Cosgrove Ponson, it was evident, was not hard up in the sense in which the words are understood by the man in the street.

===Chinese===
屋里只剩他一人，坦纳先细细环顾四周，把房中陈设一一记在心里。屋子布置成书房的样子：几张硕大的皮面座椅，一张大号卷盖书桌。四壁排满书架，间或点缀一两幅上佳的版画。满室浮动着无数玫瑰的浓香，花插在银盆和古瓷碗里。书报文件大多是赛马一类的玩意儿，散乱在椅子上和小桌上。显而易见，「手头拮据」这几个字，若按街头常人的理解，是落不到科斯格罗夫·庞森头上的。

===Original===
Tanner waited motionless for a few minutes, then rising softly, he tiptoed over to the roll-top desk and tried the lid. It was locked. Slipping a small tool from his pocket, he gently inserted it in the lock, and after a few turns he was able to push the shutter noiselessly up.

===Chinese===
坦纳一动不动地等了几分钟，随后轻轻起身，蹑手蹑脚挨到卷盖书桌前，试了试盖板。锁着。他从口袋里摸出一件小工具，轻轻插进锁孔，拨弄了几下，便把卷盖无声无息地推了上去。

===Original===
The desk was littered with papers. Tanner sat down before it and began a systematic though rapid search. He wanted to find out for himself Cosgrove’s exact financial position as well as, if possible, the names of any lady friends, one of whom might have impersonated Mrs. Franklyn’s servant.

===Chinese===
书桌上摊满纸片。坦纳在桌前坐下，开始一场有条不紊而又下手极快的搜查。他想亲自弄清科斯格罗夫财务上的确切底细；若有可能，还想摸出他女友们的名姓——冒充弗兰克林太太家女仆的，说不定就是其中一位。

===Original===
But he had no luck. It seemed likely Cosgrove must have some other desk or sanctum in which he kept his more private correspondence. There were here notes, invitations, bills, a few receipts, and other miscellaneous papers, but no bankbook nor anything to give a clue to his means. Nor were any of the letters from female correspondents couched in sufficiently familiar language to seem worthy of a second thought.

===Chinese===
可是他运气不佳。看来科斯格罗夫多半另有一张书桌或一间密室，收存更私密的信件。这里只有便条、请帖、账单、几张收据和别的杂七杂八的纸片，既没有银行存折，也没有任何能透露他财路的东西。女性来信倒有几封，措辞却无一亲昵到值得再看第二眼。

===Original===
Considerably disappointed, Tanner pursued his search according to the regular routine he employed in such cases, ending up when he had finished with the letters by drawing a small mirror from his pocket and with it examining the blotting paper. He rapidly scanned the various sheets, and was just about to put them down as useless when his eye lit on the blurred and partial impression of an address. It consisted of three lines. The first he could not read, the second he thought was Gracechurch Street, following an undecipherable number, while the third was clear﻿—the word “City.” He had not noticed this address on any of the papers, and he now remarked it only because it seemed to suggest finance. Thinking it might be worth while trying to decipher the name, he slipped the page out of the blotter and secreted it in his pocket. Then silently closing the desk he tiptoed to the door. After listening for a moment at the keyhole he opened it and stepped stealthily out.

===Chinese===
坦纳大失所望，便按他办理这类案子的惯常路数继续搜查：信件翻完之后，末了从口袋里掏出一面小镜子，就着它察看吸墨纸。他飞快扫过一页页纸，正要把它们当作无用之物放下，目光却落在一个模糊残缺的地址印痕上。那地址共三行：第一行认不出来；第二行他猜是格雷教堂街（Gracechurch Street），前面缀着一个辨不清的号码；第三行却清清楚楚——「伦敦城」（City）一词。方才那些文件里他没见过这个地址，此刻之所以留意，只因它似乎透着银钱的气息。他想，这名字或许值得费神一辨，便把那页纸从吸墨本里抽出来，藏进口袋。随后无声地合上书桌，蹑手蹑脚走到门边，在锁孔前听了片刻，开门闪身而出。

===Original===
Several doors opened off the passage, and Tanner stood for a moment wondering which led to the room of which he was in search. At last he selected one, and having ascertained from the keyhole that all was quiet within, he silently turned the handle. It opened into a dining-room. Withdrawing in the same noiseless manner he tried the next, to find himself in a spare bedroom. But the third door led to his goal. It was evidently Cosgrove’s dressing-room, and there at the opposite wall was what he was looking for﻿—a long line of Cosgrove’s boots and shoes. A moment’s examination sufficed. Cosgrove’s foot was too big to have made the tracks of the fifth man at the Luce Manor boathouse. Silently he returned to his seat in the library.

===Chinese===
走廊两侧开着好几扇门，坦纳站了片刻，掂量哪一扇通向他要找的房间。终于选定一扇，先从锁孔探明屋内悄无声息，便无声地旋动把手。门开处是一间餐室。他以同样悄没声的方式退出来，再试第二扇，发觉置身一间备用卧室。第三扇门却正中目标。那分明是科斯格罗夫的更衣室，对面墙边赫然摆着他要找的东西——科斯格罗夫的一长排靴子鞋子。略一查验便够了。科斯格罗夫的脚太大，不可能是卢斯庄园船屋里留下第五个人那些脚印的脚。他悄然回到书房的座位上。

===Original===
He looked at his watch. His search had lasted thirty-five minutes. He rang the bell.

===Chinese===
他看看表。搜查共用了三十五分钟。他按了铃。

===Original===
“I am sorry I cannot wait for Mr. Ponson,” he told the butler. “I shall write to him.”

===Chinese===
「很抱歉，我等不到庞森先生了，」他对管家说，「我会给他写信的。」

===Original===
That evening he sat down to reexamine the sheet of blotting paper. He studied the second line for several minutes, and at last came to the conclusion his first idea had been correct. It was apparently Gracechurch Street. But the number was quite beyond him.

===Chinese===
当晚他坐下来，重新研究那页吸墨纸。第二行他琢磨了好几分钟，最后断定自己最初的判断不错：显然是格雷教堂街。可那个号码，他实在无从辨认。

===Original===
Taking a street directory he began to go through the Gracechurch Street names, comparing each with the blot-sheet marks. He had been through about half when he came on one that seemed the correct shape﻿—Messrs. Moses Erckstein & Co. And when he saw that Messrs. Erckstein were moneylenders, he felt hopeful that he was on the right track. But he was very thorough. He worked through the whole list, lest there should be some other name even more like that on the sheet. But there was none.

===Chinese===
他取来一份街道名录，着手逐一翻检格雷教堂街的商号，同吸墨纸上的印记比对。翻到约莫一半，撞上一个字形似乎正相吻合的——摩西·厄克斯坦公司（Messrs. Moses Erckstein & Co.）。待看清厄克斯坦公司是做放贷生意的，他心头一热，觉得这回摸对路了。但他办事极缜密，仍把整张名单从头到尾翻完，唯恐还有旁的名字同纸上那个更相像。并没有。

===Original===
Next morning he called on Messrs. Erckstein. He was again wearing his silk hat and frock coat, and with these clothes he put on, to some extent at least, the manners of what our friends across the pond call a club man. He had made inquiries about the firm, and he now asked for the senior partner. After a delay of a few minutes he was shown into the latter’s room.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，他去拜访厄克斯坦公司。又是丝质大礼帽加燕尾长礼服，而且穿上这身行头，多少也摆出了我们大洋彼岸的朋友们所谓的「俱乐部绅士」做派。那家商号的底细他已打听过，进门便求见资深合伙人。等了几分钟，他被引进了后者的房间。

===Original===
Mr. Erckstein was stout and dark, with a short black beard and Semitic features. Tanner had found out that, though he had been a German before the War, he was now a Pole.

===Chinese===
厄克斯坦先生生得矮胖黝黑，蓄一部黑色短须，一副闪米特人的面貌。坦纳已查明：此人战前本是德国人，如今成了波兰人。

===Original===
He proved an unwilling witness. It was not until Tanner had wasted over an hour, and threatened his informant with a summons to Court, where his books and his methods would be probed mercilessly in public, that he got what he required.

===Chinese===
这是个不情愿的证人。坦纳耗了一个多钟头，又扬言要发传票把他拘到法庭上去——到那时，他的账本、他的手段都要当众被毫不留情地彻查——这才拿到了他要的东西。

===Original===
Cosgrove Ponson, it appeared, was, and had been for many years, heavily in the firm’s debt. Including interest at the exorbitant rate charged, he now owed the moneylenders close on £30,000. Moreover, he had recently been severely pressed for part payment. Tanner, after a lot of trouble, saw copies of the letters sent, the last of which politely but unmistakably threatened proceedings and ruin unless the interest at least was immediately paid.

===Chinese===
原来，科斯格罗夫·庞森欠着这家公司的债，欠得很重，而且已欠了多年。连本带利——利钱是敲骨吸髓的高利——他如今欠这些放贷商的已近 30,000 英镑。不但如此，近来对方还狠狠逼过他，要他先清偿一部分。坦纳费了不少周折，才看到那些催债信的副本；最后通牒的那封客客气气、却毫不含糊地威胁说，利息若不立即付清，便要兴讼，叫他倾家荡产。

===Original===
“Why did you lend such a large sum?” Tanner asked.

===Chinese===
「你们怎么会放出这么大一笔款子？」坦纳问。

===Original===
“Because of his uncle, Mr. Tanner. Sir William Ponson thought a lot of Mr. Cosgrove, and he would have helped him. Now that he is dead we shall get our money. We understand Mr. Cosgrove comes into a handsome legacy.”

===Chinese===
「看在他叔叔的份上，坦纳先生。威廉·庞森爵士很器重科斯格罗夫先生，本来是会帮衬他的。如今他死了，我们的钱也就到手了。我们听说科斯格罗夫先生要继承一笔可观的遗产。」

===Original===
When Tanner left the office he was more than satisfied as to the strength of Cosgrove’s motive for the crime. Far stronger it appeared to him than that of Austin. It looked as if he was on the right track at last.

===Chinese===
离开那间办公室时，坦纳对科斯格罗夫作案动机之强，已是再满意不过。在他看来，这动机比奥斯汀的强得多。看来他总算摸上正路了。

===Original===
Hailing a taxi, he handed the driver a pound in advance and instructed him in detail as to what he wished done. Then he stepped into the vehicle and was driven to Knightsbridge.

===Chinese===
他扬手唤来一辆出租汽车，先塞给司机一镑钱，把自己要办的事详详细细交代清楚。随后上了车，被载往骑士桥。

===Original===
Within view of Cosgrove’s chambers the car swung close to the sidewalk and the engine stopped. The driver sprang down, and opening the bonnet, became engrossed with his engine. It was obvious a slight mishap had taken place.

===Chinese===
在望得见科斯格罗夫寓所的地方，汽车一拐贴近人行道，熄了火。司机跳下车，掀开引擎盖，一头扎进机器里忙活起来。明摆着是出了点小故障。

===Original===
Tanner sat well back in the car watching the house before him. It was getting on towards one. For more than half an hour the repairs continued. Then the Inspector saw Cosgrove leave his door and hail a taxi. He called softly to his own driver, and the work at the engine being completed at just that moment, the latter mounted and started the car.

===Chinese===
坦纳深深缩在汽车后座里，盯着面前那幢房子。时间快到一点了。修理折腾了半个多钟头。这时探长看见科斯格罗夫出门，扬手叫了辆出租汽车。他轻声唤自己的司机——引擎偏赶在这当口修好了——司机跳上车，发动起来。

===Original===
“Keep that taxi in sight,” Tanner ordered as they moved forward.

===Chinese===
「盯住那辆出租车。」车子开动时，坦纳吩咐道。

===Original===
The chase was not a long one﻿—down Piccadilly, across the Circus and into Shaftesbury Avenue. There the quarry turned into a narrow lane and Tanner, leaping out of his taxi, saw the other stop at the stage door of the Follies Theatre. He turned back to his own car.

===Chinese===
这一场追踪不长——顺皮卡迪利而下，穿过皮卡迪利广场，拐进沙夫茨伯里大道（Shaftesbury Avenue）。到了那儿，目标折进一条窄巷；坦纳跳下自己的车，眼见那辆车停在「福利斯」剧院（Follies Theatre）的后门前。他折回自己车边。

===Original===
“Pick me up when I sign and follow it again,” he said to his driver, then, becoming absorbed in a bookseller’s window immediately opposite the end of the lane in which the other car stood, he waited.

===Chinese===
「我一打手势你就开车过来，咱们接着跟。」他对司机说；随即装出一副全副心思都在一家书店橱窗上的样子——那书店正对着那条窄巷的巷口，那辆车就停在巷里——他就在那儿等着。

===Original===
With the corner of his eye he had seen Cosgrove enter the theatre, and after some ten minutes he observed him emerge following a lady whom he handed into his taxi. Rapidly Tanner regained his own vehicle, and as the other swept out of the lane and turned west, his driver took up his former position behind it.

===Chinese===
他眼角里瞥见科斯格罗夫进了剧院；约莫十分钟后，又见他跟在一位女士身后出来，扶她上了自己的车。坦纳疾步回到自己车上；那辆车驶出窄巷向西一转，他的司机便又照旧缀了上去。

===Original===
Once again the chase was short. Reaching the Strand, the leading car turned into the courtyard of the Savoy. As he stepped out of his taxi Tanner was in time to see his victims entering the great building. He followed quickly to the restaurant, and while they were looking for a table, slipped a couple of pounds into the head waiter’s hand.

===Chinese===
这一回追踪也不长。到了斯特兰德街（the Strand），前头那辆车一拐，驶进萨伏伊的院子。坦纳下了出租汽车，正好望见他跟踪的这对男女走进那座大厦。他快步跟进餐厅，趁他们张罗座位的当口，往领班侍者手里塞了两镑钱。

===Original===
“I am from Scotland Yard,” he whispered. “Put me beside that lady and gentleman like a good fellow.”

===Chinese===
「我是苏格兰场的，」他压低声音说，「行个好，把我在那位小姐和先生近旁安个座。」

===Original===
The head waiter led him forward and presently he found himself seated at a small table immediately behind Cosgrove. The lady was on Cosgrove’s right and from where he sat the Inspector could see her without appearing to stare. He recognised her immediately as Miss Betty Belcher, one of the most talented and popular actresses in London.

===Chinese===
领班侍者把他引上前去，不多时他便坐到了科斯格罗夫正后方的一张小桌旁。那位女士坐在科斯格罗夫右手；从他坐的地方，探长可以把她看个清楚，又不显得失礼盯人。他一眼认出她是贝蒂·贝尔彻小姐（Miss Betty Belcher）——伦敦最有才华、最叫座的女演员之一。

===Original===
She was a woman of about thirty, small, sprightly, and rather inclined to stoutness. Her features were delicate, her complexion creamy, and her eyes large and of the lightest blue. Her lips were just a trifle thin, and in repose wore the suspicion of a pout. But her glory was her hair. It was of a deep rich gold, piled up in great masses above her low forehead. Famous for her play in light, sparkling parts, her vivacity on the stage was unrivalled. But here she was not vivacious. On the contrary, both she and Cosgrove seemed ill at ease. While the waiter was serving them they discoursed on everyday topics, but when he passed on their voices dropped and Tanner could no longer overhear them.

===Chinese===
她约莫三十岁年纪，娇小活泼，身段已略有些发福。眉目清秀，肤色如乳，一双大眼睛是极淡的蓝色。嘴唇只嫌稍薄了些，安静时总带着一丝似有若无的撅嘴意味。可她真正的荣耀是一头秀发——深浓的金色，厚厚堆积在低低的额头上方。她以出演轻快俏皮的角色驰名，台上的活泼灵动无出其右。可此刻她并不活泼。恰恰相反，她同科斯格罗夫都显得局促不安。侍者在旁伺候时，两人还聊些日常闲话；侍者一走开，他们便压低了嗓音，坦纳再也听不见了。

===Original===
It was evident from their expressions they were discussing some serious matter, and Tanner strained his ears to learn its nature. For a time he was unsuccessful, but at last during lulls in the general conversation he caught enough to enlighten him. Disconnectedly and without the context he heard Cosgrove use the words “inquest adjourned,” and “detective,” and later the lady said something very like “suspicion aroused” and once again, unquestionably, the phrase “the alibi should hold.”

===Chinese===
从两人的神情看，他们谈的显然是桩要紧事，坦纳竖起耳朵想听出个所以然。一时没有结果，但后来趁着满堂人声的低落间隙，他总算捕听到足够解惑的东西。零零碎碎、不着上下文，他听见科斯格罗夫说出「验尸审讯延期」和「侦探」几个词；随后那位女士说了句极像「嫌疑已经引起」的话，还有一句，千真万确，是「不在场证明站得住」。

===Original===
Inspector Tanner was extremely interested. Obviously they were talking about the Luce Manor tragedy, and from the reference to the alibi they seemed to have very firsthand information of Austin’s affairs. This, however, was natural enough and by no means suspicious. But the expression of anxiety on the lady’s face was not so natural. Tanner wished he was sure of its cause.

===Chinese===
坦纳探长大感兴趣。他们谈的显然是卢斯庄园的惨案；而从提到不在场证明的那句口风听来，他们对奥斯汀的事似乎知之甚详，而且是第一手的。不过这也再自然不过，绝谈不上可疑。倒是那位女士脸上焦灼的神色，不那么自然。坦纳但愿自己能拿准那是为了什么。

===Original===
After coffee Cosgrove lit a cigarette, and the Inspector was rather thrilled to notice it was of a light-brown colour. His thought turned to the end he had discovered in the Luce Manor boathouse. If Cosgrove’s case contained the same unusual brand as that found at Luce Manor, his suspicions would undoubtedly be strengthened.

===Chinese===
咖啡上过，科斯格罗夫点起一支香烟，探长颇为心头发热地注意到，那烟是浅褐色的。他的思绪转到自己在卢斯庄园船屋里发现的那枚烟蒂上。倘若科斯格罗夫烟盒里装的正是在卢斯庄园发现的那种不寻常的牌子，他的怀疑无疑又要加深一层。

===Original===
Cosgrove smoked quietly, while Tanner paid his bill and got ready to leave the restaurant. At last the others made a move, Cosgrove dropping the cigarette end into the saucer of his coffee cup. Tanner moved aside, and turned his back till they had passed, then returning to his table as if he had forgotten something, he rapidly picked up the cigarette end, quenched it and dropped it into one of the little boxes he always carried. Then he hurried out after his quarry, and as they took another taxi, reentered his own.

===Chinese===
科斯格罗夫安安静静地抽着烟，坦纳则付了账，做出准备离店的样子。末了那一对起身要走，科斯格罗夫随手把烟蒂丢进咖啡杯的碟子里。坦纳闪到一旁，背过身去等他们走过，然后装作落下了什么东西回到桌边，麻利地拾起那枚烟蒂，掐灭，丢进他随身常带的一只小盒子里。这才匆匆追出店门；见他们又上了一辆出租汽车，他也重新坐进自己那辆。

===Original===
This time they had a longer run. It was not till they reached Lyme Avenue, Chelsea, that the chase ended. There at the door of a block of flats the two dismounted. But Cosgrove did not go in. When the lady had disappeared he returned to his taxi, and started back towards London. Tanner’s driver had run on towards the end of the road, but he skilfully manoeuvred for position, and soon was back in his own place behind the other.

===Chinese===
这一趟跑得远些。一直追到切尔西（Chelsea）的莱姆大道（Lyme Avenue），跟踪才算到了头。两人在一幢公寓楼门口下了车。但科斯格罗夫没有进去。待那位女士的身影消失，他折回自己车上，往伦敦方向回驶。坦纳的司机本已直开到街尾，却巧妙地兜了个位，不多时又缀回那辆车后头。

===Original===
At the door of the Huntingdon Club in Piccadilly, Cosgrove dismounted, paid off his driver and entered the building. This suiting Tanner’s plans as well as anything else, he also paid his man, and after a few minutes followed Cosgrove into the club.

===Chinese===
在皮卡迪利的亨廷登俱乐部（Huntingdon Club）门口，科斯格罗夫下了车，付钱打发走司机，走进那座楼。这倒正合坦纳的打算，他也付了自己司机的车钱，隔了几分钟，跟着科斯格罗夫进了俱乐部。

===Original===
Handing the porter another of his false cards﻿—Mr. Percival Hepworth-Jones, The Constitutional﻿—he asked for Cosgrove, and was shown into a waiting-room.

===Chinese===
他递给门房另一张假名片——「珀西瓦尔·赫普沃斯-琼斯先生（Mr. Percival Hepworth-Jones），宪政俱乐部（The Constitutional）」——求见科斯格罗夫，被引进一间等候室。

===Original===
In about ten minutes Cosgrove appeared﻿—a questioning, puzzled look on his thin, good-looking face.

===Chinese===
约莫十分钟后，科斯格罗夫来了——清瘦俊秀的脸上带着探询而大惑不解的神情。

===Original===
“I must apologise, Mr. Ponson,” began the Inspector, “for sending in a card which is not my own. You are wondering where you have seen me before?”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生，我得向您致歉，」探长开口道，「递进来的名片不是我的。您正在纳闷，先前在哪儿见过我吧？」

===Original===
“I confess that I am. I know your face, but I can’t recall our meeting.”

===Chinese===
「不瞒您说，正是。您这张脸我认得，可想不起我们在哪儿打过交道。」

===Original===
“We didn’t meet, but you saw me at the inquest at Luce Manor. I am Inspector Tanner of Scotland Yard, and I have been put in charge of the case.”

===Chinese===
「我们不曾谋面，不过卢斯庄园验尸审讯那天，您见过我。我是苏格兰场的坦纳探长，这件案子已交由我主办。」

===Original===
The Inspector, who was watching the other keenly, noticed a sudden look flash across his face and then disappear. It was not exactly a look of alarm. Rather was it that of a man brought suddenly face to face with a danger he had long recognised﻿—a kind of bracing of himself to meet a crisis which was at last at hand. But his manner was free from any trace of anxiety as he motioned his visitor to a chair.

===Chinese===
探长一直锐利地注视着对方，只见他脸上倏地掠过一种神情，随即隐去。那不太像惊惶。倒更像一个人猛然间同他早认下的危险打了个照面——一种打起精神、迎候危机的架势，那危机终于临头了。但他请来客落座时，举止间已不见一丝焦灼。

===Original===
“Ah yes,” he said, “of course. I remember now. My cousin, Austin Ponson, told me about you. So you fear my uncle’s end might have been suicide? It is a horrible idea, and you won’t mind my saying that I cannot but think you are wrong. He was not at all that kind of man.”

===Chinese===
「啊，对了，」他说，「当然。我这会儿想起来了。我堂弟奥斯汀·庞森说起过您。这么说，您担心我叔叔的死可能是自杀？这念头太可怕了；恕我直言，我不能不认为您想错了。他压根儿不是那种人。」

===Original===
“So I am beginning to think, Mr. Ponson. But orders are orders. I have been told to investigate and report, and I must do so.”

===Chinese===
「我也开始这么想了，庞森先生。可是命令归命令。上头吩咐我调查呈报，我就不能不办。」

===Original===
Cosgrove agreed and they conversed for some time. Tanner asked a good many questions, but without learning anything of interest. Then at last he came to the real object of his visit. Speaking very much as he had done to Austin, he asked Cosgrove to state his own movements on the fatal Wednesday night. Cosgrove, unlike Austin, got on his high horse.

===Chinese===
科斯格罗夫表示认同，两人攀谈了一阵。坦纳问了不少问题，却没问出什么值得留意的东西。末了，他终于说到此行的真正来意。他措辞同对奥斯汀说的差不太多，请科斯格罗夫交代案发那个星期三晚上自己的行踪。科斯格罗夫却不像奥斯汀那样好说话，立刻端起了架子。

===Original===
“Really, Inspector, I think that’s a little too much. Why should I tell you anything of the kind? What has it to do with the affair?”

===Chinese===
「说真的，探长，这未免有点过分了吧。我凭什么要向您交代这种事？这同案子有什么相干？」

===Original===
Tanner could be direct enough when he saw cause.

===Chinese===
坦纳认准了由头，说话也够直接。

===Original===
“Why this, Mr. Ponson,” he answered, still watching the other keenly. “As you must see, the possibility of suicide involves that of murder also. The question I have asked you is asked in such cases as a matter of course to everyone interested.”

===Chinese===
「相干在这里，庞森先生，」他答道，目光依旧锐利地盯着对方，「您必定看得出，既有自杀的可能，便也牵连到谋杀的可能。我方才问您的问题，办这类案子时，对每一个利害相关的人照例都要问到。」

===Original===
Cosgrove started slightly at the last words.

===Chinese===
听到最后几个字，科斯格罗夫微微一震。

===Original===
“Interested?” he repeated. “What do you mean by that? Do you think I was interested?”

===Chinese===
「利害相关？」他重复道，「这话什么意思？您以为我利害相关？」

===Original===
“Mr. Ponson, as reasonable men we must both see that you were interested. You will forgive me﻿—I don’t wish to be offensive﻿—but it is common knowledge that you are in low water financially, and that you benefit considerably under the will.”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生，咱们都是讲道理的人，都得承认您确实利害相关。恕我直言——我无意冒犯——可您手头拮据，人尽皆知；而那份遗嘱里，您得到的好处也着实可观。」

===Original===
“Good Heavens!” Ponson cried angrily, “and do you actually mean to say you suspect me of *murdering* Sir William?”

===Chinese===
「天哪！」庞森怒气冲冲地喊道，「照这么说，您莫非当真疑心是我*谋害*了威廉爵士？」

===Original===
“I mean nothing of the kind, Mr. Ponson. I only want to justify myself in asking you the question to which you have just objected.”

===Chinese===
「我绝无此意，庞森先生。您方才不肯回答我那个问题，我不过是想说明白，我凭什么要问罢了。」

===Original===
Cosgrove did not reply. At last Tanner went on in a courteous tone:

===Chinese===
科斯格罗夫没有作声。末了，坦纳换上客客气气的口吻接着说：

===Original===
“Obviously, I cannot force you to answer me, Mr. Ponson. But it must be clear to you that should you decline you may raise suspicions which, no matter how unfounded, are bound to be unpleasant. Please don’t think I am speaking threateningly. You can see the matter as clearly as I.”

===Chinese===
「当然，我不能强迫您回答，庞森先生。可您也必定看得清楚：您若是回绝，也许反而惹来嫌疑——那嫌疑纵然全无根据，终究叫人不痛快。请别以为我在出言恫吓。这层利害，您同我一样看得明白。」

===Original===
The Inspector’s moderation appeared to bring Cosgrove, to a decision. He moved nervously, and then replied:

===Chinese===
探长的克制似乎促使科斯格罗夫拿定了主意。他局促地动了动身子，答道：

===Original===
“I suppose you are right, Inspector, and that I have no cause to resent your inquiries. I haven’t the slightest objection to telling you where I was when you explain yourself as you have just done.”

===Chinese===
「我想您说得对，探长，您的查问我没什么可怨的。您既然像刚才这样把话说明白了，我那晚在哪儿，我也毫无不可奉告之理。」

===Original===
“I am much obliged. It will save me a lot of trouble.”

===Chinese===
「多谢。这能省我不少事。」

===Original===
Cosgrove settled himself more comfortably in his armchair.

===Chinese===
科斯格罗夫在扶手椅里换了个更舒坦的坐姿。

===Original===
“I remember that Wednesday night,” he began, “for I did one of the silliest things that night that I have ever been guilty of, and I think I may say without bragging I am usually as wide awake as most people. But I shall tell you.

===Chinese===
「我记得那个星期三晚上，」他开了腔，「因为那天夜里我干了一件平生数得着的蠢事；不夸口地说，我平素同常人一样精明，可还是干了。不过我这就讲给您听。

===Original===
“You know, or perhaps you don’t, that I go in a good deal for racing. I have a small stable not far from Bath, and I make a bit off dealing and training as well as on the course. For some time I have wanted another horse, and there was one for sale near Montrose that seemed the thing, so much so that I determined to run down and see it. I have a good many engagements, mostly of a social kind, and I found that the only day I could go was the Thursday of last week﻿—the day after my poor Uncle disappeared. There was an ‘At Home’ at the Duchess of Frothingham’s on the Wednesday afternoon, and a ball at Lincolnshire House on Friday evening, at both of which I wished to be present, so I decided to travel to Montrose by the night train on Wednesday, see the horse on Thursday, return to London that night, and get some sleep on Friday, so as to be fresh for the evening.

===Chinese===
「您知道也罢，不知道也罢，我在赛马上头是下了大本钱的。我在巴斯（Bath）附近有个小马房，贩马、练马，加上赛场上的进项，都能赚上一笔。早想再添一匹马了，恰听说蒙特罗斯（Montrose）附近有一匹待售，瞧着正合心意，合意到我一狠心决定专程跑去看一趟。我的应酬很多，大半是社交场面，盘算下来，唯一抽得出身的只有上星期四——就是我可怜的叔叔失踪的第二天。星期三下午，弗罗辛厄姆公爵夫人（Duchess of Frothingham）府上有场『午后招待会』（At Home），星期五晚上林肯郡府（Lincolnshire House）又有舞会，两处我都想在场。于是我定下主意：星期三坐夜车去蒙特罗斯，星期四相马，当晚回伦敦，星期五白天补一觉，好在晚间养足精神赴会。

===Original===
“This programme I carried out, but not quite as I had intended. I was at the Duchess of Frothingham’s till a little after five, when I returned home, dined and drove to King’s Cross to catch the 7:15 for the north. This train was due at Montrose about 5:30 the next morning, and it was my intention to drive the three or four miles out to the training stables and see the horses at their early exercise, returning to town when convenient.

===Chinese===
「这个计划我照办了，只是同原打算的稍有出入。我在弗罗辛厄姆公爵夫人府上待到五点过一点儿，回家用罢晚餐，便驱车前往国王十字车站（King's Cross），赶 7:15 北上的火车。这趟车次日清晨约 5:30 抵达蒙特罗斯；我打算一到就驱车出城三四英里，直奔那处练马场，看马群清晨操练，再择便回城。

===Original===
“I reached King’s Cross in good time, found the sleeping berth which I had engaged was ready for me, deposited my things therein, and began to make myself comfortable. Wishing to smoke I drew out my cigar-case, and then I discovered it was empty. I was considerably annoyed, not only for my own sake, but because I wished to be able to offer a smoke to the man with whom I hoped to deal. I glanced at my watch. It was still ten minutes past seven. Thinking I had plenty of time I seized my hat and ran to the refreshment room, which was close by. As I was selecting some cigars I glanced up at the refreshment room clock. It was pointing to 7:15.

===Chinese===
「我到国王十字车站时间充裕，找到订下的卧铺，铺位已经备好，便把行李安顿进去，着手安顿自己。想抽口烟，一摸雪茄盒，才发现是空的。我心里老大不痛快：不光是为自己，还因为我盼着同那位卖主打交道时能敬人家一支烟。看看表，才过 7:10。自以为时间绰绰有余，我抓起帽子就奔了站台近旁的小卖部。正挑着雪茄，一抬眼瞅见小卖部的钟——时针正指 7:15。

===Original===
“ ‘Your clock’s fast, I suppose?’ I said.

===Chinese===
「『你们的钟快了吧？』我说。

===Original===
“ ‘No, I don’t think so,’ the girl answered.

===Chinese===
「『不，我看不快。』女店员答道。

===Original===
“I left the cigars on the counter and ran out on the platform. But the clock was right, and all I saw was my train moving off. It was already going too fast to board, and I had to accept the fact that it was gone with my luggage in the sleeping berth.

===Chinese===
「我把雪茄撂在柜台上，拔腿奔上月台。可那钟没走错，我只来得及望见我的火车正开出去。车速已快得无法再攀上去，我只好认下这事实：车走了，连同我卧铺里的行李。

===Original===
“I looked at my watch. It was still showing two minutes to the quarter. It was usually an excellent timekeeper, but in some way which I can’t account for just on the occasion that mattered, it was three minutes slow. Pretty maddening, wasn’t it?”

===Chinese===
「我看看表。表针还差两分才到一刻。它向来走得极准，偏偏在这个要命的节骨眼上，不知怎么就慢了三分钟。气人不气人？」

===Original===
“It’s the way things happen,” said Tanner philosophically.

===Chinese===
「事情偏就是这么巧。」坦纳带着几分哲人气度地说。

===Original===
“Isn’t it? Well, I got my cigars, and then I went to the Stationmaster’s Office and asked what I should do. It appeared there was a comparatively slow train to Dundee at 8:30, but the next to Montrose was the express at 10:30. Even by taking a car from Dundee this earlier train would not get me to the stables in time for the morning exercise, so I decided my best plan would be to take the 10:30. I then asked about a sleeping berth. But here my luck was out. All those on the 10:30 were engaged.

===Chinese===
「可不是么？唉，我买了雪茄，便到站长室去讨主意。原来 8:30 有一班去邓迪（Dundee）的较慢的车，可下一班到蒙特罗斯的，要等 10:30 的快车。即便从邓迪雇汽车赶路，坐早一班也赶不上清晨操练，于是我认定上策还是坐 10:30。跟着去问卧铺——这回运气可不济了，10:30 那趟的卧铺全订了出去。

===Original===
“ ‘And what about my things that have gone on in the 7:15?’ I asked.

===Chinese===
「『那我随 7:15 那趟车先走的行李怎么办？』我问。

===Original===
“ ‘They will be all right,’ the clerk answered. ‘The first stop of both the 7:15 and the 10:30 is Grantham, and I shall wire the agent there to have your things collected from the 7:15 and handed in to you on the 10:30.’

===Chinese===
「『不碍事的，』站员答道，『7:15 和 10:30 两趟车头一站都停格兰瑟姆（Grantham），我这就给那边的司事拍电报，叫人把您的行李从 7:15 上卸下，转到 10:30 上交还给您。』

===Original===
“This seemed the best arrangement, and I thanked the man, and having telegraphed to the dealer at Montrose, I strolled out of the station, wondering how I could best put in my three hours.

===Chinese===
「这安排再妥当不过，我谢过那位站员，又给蒙特罗斯的马贩子拍了电报，便踱出车站，盘算这三个钟头怎么打发才好。

===Original===
“More by force of habit than otherwise I took a taxi and drove back to my rooms. But my little adventure had made me restless, and I couldn’t settle down to spend a lonely evening. I would have liked to go to the Follies and see my friend Miss Betty Belcher, but I knew she was acting all the evening except during part of the second act, say from about 9:30 till 10:00. To pass the time till 9:30 I at last decided to go to the Empire. I did so. I left my rooms almost at once, drove to the Empire, sat there for an hour or so, and then went to the Follies. From about 9:30 till 10:00 I sat with Miss Belcher in her room, then when it was nearly time for her to go on again I made my adieux and returned to King’s Cross. I took care not to miss the 10:30, and at Grantham I got out and found a porter looking for me with my things. I duly reached Montrose about half-past eight next morning. So I trust you won’t any longer suspect me of being at Halford.”

===Chinese===
「多半是习惯使然，我坐上出租汽车回了自己的寓所。可这场小小的意外搅得我心神不宁，安不下心来独自消磨一个晚上。本想去『福利斯』看我的朋友贝蒂·贝尔彻小姐，可我知道她整晚都要登台，只有第二幕的一小段空当，大约从 9:30 到 10:00。为打发 9:30 之前的时光，我最后决定去帝国剧院（the Empire）。也真去了。我几乎立刻出了门，坐车到帝国剧院，坐了个把钟头，再转去『福利斯』。约莫 9:30 到 10:00，我在贝尔彻小姐的房间里陪她坐了坐；等她快到再度上场的时分，我便道了别，回国王十字去。这一回我格外当心，再没误 10:30 的车；到格兰瑟姆我下了车，正有个搬运工举着我的行李寻我。次日早晨八点半，我如期到了蒙特罗斯。所以，我想您总该不再疑心我到过哈尔福德了吧。」

===Original===
Tanner laughed.

===Chinese===
坦纳笑了。

===Original===
“How was I to know, sir, what you did until you told me? I am very greatly obliged. You have saved me an immensity of useless work.”

===Chinese===
「您不说，先生，我又如何知道您的行踪呢？实在感激之至。您替我省下了一大堆无用功。」

===Original===
Cosgrove Ponson was quite mollified. He seemed like a man from whose shoulders a weight had been removed. He took out his cigar-case.

===Chinese===
科斯格罗夫·庞森心气全平了。他那样子，像一个卸下了肩头重担的人。他掏出雪茄盒。

===Original===
“I don’t know if it will confirm my story,” he said smiling, “but I hope in any case you will smoke one of the actual cigars I bought at King’s Cross.”

===Chinese===
「不知这一支能否给我的话作个见证，」他含笑说，「不过无论如何，望您赏光，抽一支我在国王十字买下的那批雪茄中的真货。」

===Original===
The Inspector accepted. He asked a few more questions, obtained the name of the Montrose horse-dealer, and then, with further compliments and thanks, took his leave.

===Chinese===
探长领了情。又问了几个问题，记下蒙特罗斯那位马贩子的姓名，然后再三致意道谢，起身告辞。

===Original===
It was obvious to him that, if true, Cosgrove’s story made a complete alibi. If he had been at King’s Cross at 7:30 o’clock, at the Follies Theatre at 9:30, and had travelled from King’s Cross to Montrose by the 10:30 express it was out of the question that he could have been at Luce Manor. Satisfied as Tanner was as to the soundness of Austin Ponson’s alibi, Cosgrove’s was even more convincing, and what was better still, it would be easier to test. Though he did not believe Cosgrove would make such a statement unless it could bear the touchstone of inquiry, Tanner felt his obvious next business would be to check what he had just heard so fully as to remove all possible doubt of Cosgrove’s innocence.


===Chinese===
他看得很明白：如果属实，科斯格罗夫这套说法便构成一份完整的不在场证明。倘若他 7:30 在国王十字，9:30 在『福利斯』剧院，又坐 10:30 的快车从国王十字去了蒙特罗斯，那他绝无可能分身到过卢斯庄园。坦纳对奥斯汀·庞森不在场证明的牢靠固然已然信服，而科斯格罗夫这份更加令人信服；更妙的是，查证起来也更容易。虽说他不相信科斯格罗夫会抛出一套经不起盘查试金石的陈述，但他心里明白，下一步显然就是把方才所闻逐一核实，核实到再无半点可疑之处，把科斯格罗夫的清白坐实。

## VII · Cosgrove’s Trip North / VII · 科斯格罗夫北上之行

===Original===
On leaving Cosgrove Ponson, Inspector Tanner walked slowly up the shady side of Regent Street, his mind still running on the statement to which he had just listened. To test its truth was his obvious first duty, and as he sauntered along he considered the quickest and most thorough means of doing so. From the very nature of the story he felt inclined to believe it. Too much independent testimony seemed to be available for the alibi to be a fraud.

===Chinese===
离开科斯格罗夫·庞森之后，坦纳探长沿着摄政街（Regent Street）背阴的一侧缓步而行，脑子里还在转着他方才听到的那番供述。要查明它的真伪，显然是他头一桩分内之事；他一路信步走去，心里盘算着办这件事最快又最彻底的法子。单看这故事本身的性质，他就倾向于相信它。可供查证的独立证词似乎太多，这份不在场证明不像是捏造的。

===Original===
And if this was so, he, Tanner, was on the wrong track, and was wasting time. He had already lost nearly a week over Austin, and all the time he was working on these blind alleys the real scent was getting cold. But as he reviewed the facts he had learnt, he felt he could hardly have acted otherwise than as he had.

===Chinese===
倘若果真如此，那么他坦纳就走错了路，白白耗着工夫。他在奥斯汀身上已经搭进去将近一个星期，而在这些死胡同里打转的每一刻，真正的线索都在冷下去。不过，把他查知的种种事实在心里过了一遍，他又觉得，换作旁人只怕也只能这么办。

===Original===
He considered Cosgrove’s statement point by point. Firstly, was it true he could only have gone to Scotland on the night in question of all others? In answer to this it should be easy to find out if he really was at the Duchess of Frothingham’s on the same afternoon. Then the missing of the train at King’s Cross must be known to several persons﻿—the clerk at the stationmaster’s office, the barmaid who sold the cigars, as well possibly as the sleeping-car attendant, and the telegraph-office clerk, and copies of the wires to Grantham and to Montrose should be available. He was not sure that confirmation of Cosgrove’s visit to the Empire would be obtainable, though some attendant might have noticed him. But there should be ample proof of his call on Miss Belcher at the Follies Theatre. Not only would there be the testimony of Miss Belcher herself, but some of the many attendants must almost certainly have seen him. Then, if Cosgrove was not actually seen leaving London by the 10:30 p.m., he must have been observed in that train at Grantham, where his luggage was handed in to him. Finally, to ensure that he did not leave the train there and return to Luce Manor, as well as to test the genuineness of the whole journey, Tanner could see Colonel Archdale, the horse owner of Montrose.

===Chinese===
他把科斯格罗夫的供述逐条推敲。首先，他当真非得在那么多夜里单单挑中案发当晚去苏格兰么？要回答这一点，查明他那天下午是否真在弗罗辛厄姆公爵夫人府上，应当不难。其次，在国王十字误车一事必然有若干人知情——站长室的职员、卖雪茄的女店员，说不定还有卧铺车厢的侍者、电报房的报务员，而发往格兰瑟姆和蒙特罗斯的电报底稿也该查得到。至于科斯格罗夫去帝国剧院一事能否取得佐证，他心里没底，虽说哪个侍者兴许留意过他。但科斯格罗夫到「福利斯」剧院拜访贝尔彻小姐一事，证据应当绰绰有余：不但有贝尔彻小姐本人的证词，那么多侍者里也几乎必定有人见过他。再次，纵然没人亲眼见科斯格罗夫乘 10:30 的车离伦敦，到了格兰瑟姆——他的行李就是在那儿交到他手上的——总该有人在那趟车上见过他。最后，为确保他没有在格兰瑟姆中途下车折返卢斯庄园，也为检验整个旅程的真伪，坦纳可以去见蒙特罗斯的马主阿奇代尔上校（Colonel Archdale）。

===Original===
And then another point struck him. What, he wondered, were the precise relations between Cosgrove Ponson and Miss Betty Belcher? From their demeanour at the restaurant, they were certainly on pretty intimate terms. In this case could Miss Belcher’s testimony to Cosgrove’s call at the theatre be relied on? Here was what undoubtedly might be a flaw in the alibi, and he felt he must handle this part of it with special care.

===Chinese===
跟着又有一个念头攫住了他。科斯格罗夫·庞森和贝蒂·贝尔彻小姐之间，究竟是什么关系？从两人在饭馆里的举止看，交情显然相当亲昵。倘若如此，贝尔彻小姐关于科斯格罗夫到剧院来访的证词，还能信得过么？这无疑可能是这份不在场证明上的一个破绽，他觉得这一部分务必格外小心地对待。

===Original===
As he reached this point in his cogitations he arrived at the goal of his walk﻿—a small but extremely fashionable tobacconist’s in Oxford Street. Handing in his card, he asked to see the manager.

===Chinese===
