他正想到这里，也走到了此行的目的地——牛津街（Oxford Street）上一家门面不大却极时髦的烟店。他递进名片，求见经理。

===Original===
He was shown into a small, neatly-furnished office, and there after a few minutes a tall young man in a grey frock coat joined him.

===Chinese===
他被引进一间陈设整洁的小办公室，几分钟后，一个穿灰色长礼服的高个子年轻人进来了。

===Original===
“Hallo, Tony,” said the Inspector when the door had closed.

===Chinese===
等门关严实了，探长招呼道：「喂，托尼（Tony）。」

===Original===
The newcomer greeted his visitor breezily.

===Chinese===
新来的人满面春风地招呼客人。

===Original===
“Why, Tanner, old son,” he cried, “how goes it? You’re a stranger, you are. And what’s blown you in today?”

===Chinese===
「嗬，坦纳，老伙计，」他嚷道，「日子过得怎样？你可成了稀客啦，真是。今儿是什么风把您吹来了？」

===Original===
“Business as usual. I want your help.”

===Chinese===
「老样子，公事。我要你帮个忙。」

===Original===
“You bet your life! And when you want help you know the right place to come. Tony B. won’t see you left, eh?”

===Chinese===
「那还用说！您要帮忙，就知道该上哪儿来。托尼·B 绝不会让您白跑一趟，嗯？」

===Original===
“I know that. You’re not as bad as you look.”

===Chinese===
「这我知道。你啊，人不像长相那么糟。」

===Original===
The other winked slowly.

===Chinese===
对方慢悠悠地眨了眨眼。

===Original===
“And what’s little Albert’s trouble this time?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「小阿尔伯特这回又遇上什么难题啦？」他问。

===Original===
“Why this,” Tanner answered, taking out his two little boxes and shaking the cigarette ends on to the table. “I want to know what kind of cigarettes these are, and when they were smoked.”

===Chinese===
「喏，就是这个，」坦纳答道，掏出那两个小盒子，把烟头抖在桌上。「我想知道这是什么牌子的香烟，又是什么时候抽的。」

===Original===
“H’m. Think I’m a blooming crystal-gazer, do you? Or one of those Zancigs﻿—what do you call ’em?”

===Chinese===
「哼。您当我是顶着水晶球掐算的活神仙？还是赞西格（Zancigs）那类读心术士——他们叫什么来着？」

===Original===
As he spoke he was examining the ends with a strong glass. Then he smelt them, drew out a shred of tobacco from each and tasted it, and finally picked them up and took them out of the room.

===Chinese===
他嘴里说着，手上已经拿起放大镜端详那几个烟头。接着又闻了闻，从每个烟头里抽出一丝烟丝尝了尝，末了把它们拾起来，拿出了房间。

===Original===
“Sit tight, Albert,” he remarked as he left, “and keep your little hands out of mischief till daddy comes back.”

===Chinese===
「坐稳了，阿尔伯特，」他临出门时说，「乖乖管住你那双小手，别惹祸，等爸爸回来。」

===Original===
In a few minutes he reentered and laid the ends down on the table with beside them a whole cigarette of a dark yellow colour.

===Chinese===
几分钟后他回转来，把烟头摆回桌上，旁边放了一支深黄色的整支香烟。

===Original===
“There you are, sonny,” he announced. “All chips of the old block, those are.”

===Chinese===
「瞧好吧，小子，」他宣布道。「这几个跟这一支，全是一个模子里刻出来的。」

===Original===
“And what are they?” Tanner queried, examining the little brown tube with interest.

===Chinese===
「那么它们是什么来头？」坦纳一边问，一边饶有兴致地端详那支褐色的小纸卷。

===Original===
“Costly rubies, rich and rare,” his friend assured him. “They’re what we call ‘Muriquis,’ and they’re made in Rio by a firm called Oliveira. There ain’t many in this village, I tell you. Who are you trailing now? Is it Henry Ford or only his Majesty the King?”

===Chinese===
「名贵的红宝石，又富丽又稀罕，」他的朋友言之凿凿。「这就是我们叫做『穆里基』（Muriquis）的烟，里约一家名叫奥利维拉（Oliveira）的公司出品。我跟您说，这城里抽这玩意儿的可没几位。您这回盯的是谁的梢？亨利·福特呢，还是国王陛下？」

===Original===
“Neither,” Tanner returned seriously, “it’s that Ponson case I’m on.”

===Chinese===
「都不是，」坦纳正色答道，「是我手头那桩庞森家的案子。」

===Original===
“Never heard of it. But Ponson knows his way about in cigarettes anyway, you bet your life.”

===Chinese===
「倒没听说过。不过那位庞森在香烟上头是真在行，这没跑。」

===Original===
“And how long since they were smoked? Can you tell me that?”

===Chinese===
「那么，这两支烟是多久以前抽的？这个您说得上么？」

===Original===
“Nope. Not Tony B. This one about an hour; this one about a week at a guess. But don’t you take all you hear for gospel. I don’t know, as the girl said when her lover proposed.”

===Chinese===
「说不上。托尼·B 说不上。这一支，约莫一个钟头；这一支，估摸着有一个礼拜。不过您可别把听见的话句句当福音书。『我说不准』——姑娘听情人求婚时就是这么说的。」

===Original===
Tanner, though more bored with his friend’s conversation every time he met him, remained chatting for some minutes. The two men had been at school together, and the Inspector kept up the acquaintanceship because of the valuable information he frequently got on matters connected with tobacco. But as soon as possible he took his leave, breathing a sigh of relief when he found himself once more in the street.

===Chinese===
坦纳每回见这位朋友，都觉得他的贫嘴更叫人吃不消一分，可仍旧陪着聊了几分钟。两人是同窗，探长所以维持这段交情，是因为在烟草的事情上常能从他那里得到极有用的消息。但一逮着机会他便起身告辞，等重新站到街上，才舒了一口气。

===Original===
His interest was considerably aroused by the news he had just received. The suspicions he had entertained of Cosgrove had been somewhat lulled to rest by the latter’s story. But the fact that the cigarette-end found in the boathouse at Luce Manor was of that same rare kind which Cosgrove smoked revived all his doubts, and made him more than ever resolved to test the alibi to the utmost limit of his ability.

===Chinese===
他刚得到的消息大大勾起了他的兴味。他原先对科斯格罗夫抱有的疑心，已被后者那番供述抚平了几分；但卢斯庄园船屋里发现的那枚烟头，恰恰正是科斯格罗夫常抽的那种罕有牌子——这一事实又将他所有的疑团尽数唤醒，使他比先前更加铁了心，要竭尽所能把这份不在场证明查验到底。

===Original===
Before leaving Halford, Tanner had written to the photographers whose names he had found on the prints in the drawing-room at Luce Manor, ordering copies of Sir William’s, Austin’s, and Cosgrove’s photographs. The studio was in Regent Street, and hailing a taxi, Tanner drove there. The photographs were ready, and he put one of each in his pocket. Also he selected prints of three or four other men as like in appearance to the cousins as he could find. Then he went on to the Duchess of Frothingham’s house in Park Lane.

===Chinese===
离开哈尔福德之前，坦纳已照着他在卢斯庄园客厅那些照片上查到的照相馆字号去信，订了威廉爵士、奥斯汀和科斯格罗夫三人的相片。照相馆就在摄政街，坦纳唤来一辆出租汽车，径直驶去。照片已经备好，他每样取了一张揣进口袋，又挑了三四张旁人的——尽量挑同这对堂兄弟相貌相像的。随后他再往公园巷（Park Lane）弗罗辛厄姆公爵夫人府上去。

===Original===
He saw her Grace’s butler, and representing himself as a reporter on the staff of a well-known society journal, asked for a list of the guests present at the “At Home” on the Wednesday of the murder, discreetly insinuating that he was prepared to pay for the trouble given. The addendum had the desired effect, and after a considerable delay a copy of the list was in Tanner’s hands. A glance at it showed Cosgrove’s name among the others, and a few judicious questions established the fact that he had actually been present.

===Chinese===
他见到了公爵夫人的管家，自称是一家知名社交刊物的记者，讨要案发那个星期三「午后招待会」的来宾名单，又不动声色地暗示，这点麻烦他是愿意破费酬谢的。这补充的一句收到了预期的效果，耽搁了好一阵之后，一份名单抄件便到了坦纳手里。扫一眼便在众名之中看到科斯格罗夫的名字，再几个问得恰到好处的问题，就坐实了他当日确曾到场。

===Original===
Once more in the street, Tanner looked at his watch. It was after six o’clock.

===Chinese===
重新回到街上，坦纳看了看表。已经六点多了。

===Original===
“A little dinner and then the Empire,” he said to himself as he turned into Piccadilly. He had decided his first step must be to apply to those sources of information which could not possibly be interested in Cosgrove’s affairs; afterwards, if need be, hearing what Miss Belcher had to say on the same subject.

===Chinese===
「先随便吃点晚饭，然后去帝国剧院。」他拐上皮卡迪利时自言自语。他已拿定主意，第一步必须去找那些绝无可能与科斯格罗夫利害相关的消息来源；随后，如有必要，再听贝尔彻小姐就同一件事怎么说。

===Original===
A couple of hours later he reached the Empire. Here he made exhaustive inquiries, but without finding anyone who had seen his man. But he was not greatly disappointed, as he had already realised that confirmation of this part of the alibi was problematical, if not unlikely.

===Chinese===
两个钟头后，他到了帝国剧院。他在那里穷根究底地打问了一番，却没找到一个见过他要找的人。但他并不怎么失望，因为早已明白，这部分不在场证明能否取得佐证本就悬，弄不好根本无从谈起。

===Original===
Returning to his taxi, he continued his journey till he reached King’s Cross. It was just nine o’clock, and the great station was partially deserted, there being a lull in the traffic about that hour. For the first time that day Tanner felt cool, and he began to realise that he was tired. But apart from the general urgency of his business, he expected the persons he wished to see were on evening duty, and he decided he must finish his inquiries then and there. He therefore went to the stationmaster’s office, and sent in his card. A dark, intelligent looking young man with an alert manner received him, and to him Tanner explained his business.

===Chinese===
他回到自己的车里，继续赶路，直到国王十字。刚九点，那座大车站已半见空落——这个钟点客运正有一段间歇。坦纳这一天头一回觉出凉意，也开始意识到自己累了。但他的差事本就十万火急，何况他要见的人想必都当夜班，便决定索性一鼓作气把查问办完。于是他走到站长室，递进名片。出来接待他的是个肤色黝黑、模样伶俐、神气干练的年轻人，坦纳便向他说明来意。

===Original===
“I did hear something about it,” the young man returned. “If you will wait a moment I’ll make inquiries.”

===Chinese===
「那件事我确曾听说过一点，」年轻人答道。「您稍候片刻，我去问问。」

===Original===
He left the room, returning presently with a clerk.

===Chinese===
他出了屋，不多时领着一个职员回来。

===Original===
“Mr. Williams here remembers the affair. He dealt with it. Tell this gentleman what you know, Williams.”

===Chinese===
「这位威廉斯（Williams）先生记得那桩事，就是他经手的。威廉斯，把你所知道的告诉这位先生。」

===Original===
“On Wednesday evening, the 7th instant, about 7:20 or 25,” began Williams, “a man called at the office and said he had booked a berth to Montrose on the 7:15 p.m., but that he had missed the train while in the refreshment room. He said his suitcase and waterproof had gone on in the train, and he asked what I would advise him to do.”

===Chinese===
「本月 7 日，星期三晚上，大约 7:20 到 7:25 之间，」威廉斯开了腔，「有位先生到办公室来，说自己在 7:15 那班北上蒙特罗斯的车上订了卧铺，却在小卖部里误了车。他说他的手提箱和雨衣都随车走了，问我看他该怎么办。」

===Original===
“That’s the man,” said Tanner, nodding. “Yes?”

===Chinese===
「就是这个人，」坦纳点头道。「后来呢？」

===Original===
“I told him the trains. The next to Montrose was the 10:30 p.m., and his mistake only meant that he would reach there at 8:24 a.m. instead of 5:25. But it seemed he wanted to arrive early, and I mentioned the 8:30 p.m. which runs from here to Dundee, suggesting he could go on by car. But on going into it he decided even this would be too late, and said he would travel on the 10:30. With regard to his luggage I offered to wire Grantham, which is the first stop of both the 7:15 p.m. and the 10:30, to have it collected from the sleeping car on the 7:15 p.m., and put into the 10:30. He agreed to this, and I sent the telegram at once.”

===Chinese===
「我把车次讲给他听。下一班到蒙特罗斯的是 10:30 的车，他这一误，无非是次日 8:24 才到，赶不上 5:25。可他像是急着早到，我便提起 8:30 那班从这里开往邓迪的车，说可以先坐到那儿再雇汽车接着赶。但他一盘算，觉得连这也太迟，就说还是坐 10:30。至于行李，我提出给格兰瑟姆拍电报——7:15 和 10:30 两班车都在那儿头一个停靠——叫人把行李从 7:15 的卧铺车厢里取出来，转到 10:30 那班上去。他同意了，我当即把电报发了出去。」

===Original===
“Would you know the man if you saw him again?”

===Chinese===
「要是再见到这个人，您还认得出么？」

===Original===
“Yes, I believe I should.”

===Chinese===
「认得，我想认得出。」

===Original===
“Any of these he?” and Tanner handed over the half-dozen photographs.

===Chinese===
「这些人里有他没有？」坦纳把那半打照片递了过去。

===Original===
The clerk instantly passed over Sir William’s and those of the strangers, then he examined Austin’s for some moments with a puzzled expression, but when he came to Cosgrove’s he hesitated no longer.

===Chinese===
那职员霎时便略过威廉爵士的和几个陌生人的照片，端详奥斯汀的那张时还面带困惑地琢磨了一阵，可翻到科斯格罗夫的那张，就再也不迟疑了。

===Original===
“That’s the man,” he said, repeating Tanner’s words of a moment before, “I should know him anywhere.”

===Chinese===
「就是这个人，」他重复着坦纳方才的话说，「到哪儿我都认得出他。」

===Original===
“So far so good,” thought Tanner as he stepped out once more on to the concourse. “Now for the refreshment room.”

===Chinese===
「到目前为止还算顺利，」坦纳重新迈步走到站前大厅里，心里想道。「接下来是小卖部。」

===Original===
He found the platform from which the 7:15 had started on the night in question, and looked about him. There was little doubt as to where Cosgrove had gone for his cigars. On the platform itself was a large sign “First-Class-Refreshment Room.” The Inspector pushed open the door and entered.

===Chinese===
他找到案发当晚 7:15 那班车的发车站台，四下打量。科斯格罗夫到哪里买的雪茄，几乎毋庸置疑。站台上就立着一块大牌子：「头等小卖部」（First-Class-Refreshment Room）。探长推门而入。

===Original===
“Good evening,” he said, raising his hat politely to the presiding goddess. “I want a few cigars, please.”

===Chinese===
「晚上好，」他彬彬有礼地向柜台后当值的女神抬了抬帽子，说道。「劳驾，我买几支雪茄。」

===Original===
“I have only these,” the girl answered, placing two partially emptied boxes before him.

===Chinese===
「只有这两样了，」姑娘答道，把两盒已去了些的雪茄摆到他面前。

===Original===
Tanner examined them.

===Chinese===
坦纳察看了一番。

===Original===
“I am not much of a judge,” he informed her, “but these look the lighter. I’ll have half a dozen, please. That is,” he went on with a whimsical glance at the clock, “if it’s safe.”

===Chinese===
「我算不上什么行家，」他向她说，「不过这几支瞧着颜色浅些。劳驾，来半打。我是说，」他意有所指地朝那口钟瞥了一眼，接着说，「只要万无一失的话。」

===Original===
The barmaid looked at him as if she thought he was crazy, but she did not speak and Tanner explained:

===Chinese===
女店员望着他，一脸当他发了疯的神气，却没有作声，于是坦纳解释道：

===Original===
“A friend of mine had an experience here the other night buying cigars, so he told me. He missed his train over the head of it. I was wondering if I should do the same.”

===Chinese===
「前几天晚上，我有位朋友在这儿买雪茄，赶上了一桩事，是他亲口告诉我的。就为这个，他把火车都误了。我正寻思，自己会不会也来这一出呢。」

===Original===
A light seemed to dawn on the girl. She laughed.

===Chinese===
姑娘似乎恍然明白过来，笑了。

===Original===
“I remember your friend. I couldn’t help smiling, but I was sorry for him too. He came in here and chose a dozen cigars, and then he looked up and saw the clock.

===Chinese===
「我记得您那位朋友。我当时忍不住直想笑，可也替他难过。他进来挑了一打雪茄，一抬头，瞧见了那口钟。

===Original===
“ ‘Your clock’s fast,’ he says.

===Chinese===
「『您的钟快了，』他说。

===Original===
“ ‘I *don’t* think,’ I says, and with that he hooked it out of the door, fair running, and all the cigars lying on the counter. I couldn’t but laugh at him.”

===Chinese===
「『我*才*不信呢，』我说。话音未落，他就一溜烟窜出了门，撒腿就跑，一打雪茄全撂在柜台上。我实在忍不住笑。」

===Original===
“But *he* didn’t laugh, for he missed his train,” prompted Tanner.

===Chinese===
「可*他*没笑成，车毕竟误了，」坦纳提了一句。

===Original===
“Oh, he missed his train right enough. He came back and showed me his watch﻿—three minutes slow. But he got his cigars all right.”

===Chinese===
「可不是，车是真误了。他还折回来给我看了他的表——慢了三分钟。不过雪茄到底还是到手了。」

===Original===
Tanner took Austin’s photograph from his pocket, and glancing at it casually, passed it to the girl.

===Chinese===
坦纳从口袋里取出奥斯汀的照片，装作漫不经心地扫了一眼，递给姑娘。

===Original===
“He’s a good old sport, he is,” he announced, “but to look at him there you wouldn’t think butter would melt in his mouth. What do you say?”

===Chinese===
「他是个顶有趣的老好人，真是，」他说道，「可瞧他照片上那副模样，倒像老实得连嘴里含块黄油都怕化了似的。你说呢？」

===Original===
The girl wrinkled her pretty eyebrows.

===Chinese===
姑娘那双秀气的眉毛蹙了起来。

===Original===
“But that isn’t the man,” she exclaimed.

===Chinese===
「可这不是那个人呀，」她叫道。

===Original===
Tanner took the card.

===Chinese===
坦纳收回相片。

===Original===
“I’m a blooming idiot,” he said. “I’ve shown you the wrong photo. This was the one I meant.” He handed over the print of Cosgrove.

===Chinese===
「我真是个十足的傻瓜，」他说。「拿错了照片给您看。我原是要给您看这张。」他把科斯格罗夫的照片递了过去。

===Original===
“Why, yes,” the girl answered unhesitatingly. “That’s him and no mistake.”

===Chinese===
「嗳，正是，」姑娘毫不迟疑地答道。「就是他，错不了。」

===Original===
“He’s a good soul enough,” went on Tanner, “but he was very sick about that train, I can tell you.”

===Chinese===
「他倒是个挺好的人，」坦纳接着说，「不过为那趟车，他懊恼得很，这我可以告诉您。」

===Original===
They conversed for a few moments more as the Inspector lit one of his purchases. Then with a courteous “Goodnight,” he left the bar.

===Chinese===
探长点燃刚买的一支雪茄，两人又闲谈了几句。随后他客客气气道了声「晚安」，离开了柜台。

===Original===
Whatever else might be true or false in Cosgrove’s statement, thought Tanner, it was at least bedrock that he had missed the 7:15 train as he had said. The thing now to be ascertained was whether he really had travelled by the 10:30.

===Chinese===
坦纳心想，科斯格罗夫的供述里其余种种无论真假，至少有一点是铁打的事实：他确实如自己所说，误了 7:15 那班车。如今要查明的，是他是否真坐了 10:30 那班。

===Original===
By dint of persistent inquiries the Inspector found a number of the men who had been on duty when that train left. But here he was not so successful. No one so far as he could learn had seen Cosgrove.

===Chinese===
探长死缠烂打地问了个遍，找到了那班车发车时当班的几名路员。可这回就不那么顺了。据他所知，谁都没见过科斯格罗夫。

===Original===
But this was not surprising. Tanner could not and did not expect confirmation from these men. They had had no dealings with Cosgrove which would have attracted their attention to him. The point could be better tested at Grantham, where whoever gave him his luggage should remember the circumstance.

===Chinese===
但这并不出人意料。坦纳本来也不能、也不曾指望从这些人嘴里得到佐证。他们同科斯格罗夫没有任何打过的交道，不足以让他们的目光在他身上停留。这一节点，到格兰瑟姆去验证更合适——在那里，无论经手把行李交给他的是谁，总该记得这回事。

===Original===
Inspector Tanner glanced at the clock. It was ten minutes to ten. Why, he thought, when he was so far, should he not carry the thing through right then? He looked up the time tables. A train left at 10:00 p.m. for Grantham, arriving at 12:28. The 10:30 p.m., following, reached the same station ten minutes later, proceeding at 12:43. If he went by the 10:00 he would have fifteen minutes at Grantham to make inquiries, and he could go on by the 10:30 to Montrose and interview Colonel Archdale. And if fifteen minutes proved insufficient for his Grantham business he could sleep there, and go on in the morning.

===Chinese===
坦纳探长看了看钟。十点差十分。他想，既然都到这一步了，何不趁热打铁，索性一竿子插到底？他翻出时刻表。10:00 有一班开往格兰瑟姆的车，12:28 到；随后的 10:30 那班十分钟后抵达同一站，12:43 继续。若坐 10:00 那班，他在格兰瑟姆有十五分钟可以查问，再接着坐 10:30 去蒙特罗斯，面见阿奇代尔上校。倘若十五分钟不够办完格兰瑟姆的事，他可以在那儿过夜，翌日清晨再走。

===Original===
Five minutes later he was in the train. Though, compared to that following, it was a slow train, it only made four stops﻿—at Hatfield, Hitchin, Huntingdon and Peterborough. A minute before time it drew up at Grantham.

===Chinese===
五分钟后，他已经坐进了车厢。同后头那班相比，这是一趟慢车，但沿途只停四站——哈特菲尔德（Hatfield）、希钦、亨廷登（Huntingdon）和彼得伯勒（Peterborough）。它提前一分钟进了格兰瑟姆站。

===Original===
Here Tanner had even less difficulty than at King’s Cross. An official at the stationmaster’s office remembered the episode of the telegram, and was able in a few seconds to find the porter to whom he had entrusted the matter. This man also clearly recollected the circumstances and unhesitatingly identified Cosgrove from his photograph.

===Chinese===
在这里，坦纳比在国王十字还省事。站长室的一名路员记得电报那桩事，顷刻间便找着了当时交他承办的搬运工。这人也清楚记得当时的情形，一看照片便毫不迟疑地认出了科斯格罗夫。

===Original===
“Just tell me what occurred when you met Mr. Ponson, will you?” asked Tanner.

===Chinese===
「就请你讲讲，你见到庞森先生时都发生了什么，好么？」坦纳问。

===Original===
“Well, sir,” the man answered, “I was going along the train with ’is bag and coat, and ’e comes out of a first-class carriage bare ’eaded, and when ’e sees the bag ’e says, ‘that’s my bag, porter,’ ’e says, and ’e gives ’is name. ‘Shove it in ’ere,’ ’e says. ’E ’ad ’is ’at on the seat for to keep ’is place, and that’s all I knows about it.”

===Chinese===
「是这么回事，长官，」那人答道，「我提着他的提包和外套顺着车厢找，他从一节头等车厢里光着脑袋出来，一瞧见提包就说：『这是我的包，搬运工，』他说，还报了名字。『塞这儿吧，』他说。他早把帽子搁在座位上占着位子了，我知道的就这些。」

===Original===
The confirmation seemed so complete that Tanner was tempted to return to town instead of taking the long journey to Montrose. But before everything he was thorough. He had paid too dearly in the past for taking obvious things for granted. In this case every point must be tested.

===Chinese===
佐证似乎已如此齐备，坦纳禁不住想干脆回伦敦，省得再跑蒙特罗斯那趟远路。但他做事头一条就是彻底。过去为「想当然」三个字，他吃的亏太大了。这桩案子里，每一个环节都必须查验。

===Original===
Soon, therefore, he was moving slowly out of Grantham on his way north. He had not been able to get a sleeping berth, but he made himself as comfortable as possible in the corner of a first-class compartment, and there he slept almost without moving till the bustle at Edinburgh aroused him. Here a restaurant car was attached, and shortly after Tanner moved in and breakfasted.

===Chinese===
于是不多时，他便乘着火车缓缓驶出格兰瑟姆，一路北上。卧铺是弄不到了，他便在头等车厢的角落里将就着安顿得尽量舒服，几乎一动不动地睡到爱丁堡站上的一片喧腾把他惊醒。餐车在这里挂上了，不久坦纳便过去用了早餐。

===Original===
At Montrose he went to a barber’s and was shaved, then, hiring a car, he was driven out to the training stables.

===Chinese===
到了蒙特罗斯，他先进理发店刮了脸，随后雇了辆汽车，一路驶到练马场去。

===Original===
Colonel Archdale was an elderly man of a school Tanner had imagined was extinct﻿—short, red-faced and peppery, and dressed in a check suit and riding breeches. The Inspector had called at the house, a low, straggling building of the bungalow type, but had been sent on to find its master at the stables, half a mile distant.

===Chinese===
阿奇代尔上校是个上了年纪的人，属于坦纳以为早已绝迹的那一类——个子矮小，红脸膛，脾气火暴，穿一身格纹西装和马裤。探长先到宅上去过，那是一座平房式的低矮散落的建筑，却被指点去半英里外的马房寻它的主人。

===Original===
“Mornin’,” the Colonel greeted him, as Tanner handed him his card and asked for a few moments conversation. “Certainly, I’ll go up to the house with you in a minute.”

===Chinese===
「早啊，」上校同他见礼，这时坦纳递上名片，求他赏几分钟谈一谈。「成，我这就跟您上宅子里去。」

===Original===
“I shouldn’t, sir, dream of troubling you so far,” Tanner assured him. “Besides, it is not necessary. A minute or two here when you are disengaged is all I want.”

===Chinese===
「那怎么敢当，长官，我做梦也不敢劳动您走那么远，」坦纳忙说。「再说也用不着。等您得空，在这儿谈上一两分钟，就尽够了。」

===Original===
“Be gad, sir, you’re modest. Comin’ all the way from London for a minute or two,” and calling out some directions to a groom, he led the way into a kind of small office at the end of the stable.

===Chinese===
「老天作证，长官，您可真谦虚。大老远从伦敦来，就为一两分钟，」他朝一个马夫喊了几句吩咐，便领路走进马房尽头一间类似小办公室的屋子。

===Original===
“Well, sir,” he said as he seated himself before a small roll top desk, and pointed to a chair, “and what can I do for you?”

===Chinese===
「好吧，长官，」他在一张小卷盖式书桌后落座，指了指一把椅子，说道，「我能为您效什么劳？」

===Original===
“I am engaged, sir,” Tanner answered, “in making some confidential inquiries into the movements of a man, who, I understand, was recently here﻿—Mr. Cosgrove Ponson of London.”

===Chinese===
「我眼下在办的事，长官，」坦纳答道，「是要对一个人的行踪作些机密查证。据我所知，此人不久前来过贵地——伦敦的科斯格罗夫·庞森先生。」

===Original===
“He was here”﻿—the Colonel hesitated a moment﻿—“this day week. And what the devil has he been doin’?”

===Chinese===
「他来过」——上校迟疑了片刻——「一星期前的今天。他到底闯了什么祸？」

===Original===
“Nothing, sir, so far as we know. It is the case of another man altogether, but it is necessary for us to know if Mr. Ponson really was out of London on that day.”

===Chinese===
「据我们所知，什么祸也没闯，长官。这桩案子彻头彻尾关乎另一个人，但我们有必要弄清楚，庞森先生那一天是不是真的不在伦敦。」

===Original===
“Well I’ve told you he was here. Is that evidence enough?”

===Chinese===
「我不是说了么，他来过这儿。这证据还不够？」

===Original===
“Quite, sir, as far as that goes. But I would like also to know some details to assure myself if his business here was genuine. What was his business, if I might ask?”

===Chinese===
「就这一点而言，尽够了，长官。不过我还想得知一些细节，好让自己确信他此行的正事是不是真的。容我问一句，他来是为何事？」

===Original===
“You may ask and I’ll tell you too, be gad. He wanted to see Sir Jocelyn, that’s a three-year-old I’m goin’ to sell. Devilish good bit of horseflesh too. But he wouldn’t stretch to my figure. I wanted seven hundred, and he would only go five fifty. So it was no deal.”

===Chinese===
「您只管问，我也只管说，老天作证。他想看乔斯林爵士（Sir Jocelyn），那是匹我打算出手的三岁马。那可是顶好的一匹马。可他舍不得出到我的价。我要七百，他只肯出五百五。于是没成交。」

===Original===
“He came about this time in the morning, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
「我想，他是早上这个钟点到的吧？」

===Original===
“Yes, and a confoundedly silly time it was to come. He was to have been here at six for the morning exercise, but he missed his train, so he said, in London.”

===Chinese===
「正是，这钟点来也真是蠢得可以。他本该六点钟到，赶清晨操练，可他在伦敦误了车——他自己是这么说的。」

===Original===
“I understood so from him. Just one question more, sir. When was the arrangement about his visit made?”

===Chinese===
「他也是这么同我说的。再问一个问题就好，长官。他这次来访是几时约定的？」

===Original===
“Some days before; I think it was on Monday evening I got his wire asking would Thursday suit me.”

===Chinese===
「提前好几天；我记得是星期一晚上收到他的电报，问星期四合不合适。」

===Original===
“This is the man you mean, I presume?” and Tanner took out Cosgrove’s photograph.

===Chinese===
「您说的就是这个人吧？」坦纳取出科斯格罗夫的照片。

===Original===
The colonel nodded as he answered: “That’s he.”

===Chinese===
上校点头答道：「就是他。」

===Original===
“And there was nothing, sir, in the whole episode that seemed to you suspicious or otherwise than it appeared on the surface?”

===Chinese===
「那么，长官，整个这段事里，就没有一点什么在您看来可疑、或是同表面情形两样的地方？」

===Original===
“Not a thing.”

===Chinese===
「一点没有。」

===Original===
Tanner rose.

===Chinese===
坦纳起身。

===Original===
“Allow me then, sir, to express my thanks for your courtesy. That is all I want to know.”

===Chinese===
「那么请容我，长官，为您的礼遇道谢。我要知道的就这些。」

===Original===
Declining an invitation to go up to the house for a drink﻿—“too devilish risky to keep it here, be gad”﻿—he returned to Montrose and looked up the trains to London. There was one at 2:29 which, travelling by Edinburgh and Carlisle, reached St. Pancras at 6:30 the following morning. This, he decided, would suit him admirably, and when it came in he got on board.

===Chinese===
谢绝了上宅喝一杯的邀请——「搁在这儿太悬，老天作证」——他便回蒙特罗斯去查往伦敦的火车。2:29 有一班，取道爱丁堡和卡莱尔（Carlisle），次日清晨 6:30 抵圣潘克拉斯。这一班，他认定再合适不过，车一进站，他便上了车。

===Original===
As he sat a little later gazing out on to the smiling Fifeshire country, he went over once more, point by point, that portion of Cosgrove’s alibi which he had already checked. So far as he had gone it certainly seemed to him very complete. In the first place, not only was the journey north made with, so far as he could ascertain, a quite genuine purpose, but the selection of that particular night was reasonably accounted for. The arrangement for it had been made at least as early as the previous Monday, which, again, would be a reasonable time in advance. Tanner could see nothing in any way suspicious or suggestive of a plant about the whole business.

===Chinese===
稍后，他坐在车中，望着窗外法夫郡（Fifeshire）一派明媚的田野，把科斯格罗夫那份不在场证明中业已查验过的部分，又逐条在心里过了一遍。就他已经查到的而言，它在他眼里确实相当完密。首先，这趟北上不但就他所能查明的而言目的十足真实，偏偏选定那一晚出行也有说得通的原由。这项安排至少在前一个星期一就已定下，而这也又是一个合乎情理的提前量。整桩事里，坦纳看不出半点可疑之处，或像是预设的局。

===Original===
Then, coming to details, the missing of the train at King’s Cross might of course have been faked, but there was no evidence to support such a supposition. On the contrary, everything he had learnt seemed to prove it genuine. But even if it had been a plant, it was demonstrated beyond a shadow of doubt that Cosgrove *had* missed the 7:15 p.m. as he said, and that, further, he *had* travelled to Montrose by the 10:30. Even as the case stood Tanner felt bound to accept the alibi, but if he could confirm Cosgrove’s statement of his visit to his rooms at 7:45 or 8 o’clock, and to the Follies about 10:00, any last shred of doubt that might remain must be dispelled. This, he decided, would be his next task.

===Chinese===
再往细处看。国王十字误车一节当然也可能是做出来的，却没有任何证据支持这种揣测。恰恰相反，他查知的一切似乎都证明它属实。而即便是设的局，有一点也已论证得无可置疑：科斯格罗夫*确实*如他所说误了 7:15 的车，而且进一步，他*确实*乘坐 10:30 那班去了蒙特罗斯。单就现状而论，坦纳已觉得不能不承认这份不在场证明；可倘若他还能证实科斯格罗夫所说的 7:45 或 8 点回到寓所、10:00 前后到「福利斯」，那么残留的最后一丝疑虑也必将烟消云散。这件事，他决定下来，就是他的下一步。

===Original===
The following morning, therefore, he returned to Knightsbridge. Here, keeping his eye on Cosgrove’s door, he strolled about for nearly an hour before he was rewarded by seeing it open and Cosgrove emerge and disappear towards Piccadilly. He allowed some ten minutes more to elapse, then he walked to the door and rang. It was opened by the same dark, clean-shaven butler who had admitted him before. The man recognised his visitor, evidently with suspicion.

===Chinese===
于是次日早晨，他回到了骑士桥。在那里，他盯住科斯格罗夫家的大门，佯作闲逛地转悠了将近一个钟头，才得了报偿：门开了，科斯格罗夫出来，往皮卡迪利方向去，身影消失了。他又等了约莫十分钟，才走到门前按铃。开门的仍是先前放他进来的那个皮肤黝黑、脸上刮得精光的管家。那人认出了来客，眼里分明带着疑忌。

===Original===
“Mr. Reginald Willoughby, the Albany?” he asked with sarcasm, and a thinly veiled insolence in his tone.

===Chinese===
「雷金纳德·威洛比先生，奥尔巴尼？」他讥诮地问道，话音里藏着一层薄薄的傲慢。

===Original===
“That’s all right,” Tanner answered easily. “I know my name is not Willoughby. It’s Tanner”﻿—he handed over his real card﻿—“and if you’ll invite me in for a moment or two I’ll show you my credentials so that you’ll have no more doubt.”

===Chinese===
「没什么了不得，」坦纳从容答道。「我知道自己不叫威洛比。我叫坦纳」——他递上自己的真名片——「您若肯请我进去一两分钟，我就出示证件给您看，保管您再无怀疑。」

===Original===
The butler was evidently impressed, and proffering the suggested invitation, led the way to a small sitting room.

===Chinese===
那管家显然被镇住了，依言发出邀请，领路到一间小起居室去。

===Original===
“Mr. Ponson he phoned the Albany,” he explained, “and they said there weren’t no one of that name there, so we was wondering about your little game.”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生给奥尔巴尼挂了电话，」他解释道，「那边说没这么个人，所以我们正捉摸您这是玩的什么把戏呢。」

===Original===
Tanner, following his usual custom, rapidly sized up his man, and decided how he should deal with him. With the veneer of his calling removed the Inspector imagined he might prove a braggart, a bully, and a coward. He therefore took a strong line.

===Chinese===
坦纳循着他一贯的路数，飞快把这人掂量了一遍，定下了对付他的法子。剥去他那行当的浮面功夫，探长料想此人多半是个吹牛的、凌弱的，外加胆小鬼。于是他采取了强硬路线。

===Original===
“I suppose you know,” he began, without heeding the other’s remark, “that Mr. Cosgrove Ponson is under serious suspicion of the murder of his uncle, Sir William, at Luce Manor?”

===Chinese===
「我想你是知道的，」他不理会对方的话头，开口便道，「科斯格罗夫·庞森先生涉嫌在卢斯庄园谋杀他的叔父威廉爵士，嫌疑重大。」

===Original===
It was evident this was the last thing the butler had expected to hear. He stared at the Inspector in amazement.

===Chinese===
管家显然万万没料到会听见这话。他愕然瞪着探长。

===Original===
“Lord lumme!” he stammered, “is that a fact?”

===Chinese===
「我的老天爷！」他结结巴巴地说，「这是真的？」

===Original===
“That’s a fact,” Tanner went on sharply, “and I want some information from you. And let me advise you to give it to me correctly, for if you don’t you may find yourself in the Old Bailey charged as an accessory after the fact.”

===Chinese===
「是事实，」坦纳厉声接道，「我要从你这里得到一些情况。我也劝你一句，老老实实给我说实话，不然的话，你弄不好会站到老贝利（Old Bailey）的被告席上，以事后从犯的罪名受审。」

===Original===
The man blenched, and Tanner felt that the estimate he had made of his character was correct.

===Chinese===
那人霎时面无人色，坦纳觉得，自己对这人脾性的估量没有错。

===Original===
“I don’t know nothing about it,” he growled sulkily.

===Chinese===
「这事我啥也不知道，」他绷着脸嘟囔。

===Original===
“Oh yes, you do. Mr. Ponson told me he spent that Wednesday night here, or a part of it anyway. Is that true?”

===Chinese===
「哦，不对，你知道。庞森先生告诉我，那个星期三的晚上他是在这儿过的，至少有一部分是在这儿。是不是这么回事？」

===Original===
Tanner had set his little trap to learn whether the butler had been primed with a story by Cosgrove. His victim did not answer for a time. Clearly a struggle was going on in his mind. Then at last he said, “Has Mr. Cosgrove been arrested?”

===Chinese===
坦纳设下这个小圈套，为的是探明管家是否被科斯格罗夫预先灌过口供。他的猎物半晌没有作声。他心里分明正打着一场官司。末了他终于开口：「科斯格罗夫先生被捕了么？」

===Original===
The question still further bore out the estimate Tanner had made of the man’s character. The Inspector could follow the thought which had prompted it. If the butler was to continue uninterruptedly in his master’s service, he would rather not have the latter know he had given him away, but if Cosgrove was already in custody he would keep on the safe side and tell the truth. Tanner did not assist him to a conclusion.

===Chinese===
这一问愈发坐实了坦纳对此人脾性的估量。探长摸得清引出这一问的心思。倘若管家还想在东家手下安稳当差，他宁可不让东家知道他出卖过他；可要是科斯格罗夫已经收押，那还是站到稳妥一边、说实话的好。坦纳不帮他拿定主意。

===Original===
“Never you mind that. You concentrate on avoiding arrest yourself. Now, will you answer my question?”

===Chinese===
「那不劳你操心。你只管操心怎么保住自己不进局子。怎么样，肯回答我的问题了么？」

===Original===
After some further urging the statement came. Cosgrove had not spent the evening in his rooms. He had left about 6:45 to catch the 7:15 at King’s Cross, but he had returned unexpectedly in about an hour. He told the butler he had missed his train, and was travelling by a later one. He had gone out again, almost at once, and the butler had not seen him for two days.

===Chinese===
又催逼了几句，供词总算出来了。科斯格罗夫那晚并没有在寓所里过。他 6:45 左右出门，去赶国王十字 7:15 的车，可约莫一个钟头后突然回来了。他告诉管家自己误了车，要坐后一班走。他几乎立刻又出了门，此后两天管家没再见过他。

===Original===
Tanner asked several searching questions, and ended up completely satisfied that the man was telling the truth. There was no doubt whatever that Cosgrove’s story was true in this particular also.

===Chinese===
坦纳又盘问了好几个刁钻的问题，末了完全满意，确信这人说的是实话。毫无疑问，科斯格罗夫的说法在这一节上也是真的。

===Original===
There now remained to be checked only the matter of his visit to the Follies, and though Tanner was not certain of the necessity for this, his habit of thoroughness again asserted itself, and he drove to the theatre. There he learnt that there was no rehearsal that forenoon, and he went straight on to Chelsea. His ring at the actress’s flat was answered by a smartly dressed maid, to whom he handed his card, asking for an interview with her mistress.

===Chinese===
如今只剩下去「福利斯」那一趟还没有查验。坦纳虽说拿不准这一步是否必要，但他那事事求彻的习惯又占了上风，便驱车去了剧院。到那儿得知那天上午没有排练，他便径直赶往切尔西。他在女演员寓所门上按铃，应门的是个衣着入时的女仆，他递上名片，求见她的女主人。

===Original===
The girl disappeared and in a few moments returned.

===Chinese===
女仆进去，不多时便回来了。

===Original===
“Miss Belcher will see you now, sir.”

===Chinese===
「贝尔彻小姐这就见您，先生。」

===Original===
He was ushered into a small drawing-room, charmingly furnished in pale blue, with white enamelled woodwork. The chairs were deep and luxurious though elegant, the walls panelled with silk and bearing a few good monochrome drawings, while on the dark polished floor were thick and, as the Inspector knew, costly rugs. But though everything in the room was dainty, its outstanding feature was its roses. Roses were everywhere, massed in great silver bowls and rare old cut-glass vases.

===Chinese===
他被引到一间小客厅里，以淡蓝色装点得雅致宜人，木构件漆着白珐琅。座椅宽深而华贵，却不失秀雅；四壁镶着丝绸壁板，挂着几幅上佳的单色画；深色打蜡的地板上铺着厚实——据探长所知，价钱也厚实——的地毯。不过，这屋里虽然样样精巧，最夺目的还是那些玫瑰。玫瑰处处皆是，大朵大朵簇拥在银盆和罕见的古雕花玻璃瓶里。

===Original===
“It’s a rose case,” thought the Inspector whimsically, as he recalled that in two other sitting rooms he had had to visit﻿—those of Miss Lois Drew and Cosgrove Ponson﻿—he had found the same decoration, though in neither case with the same prodigal liberality as here.

===Chinese===
「好一桩『玫瑰案』，」探长异想天开地想道。他记起自己不得不访的另外两间起居室——洛伊丝·德鲁小姐的和科斯格罗夫·庞森的——也见到过同样的装点，只是哪一间都不及这里这样大手笔地铺张。

===Original===
He waited for over half an hour and then the door opened and Miss Belcher appeared.

===Chinese===
他等了半个多钟头，门才开，贝尔彻小姐出现了。

===Original===
Seeing her full face in the light from the window, he realised her beauty as he had not done in the restaurant. Though she was slightly﻿—Tanner thought comfortable looking, though jealous people might have used the word stout﻿—her features were so delicately moulded, her little, pouting mouth so daintily suggestive of dimples, her light blue eyes so large and appealing, her complexion so creamy, and above all and crowning all, her hair, so luxuriant and of so glorious a shade of red gold, that he began to understand the position she held in the popular favour. She was dressed in a garment which Tanner imagined was a negligee, a flowing robe of light-blue silk trimmed with the finest lace, beneath which peeped out the tiny toe of a gilt slipper.

===Chinese===
在窗前天光里正面见到她，他才认识到她的美，在饭馆里可不曾这样。她微微发福——坦纳心里说丰腴，刻薄人怕要用「富态」乃至「粗壮」这等字眼——可她五官生得那样纤秀，那小巧微嘟的嘴那样娇俏、引人想着酒涡，淡蓝色的眼睛又大又动人，肤色如乳脂，而压倒一切、冠冕一切的，是她那头浓密的头发，红金色泽何其绚烂——他这才开始明白，她在众人的宠爱里占着怎样的位置。她身上那件衣裳，坦纳猜想是件家常晨衣：一袭浅蓝色丝绸的宽袍，缀着最细的蕾丝，袍下露出金色便鞋的小小鞋尖。

===Original===
Tanner bowed low.

===Chinese===
坦纳深深一躬。

===Original===
“I beg you to pardon this intrusion, madame,” he said, “but my business is both serious and urgent.”

===Chinese===
「恕我不揣冒昧，登门搅扰，夫人，」他说，「只因我的事既重大又紧急。」

===Original===
Without speaking, the actress sank gracefully into a luxurious armchair, indicating with a careless wave of her arm a seat for the Inspector in front of her. He obeyed her gesture and continued:

===Chinese===
女演员一言不发，仪态万方地落进一张华贵的扶手椅，随手一摆手臂，示意探长坐到她面前去。他依了她的手势，继续说道：

===Original===
“I have been ordered, madame, to make an investigation into the death of the late Sir William Ponson of Luce Manor, not far from Luton. I understand that you are acquainted with his nephew, Mr. Cosgrove Ponson?” His hostess nodded, still without speaking. Tanner thought her manner unnecessarily ungracious, and determined to give a hint of the iron which lurked beneath his velvet exterior.

===Chinese===
「我奉命，夫人，调查已故的卢斯庄园威廉·庞森爵士——那地方离卢顿（Luton）不远——的死亡一案。我了解到，您与他的侄子科斯格罗夫·庞森先生相识？」女主人仍旧一言不发，只点了点头。坦纳觉得她这态度未免无礼得不必要，便决意让她略微领教一下他天鹅绒外表之下包藏的铁。

===Original===
“I deeply regret to have to inform you that there is reason to believe Sir William was murdered, and that grave suspicion rests on Mr. Cosgrove.”

===Chinese===
「我深感遗憾，不得不奉告您：有理由相信威廉爵士系被人谋害，而重大的嫌疑正落在科斯格罗夫先生身上。」

===Original===
This time the mask of indifference was pierced.

===Chinese===
这一回，冷漠的面具被戳穿了。

===Original===
“But how perfectly outrageous,” the lady cried, a flicker of anger passing over her expressive face, “and stupid and cruel as well. How dare you come here and tell me such a thing?”

===Chinese===
「这简直是蛮不讲理，」女士叫了起来，一张富于表情的脸上掠过一丝怒火，「又愚蠢又残忍。您怎么敢上我这里来，对我说这种话？」

===Original===
“Because I think you may help me to clear him. Please consider the facts. The medical evidence shows Sir William was murdered some time after 8:30 on the evening of Wednesday week. We know that Mr. Cosgrove Ponson was financially in low water﻿—in fact, was in debt for a very large sum, and under threat of exposure and ruin unless he paid up. We know also he benefited to a considerable extent under Sir William’s will. Further, in the boathouse from which Sir William’s body was set adrift, a cigarette end was found﻿—one of a peculiar brand, but little smoked in England, but which Mr. Ponson continually uses, and lastly, and this is what brings me to you today, Mr. Ponson has been unable to account satisfactorily for his time on the evening in question. He says he was with you from 8:30 till 9:00, and what I want to ask you is, Can we get proof of that? I think you will appreciate that proof of that means proof of his innocence.”

===Chinese===
「因为我想到，您也许能帮我为他洗刷。请把这些事实掂量掂量。医学证据表明，威廉爵士是在上星期三晚 8:30 之后的某个时刻遇害的。我们知道科斯格罗夫·庞森先生手头拮据——事实上欠着一笔巨款，除非还清，便随时有被揭发、被毁掉之虞。我们还知道，他在威廉爵士的遗嘱下受益可观。再者，在那间曾把威廉爵士尸体连船放下水的船屋里，寻到一枚烟头——牌子特殊，在英国少有人抽，庞森先生却素来常抽。最后——这也正是我今天登门的缘由——庞森先生对案发当晚自己的时间始终无法交代得令人满意。他说他从 8:30 到 9:00 同您在一起，我想请问您的是：这一点，我们能找到证明么？我想您理会得到，证明了这一点，便是证明了他的清白。」

===Original===
Tanner had been unobtrusively watching his companion while he spoke, and her demeanour interested him keenly. While he was recounting the medical evidence and Cosgrove’s financial position she had listened perfunctorily, as if bored by such trifles being brought to her notice. But when he mentioned the cigarette she started and a look first of fear and then of anger showed momentarily in her eyes. It seemed to Tanner she might have so acted if she knew Cosgrove was guilty﻿—as if she was aware of and prepared for all he had to say except this about the cigarette, and that her anger was against Cosgrove for having smoked under such circumstances. She did not speak for some moments, and Tanner felt instinctively she had seen his little trap, and was considering a way out. At last she appeared to come to a conclusion, and replied in a quiet voice:

===Chinese===
坦纳说话时一直在不动声色地观察着他的对座，她的神态令他大感兴趣。在他叙述医学证据和科斯格罗夫的财务状况时，她听得意兴阑珊，仿佛这些鸡毛蒜皮也配拿到她跟前来说，叫人心烦。可一听到烟头二字，她猛然一震，眼里先是掠过惧色，随即是怒意。在坦纳看来，倘若她明知科斯格罗夫有罪，她方才会如此表现——仿佛他可能说的一切她都早有预料、早有准备，独独除了香烟这一桩；而她的怒气，是对着科斯格罗夫发的，怪他在那样的情形下还要抽烟。她半晌没有言语，坦纳凭直觉感到，她已识破他这个小圈套，正在寻思脱身之计。末了她似乎拿定了主意，用平静的声音答道：

===Original===
“What Mr. Ponson has told you is quite true, or at least almost. He was at my room at the Follies for about half an hour that evening, but not quite at the hour you have mentioned. He came about half-past nine, and left at ten. I know the time because it is the only period in that play during which I am off the stage.”

===Chinese===
「庞森先生对您说的完全属实，至少差不多属实。那天晚上他确实到我在『福利斯』的房间里来了约莫半个钟头，只是不在您所说的那个钟点。他是九点半来的，十点走的。我所以记得时间，是因为那是全剧中我唯一不下台的空当。」

===Original===
She had avoided his trap anyway, and her answer confirmed Cosgrove’s story. But Tanner recognised he was dealing with a very clever woman, and he was by no means so convinced of the truth of her statement as he was of that of the butler. He went on:

===Chinese===
她总算绕开了他的圈套，而她的回答也印证了科斯格罗夫的说法。但坦纳清楚自己面对的是个极聪明的女人，对她的陈述，他远不如对管家的话那样信得过。他接着说：

===Original===
“Obviously, madame, if we have to go before a jury the more corroborative evidence we can get the better. Now, are there any other persons who might have seen Mr. Ponson at the theatre, and who could be called to add their testimonies?”

===Chinese===
「不消说，夫人，倘若要上陪审团，能弄到的佐证越多越好。那么，可还有旁的什么人可能在那家剧院里见过庞森先生，可以传他们来补足证词的？」

===Original===
“I don’t know if anyone else actually saw Mr. Ponson,” she answered, “but I should think it likely. Probably the doorkeeper did, or one of the other men. Have you made inquiries?”

===Chinese===
「还有谁确实见过庞森先生，我说不上，」她答道，「不过我想多半有。看门的大概见过，要么就是别的哪个当差。您问过了么？」

===Original===
“No, madame. Not yet.”

===Chinese===
「还没有，夫人。」

===Original===
“Well, you had better do so,” and she got up to indicate that the interview was at an end.

===Chinese===
「那么您最好去问问，」说着她站起身来，示意会谈到此为止。

===Original===
Tanner found himself in the street with a baffled feeling of having handled the interview badly. But it was at least obvious that the lady’s advice was good, and somewhat ruefully he drove back to the Follies.

===Chinese===
坦纳发觉自己已站在街上，心里懊恼，只觉这场会晤办砸了。但至少有一点明明白白：这位女士的忠告是好的。他悻悻然驱车回了「福利斯」。

===Original===
Here he made exhaustive inquiries, but without any very satisfactory result. The stage doorkeeper knew Cosgrove, and said he was a frequent visitor to Miss Belcher. He remembered he had come two or three evenings in the week in question at about 9:30, and stayed with the actress for about half an hour. But he could not be sure whether or not Wednesday was one of these evenings. Three or four other attendants had also seen him, but in no case had there been anything to attract their attention to him, and none of them could say on what nights he had been there. But Tanner had to admit to himself that he could hardly expect such information from persons who were not interested in Cosgrove’s visit.

===Chinese===
他在那里又穷加查问，结果却不很理想。后台看门的认得科斯格罗夫，说他常来拜访贝尔彻小姐。他记得案发那个星期里，有两三个晚上他都在 9:30 前后来，同那位女演员待上约莫半个钟头。可星期三在不在这几个晚上之列，他拿不准。另有三四个当差也见过他，但无论哪一个，当时都没有什么教他们对他多看一眼，谁也说不上他来的是哪几晚。不过坦纳也只得自认，对那些压根不关心科斯格罗夫来访的人，本就难指望问出这样的消息。

===Original===
But on another point he got positive information. His inquiries established the fact that on the Wednesday night of the murder Miss Belcher had been on the stage at 9:15. She therefore could not have been masquerading as Mrs. Franklyn’s servant at the Old Ferry.

===Chinese===
但在另一点上，他得到了确凿的消息。查问坐实了：案发那个星期三晚上 9:15，贝尔彻小姐正在台上。因此，她不可能假扮弗兰克林太太的女仆出现在老渡口。

===Original===
On the whole the Inspector felt that, in spite of his momentary suspicion of Miss Belcher’s manner, he must fully accept the alibi. The evidence of Cosgrove’s missing the 7:15 p.m. train, and travelling by the 10:30 was overwhelming. The butler’s corroboration of his master’s return to Knightsbridge was convincing. Though Tanner was not so sure of Miss Belcher’s statement, it at least agreed with Cosgrove’s. Further, the lady had not fallen into Tanner’s little trap about the hour of the call and had disagreed with what he told her Cosgrove had said.

===Chinese===
大体而论，探长觉得，尽管自己对贝尔彻小姐的态度起过一阵疑心，这份不在场证明他仍必须全部接受。科斯格罗夫误 7:15 的车、改乘 10:30 一节，证据确凿无可辩驳。管家关于主人返回骑士桥的佐证令人信服。坦纳虽不那样笃定贝尔彻小姐的陈述，它至少同科斯格罗夫的说法吻合。再者，这位女士也未曾落入坦纳就来访钟点设下的小圈套，反而驳了坦纳转述的科斯格罗夫之言。

===Original===
Then another point struck him. Cosgrove was at Knightsbridge between 7:45 and 8:00, and at King’s Cross at 10:30. Was this evidence alone not sufficient? Would it have been possible for him to have visited Luce Manor in the interval? Suppose he had used a fast motor and gone by road?

===Chinese===
跟着又一点攫住了他。科斯格罗夫 7:45 至 8:00 之间在骑士桥，10:30 在国王十字。单凭这一条，难道还不够么？这段时间里，他有没有可能去一趟卢斯庄园？假如他备一辆快车，全程走公路呢？

===Original===
Tanner did not think it could have been done. From London to Halford was thirty-five miles, and there and back made seventy. What speed could he reckon on? Considering how much of London would have to be traversed, and the amount of traffic to be expected on so important a road, Tanner felt sure not more than an average of thirty miles an hour at the outside. This would take two hours and twenty minutes at least, leaving from ten to twenty minutes. The motor never would have risked going up to Luce Manor, as it would have been heard﻿—in fact, no motor did so. That meant that ten minutes must have been spent in going from the road to the boathouse, and another ten in returning. This even if it could be done at all, would leave no time in which to commit the murder, get out the boat and set the body and the oars adrift. Tanner considered it carefully, and at last came to the conclusion the thing would be utterly impossible. Indeed, he did not believe that an average of thirty miles an hour could be maintained. No, the alibi was complete. He felt he must unhesitatingly accept it.

===Chinese===
坦纳认为这办不到。从伦敦到哈尔福德三十五英里，来回便是七十。他又能指望多快的速度？想想得穿过多大一片伦敦城，再想想那样一条要道上该有多少车马，坦纳确信至多平均每小时三十英里，顶了天了。这一来至少耗时两小时二十分钟，只余下十分钟到二十分钟。汽车断不敢冒险开上卢斯庄园去，因为会被听见——事实上也没有汽车去过。这就是说，从公路走到船屋去得花十分钟，回来再花十分钟。这样，纵然来去都赶得及，行凶、解船、把尸体连同双桨放漂的时间便一分钟也不剩了。坦纳仔细掂量，末了断定此事绝无可能。真的，连每小时三十英里的平均速度能否保持，他都不信。不，这份不在场证明完密无缺。他觉得，自己必须毫不犹豫地接受它。

===Original===
Inspector Tanner was a depressed man as he walked slowly back to New Scotland Yard. Up to the present he saw that he had been on the wrong track﻿—that all his time and trouble had been lost. He was now as far off solving the mystery, as when he started the inquiry, indeed further, for the real scent must now be cooler.

===Chinese===
坦纳探长缓步走回新苏格兰场，垂头丧气。到这时他明白了，自己一直走错了路——所有的时间和心血都白费了。此刻他距离揭开谜底，同着手查案时一样远，实际还更远，因为真正的线索如今必定更冷了。

===Original===
And Sergeant Longwell had been almost equally unsuccessful in his endeavour to trace the man who had made the fifth line of footprints on the river bank. With occasional assistance from Tanner the sergeant had made exhaustive inquiries in all the surrounding country, but without result. The only thing he had learnt which might have had a bearing on the matter was that a small, elderly man with a white goatee beard had taken the 5:47 a.m. train from St. Albans to London, on the morning of the discovery of the crime. From Halford to St. Albans was about fifteen miles, and Longwell’s theory was that this man﻿—if he were the suspect﻿—had walked during the night to St. Albans, thinking that at a large station a considerable distance from Luce Manor he would be more likely to escape observation. But there was no real reason to connect this early traveller with the visitor to the boathouse. His boots had not been observed. But even if it had been proved that he was indeed the wanted man, the detectives were no further on. For the traveller had vanished into thin air at St. Albans, and no trace of him could be found either in London or anywhere else.

===Chinese===
而朗威尔警佐追查在河岸留下第五行脚印的那个人的努力，也几乎同样无功。偶得坦纳协助，这位警佐在四周乡野作了详尽查访，却毫无结果。他查知唯一可能与此事相干的，是有一个身材矮小、上了年纪、蓄一撮白山羊胡的男人，在罪行被发现的当天早晨，从圣奥尔本斯（St. Albans）搭 5:47 的早车进了伦敦。哈尔福德到圣奥尔本斯约十五英里，朗威尔的推测是：此人——倘若他真是嫌疑犯——是连夜步行到圣奥尔本斯的，想着在离卢斯庄园相当远的一座大站，更容易躲过耳目。但并没有什么真凭实据能把这位早行客同船屋的来客连到一处。他的靴子无人留意过。可纵然证明了他确是要找的人，侦探们也没有前进半步。因为这人在圣奥尔本斯凭空消失了，无论伦敦还是别处，都寻不到他半点踪迹。

===Original===
That day a note was received at the Yard from the Chief Constable at Halford, urging that, unless there was some strong reason for its further adjournment, the inquest should be completed. The delay, it was pointed out, was objectionable for several reasons, as well as being needlessly trying to the family. Rather bitterly Tanner wired his consent to the proposal, and later in the afternoon there was a message that the adjourned inquiry would take place at 12:00 noon next day, Saturday.

===Chinese===
那一天，苏格兰场收到哈尔福德总警长的一纸公函，敦促说，除非有重大理由再行展期，验尸审讯应即办结。函中指出，拖延有种种不利，也无谓地折磨着这一家人。坦纳颇为郁愤地拍电报表示同意，午后不久便有消息传来：延期的审讯定于次日、即星期六正午 12:00 举行。

## VIII · Tanner Finds Himself Duped / VIII · 坦纳发觉自己上了当

===Original===
Twelve o’clock next day saw almost the same company assembled at the adjourned inquest in the long narrow room at Luce Manor, as had sat there on the morning following the discovery of the tragedy. But on this occasion a few additional persons were present. Some members of the outside public had gained admission on one pretext or another, while, as Tanner noted, both Austin and Cosgrove Ponson were now legally represented.

===Chinese===
次日正午十二点，休会后复讯的验尸审讯在卢斯庄园那间狭长的房间里开庭，到场的几乎仍是惨案发现的次晨坐在那里的那一班人。只是这一回，在场者多出了几位：外头的一些公众借着这样那样的由头得以入场，而且，正如坦纳注意到的，奥斯汀和科斯格罗夫·庞森这回都有了律师代表。

===Original===
The proceedings were formal and uninteresting until the doctors were called, but the medical evidence produced a veritable sensation. In the face of it only one verdict was possible, and without leaving their seats the jury returned that of wilful murder against some person or persons unknown.

===Chinese===
审讯的程序照章行事、索然无味，直到医生们出庭；医方证词一出，顿时引起一场真正的轰动。面对这样的证词，可能的裁定只有一种，陪审团不离席退议，当场即裁定：蓄意谋杀，系一名或数名身份不明者所为。

===Original===
Both Austin and Cosgrove were evidently anxious and upset, and both showed relief when the proceedings were over. But, considering his interviews with them, and the inquiries he had made, Tanner did not think these emotions unnatural or suspicious.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀和科斯格罗夫两人显然都焦灼不安，审讯结束时又都露出了如释重负之色。不过，念及自己与二人的几次晤谈和所作的查访，坦纳并不觉得这些情绪有悖常情或形迹可疑。

===Original===
Though the Inspector had hardly hoped to learn additional facts at the inquest, he was yet disappointed to find that not one single item of information had come out of which he was not already aware. Nor had any promising line of inquiry been suggested.

===Chinese===
探长原本就没怎么指望从验尸审讯上问出新的事实，可是发现审讯中竟没有一件他事先不知道的情况，他终究还是感到失望。也没有人提出任何一条有希望的查探路子。

===Original===
He was now of the opinion that the real clue to the tragedy must lie in the letter Sir William had received a week before his death, but as he could see no way of learning its contents, his thoughts had passed on to the deceased’s visits to London. About these visits one or two points were rather intriguing.

===Chinese===
他此刻的看法是：这桩惨案真正的线索，必在威廉爵士死前一星期收到的那封信里；只是既然看不出用什么法子能探知信的内容，他的念头便转到了死者生前的几趟伦敦之行上。关于这几趟出行，有一两处地方颇为耐人寻味。

===Original===
Firstly, they had occurred almost immediately after the receipt of the letter, and it was at least possible that they were a result of it. Secondly, Sir William had travelled to town two days running, or at least two weekdays running. This was not in accordance with his habit and pointed to some special and unusual business. The third point Tanner thought most suggestive of all. Though it was Sir William’s custom and preference to go to town by car, and his motor was available on these two occasions, yet he had travelled in each case by train. Why? Surely, thought Tanner, to enable him to make his calls in private﻿—to avoid letting the chauffeur know where he went.

===Chinese===
第一，出行几乎紧接在收信之后，至少有可能正是那封信引出的结果。第二，威廉爵士接连两天进城——至少是连着两个工作日进城。这不合他的习惯，指向某件特殊而反常的事由。第三点，坦纳认为最能说明问题：威廉爵士进城，素来的习惯与偏好都是坐汽车，而且那两天他的汽车都用得着，可他两趟都坐了火车。为什么？坦纳心想，必然是为了能私下里办他的事——免得让司机知道他去了什么地方。

===Original===
At all events, whether or not these conclusions were sound, Tanner decided the most promising clue left him was the following up of Sir William’s movements in the city on these two days.

===Chinese===
无论如何，这些结论成立与否且撇开不谈，坦纳认定，他手里剩下最有希望的线索，就是顺着威廉爵士这两天在城里的行踪追查下去。

===Original===
Accordingly, when the business of the inquest was over and he was once more free, he returned to the railway station at Halford. Here he was able after careful inquiries to confirm the statement made by Innes, the valet, as to the trains Sir William had travelled by on the two days. He went himself to town by the 4:32, determined that on Monday morning he would try to pick up the trail at St. Pancras.

===Chinese===
于是，验尸审讯的事一了、他重新脱开身，便回到哈尔福德火车站。在那里，经过细致的查问，他证实了贴身男仆英尼斯关于威廉爵士那两天所乘车次的说法。他自己乘 4:32 的车进了城，拿定主意星期一早晨从圣潘克拉斯车站开始寻踪。

===Original===
But before Monday morning his thoughts were running in an entirely different channel.

===Chinese===
可还没到星期一早晨，他的思绪已经流进了一条截然不同的河道。

===Original===
He had gone home on Sunday determined to enjoy a holiday. But Fate ruled otherwise. The grilling afternoon had hardly drawn to a close when a note was sent him from the Yard. It read:

===Chinese===
星期日他回到家里，一心打算好好歇上一天。无奈天意另有安排。那个烤人的下午刚一挨到头，苏格兰场便给他送来一纸便笺。上面写道：

===Original===
“*Re* Ponson Case.﻿—Halford sergeant phones important information come to hand. You are wanted to return immediately.”

===Chinese===
「事由：庞森案。——哈尔福德警佐来电：得到重要情报，请即返回。」

===Original===
Tanner caught the 7:30 train, and before nine was seated in the Halford Police Station, hearing the news. The sergeant was bubbling over with importance and excitement, and told his story with an air of thrilled impressiveness which considerably irritated his hearer.

===Chinese===
坦纳赶上了 7:30 的火车，九点不到已在哈尔福德警察局里坐着听消息了。那警佐得意得、兴奋得几乎要冒泡，讲这桩事时一副又惊又重的做派，把听的人惹得不轻。

===Original===
“About four o’clock this afternoon a young woman came to the station and asked for me,” he began. “She was a good-looking girl of about five-and-twenty. She gave her name as Lucy Penrose, and said she was typist and bookkeeper in Smithson’s, the grocer’s in Abbey Street. I didn’t know her, and she explained that she lived three miles out in the country, and had only got this job since the beginning of the month. Then she said she had just read about the inquest in the evening paper, and that she knew something she thought she ought to tell.”

===Chinese===
「今天下午四点钟左右，有位年轻女子到局里来指名找我，」他开腔道。「她是个标致姑娘，二十五岁上下。她报的姓名是露西·彭罗斯（Lucy Penrose），说自己在修道院街（Abbey Street）史密森（Smithson’s）食品杂货店里当打字员兼记账员。我原先不认识她，她解释说，她住在乡下三英里外，这个月初才谋得这份差事。接着她说，她刚在晚报上读到验尸审讯的报道，有一桩事她觉得自己应当讲出来。」

===Original===
The sergeant paused, evidently delighted with the attention the London officer was giving him.

===Chinese===
警佐顿了顿，显然很受用这位伦敦警官聚精会神的倾听。

===Original===
“She said,” he went on after a moment, “that about half-past nine on the Wednesday evening of the murder, she and a young man called Herbert Potts were walking in the spinney belonging to Dr. Graham, on the left bank of the river, and just opposite the Luce Manor boathouse. They saw a boat coming down the river with a man in it. He stopped at the boathouse, and seemed to try the water gate, but apparently couldn’t get in, for after a moment he pulled on to the steps and went ashore, making the boat fast. In a couple of minutes he came back with another man and got in the boat again, and then went in through the water gate. The other man stood on the steps and watched him, and then he went round seemingly to the door of the boathouse. That was all they saw, but, sir, they knew the men.”

===Chinese===
「她说，」过了一会儿他又接着讲，「案发那个星期三晚上九点半前后，她和一个叫赫伯特·波茨（Herbert Potts）的年轻男子在格雷厄姆医生的林子里散步，那林子在河的左岸，正对着卢斯庄园的船屋。他们看见一条船载着一个男人顺流而下。他在船屋前停住，像是要开那道闸门，却显然进不去，因为过了一会儿他把船划到台阶跟前，上了岸，把船系牢。约莫两分钟后，他带着另一个男人回来，重新上了船，从闸门里划了进去。另外那个男人站在台阶上望着他，随后像是绕到船屋门前去了。他们看见的就这么多，可是，长官，那两个人他们认得。」

===Original===
Again the sergeant paused to heighten his effect.

===Chinese===
警佐又顿了一下，好把效果做足。

===Original===
“Get on, man. Don’t be so darned dramatic,” growled Tanner irritably. “Who were they?”

===Chinese===
「往下说，伙计。别这么故弄玄虚，」坦纳恼火地低吼道，「那两个人是谁？」

===Original===
“Mr. Austin Ponson and Sir William!” The sergeant reached his climax with an air of triumph.

===Chinese===
「奥斯汀·庞森先生和威廉爵士！」警佐抖出这最要紧的一句，一派得胜的架势。

===Original===
Tanner was genuinely surprised.

===Chinese===
坦纳着实吃了一惊。

===Original===
“Couldn’t have been,” he said after a moment. “I went into all that. Mr. Austin was halfway to the Abbey ruins at that time.”

===Chinese===
「不可能是他们，」他过了一会儿才说。「那一节我全都查过了。那个时候，奥斯汀先生正在去修道院废墟的半道上。」

===Original===
“She was quite certain, and she said the man Potts was certain too.”

===Chinese===
「她说她拿得准，还说那个波茨也拿得准。」

===Original===
“Have you seen him?”

===Chinese===
「你见过他没有？」

===Original===
“No, sir. He is a bookseller’s assistant in London﻿—to Evans & Hope, in Paternoster Row. His people live here, and he was down on a couple of days’ holidays.”

===Chinese===
「没有，长官。他在伦敦当书店店员——在帕特诺斯特街（Paternoster Row）的埃文斯与霍普书店（Evans & Hope）。他家里人住在这儿，他这回是回来歇几天假的。」

===Original===
Tanner noted the address.

===Chinese===
坦纳记下了地址。

===Original===
“How was Mr. Austin supposed to be dressed?”

===Chinese===
「据他们说，奥斯汀先生当时是怎么一副穿戴？」

===Original===
“In bluish grey clothes that looked like flannel, and a white straw hat.”

===Chinese===
「蓝灰色的衣裳，看着像法兰绒的，戴一顶白草帽。」

===Original===
“And Sir William?”

===Chinese===
「威廉爵士呢？」

===Original===
“In a black cape and felt hat.”

===Chinese===
「黑披风，毡帽。」

===Original===
“They didn’t see either of them leave the boathouse?”

===Chinese===
「他们没看见两人中有谁离开船屋么？」

===Original===
“No, sir. They were passing on down the river towards the girl’s home.”

===Chinese===
「没有，长官。他们当时正顺着河往姑娘家的方向走远。」

===Original===
Tanner was silent. If this news were true, though he could hardly credit it, the alibi must be a fake after all, and Austin must have duped him. And yet, how could it be a fake? He had tested it thoroughly, and he had been satisfied about it. He did not know what to think.

===Chinese===
坦纳默然。这消息若是真的——尽管他几乎无法相信——那份不在场证明终究必是伪造，奥斯汀必定把他骗了。可是，它又怎么可能是伪造的呢？他彻头彻尾检验过它，当时也是信得过的。他不知道该作何想了。

===Original===
“Why did this girl not come forward before?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「这位姑娘先前怎么不出头来说呢？」他问。

===Original===
“She didn’t know till she read the account of the inquest that there was any question of foul play.”

===Chinese===
「她直到读了验尸审讯的报道，才知道这案子竟还牵涉凶杀。」

===Original===
Inspector Tanner was considerably perplexed. The more he thought over what he had just heard, the more disposed to believe it he became, and at the same time more puzzled about the alibi. But one fact at all events appeared to stand out clearly. If Austin had really been to the boathouse that night, it surely followed that he must be guilty of the murder? His presence there would not of course prove it, but would not the alibi? If he had merely omitted to mention the visit it would have been suggestive, but if he had invented an elaborate story to prove he was not there, it undoubtedly pointed to something serious.

===Chinese===
坦纳探长大为困惑。他越琢磨方才听到的话，越倾向于相信它，同时也就越为那份不在场证明犯难。但无论如何，有一点看来是清清楚楚立着的：倘若奥斯汀那天夜里当真到过船屋，岂不必然得出他就是凶手？他人在那里固然还证明不了凶案，可那份不在场证明呢？倘若他只是漏提了这一趟，那不过是启人疑窦；可他若是编出一套煞费苦心的说辞来证明自己不在那里，那就无疑指向重得多的东西了。

===Original===
But, as had always happened up to the present, his own next step was clear. He must see the girl and hear her statement himself, and afterwards visit Potts, the bookseller’s assistant. If he was satisfied with their story he must once again tackle Austin’s alibi and not drop it till he either found the flaw or was so convinced of its soundness as to conclude the new witnesses were lying.

===Chinese===
不过，直到此刻事情向来如此：他自己下一步该怎么办是清楚的。他必须见到那位姑娘，亲耳听她的陈述，然后再去访那个当书店店员的波茨。倘若二人的说法他信得过，他就必须再度攻一攻奥斯汀的不在场证明，不查出破绽绝不撒手——除非它严丝合缝到令他只能断定：新出的这两个证人在撒谎。

===Original===
Next morning he was early at the grocery establishment of Mr. Thomas Smithson, in close conversation with a tall and rather pretty girl in a cream-coloured blouse and blue skirt. She repeated the sergeant’s statement almost word for word, and all Tanner’s efforts could neither shake her evidence nor add to it. She was quite sure the man in the boat was Austin; she had seen him scores of times; he was a well-known Halford figure. So was Sir William; she had seen him scores of times also. No, it was not too dark to see at that distance; her sight was excellent, and she was quite certain she had made no mistake.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，他便到了托马斯·史密森（Thomas Smithson）先生的食品杂货铺，同一位高挑俊俏、穿奶油色衬衫蓝裙子的姑娘密谈起来。她把警佐那番陈述几乎一字不差地复述了一遍；坦纳百般设法，既撼不动她的证词，也添不进半点东西。她拿得稳船里那个男人就是奥斯汀；她见过他几十回，他是哈尔福德一带的名人。威廉爵士也是；她同样见过他几十回。不，那么点距离并不黑得看不清；她目力极好，她敢肯定自己绝没有看错。

===Original===
She was very shamefaced about the cause of her presence on the river bank, and begged Tanner to respect her confidence. He promised readily, saying that unless absolutely unavoidable, her name would not be brought forward.

===Chinese===
至于自己那晚何以会在河岸上，她满脸羞惭，恳请坦纳为她保守这段隐情。他一口应承，说除非万不得已，绝不把她的名字端出去。

===Original===
He returned to town by the next train, and drove to Paternoster Row. Here he had no difficulty in finding Herbert Potts. He was a man on the right side of thirty, with a dependable face, and a quiet, rather forceful manner. He seemed considerably annoyed that his excursion with Miss Penrose should have become known, fearing, as he said, that the girl would get talked about, and perhaps have to give evidence in court. But about the events on the night in question he corroborated her entirely. He also was positive the man in the boat was Austin. Though now employed in London, he was a Halford man and knew Austin’s appearance beyond possibility of mistake. The Inspector left him, feeling that in the face of these two witnesses he could no longer doubt Austin had been at the boathouse, and therefore had faked his alibi.

===Chinese===
他乘下一班车回了城，驱车前往帕特诺斯特街。找到赫伯特·波茨没费一点事。此人不到三十，面相可靠，举止沉静而有魄力。他同彭罗斯小姐的那次出游竟致外泄，他显得相当恼火——照他的说法，是怕姑娘遭人议论，说不定还得上法庭作证。但关于案发当晚的情形，他的说法与姑娘的完全吻合。他也一口咬定船里那个男人就是奥斯汀。他如今虽在伦敦做事，却是哈尔福德本地人，奥斯汀的相貌他认得绝无差错的余地。探长离开他时心里明白：面对这两个证人，他再无法怀疑奥斯汀到过船屋，因而那份不在场证明是伪造的了。

===Original===
But how? That was the question he must now set himself to solve.

===Chinese===
可是，怎么伪造的呢？这便是他如今必须着手解答的问题。

===Original===
It seemed clear that Austin’s statement up to the time of his leaving the boat club pavilion, and after his arrival back there, was true. The testimony of the boatman Brocklehurst, Miss Drew, and Austin’s butler was overwhelming. The flaw therefore must lie in the evidence of what took place between those hours. Tanner went over this once again.

===Chinese===
看来有一点是清楚的：奥斯汀的陈述，从离开划船俱乐部会所之前、到回到会所之后的部分，都属实。船工布罗克赫斯特、德鲁小姐和奥斯汀家管家的证词，分量压得人无从抵赖。破绽因此必定出在中间那几个小时发生的事情上。坦纳把这一段又推敲了一遍。

===Original===
It hinged, as he had recognised before, on the shoes. And firstly, had the prints at the Abbey been made by those shoes? He had thought so at the time, and on reconsidering the matter he felt more certain than ever that he was right. A very trifling dint in the edge of one of the soles, evidently caused by striking a sharp-edged stone, was reproduced exactly in the clay. It was unthinkable that another pair of precisely similar shoes should have a precisely similar dint in the exact same place. No, when or by whom worn, Austin’s shoes had made the tracks. So much was beyond question.

===Chinese===
关键，正如他先前就认识的，系在那双鞋上。其一，修道院的脚印是那双鞋踩出来的么？他当时便这样判断；如今再把这件事掂量一遍，他比先前更确信自己不错。一只鞋底边缘上有一道极浅的凹痕，显见是磕在利石棱上磕出来的，而粘土里把这道凹痕原原本本印了出来。另一双分毫不差的鞋，恰好在同一位置带一道分毫不差的凹痕——这是不可想象的。不，不管何时穿、穿在谁脚上，踩出那些足迹的都是奥斯汀的鞋。这一层已无可置疑。

===Original===
Then with regard to the time at which the prints had been made. On this point the evidence of the butler corroborated Austin’s story. The butler had stated the shoes had been in Austin’s dressing-room in his, the butler’s, charge during the entire time from the Monday on which they were purchased till the Friday, with the single exception of this particular period on Wednesday evening. If this were true it followed that some person other than Austin wore the shoes, and made the tracks during this period. But was it true?

===Chinese===
再说脚印留下的时间。在这一点上，管家的证词与奥斯汀的说法相印证。管家陈述说：从买鞋的那个星期一起直到星期五，那双鞋一直放在奥斯汀的更衣室里由他保管，唯独星期三傍晚那段时间除外。倘若这是实情，那么便是奥斯汀以外的什么人穿了那双鞋、在那段时间里踩出了足迹。可这是实情么？

===Original===
Tanner recalled point by point his interview with the butler. Invariably he reached his conclusions quite as much from the manner and bearing of the persons he interrogated as from their statement. And in this case he was forced to admit the butler seemed to speak as a perfectly honest man. The Inspector felt he did not possess sufficient intelligence to make his story sound as convincing as it had, unless he himself believed it to be true.

===Chinese===
坦纳把同管家的那次谈话一点一点回想了一遍。他向来下结论，一半凭受问者的陈述，一半凭他们的神色举止，两下并重。而在这一桩上，他不得不承认，管家说起话来像个再诚实不过的人。探长觉得，此人的头脑不足以把他那套话说得那样令人信服，除非他自己便信它是真的。

===Original===
But might not the man have been mistaken?

===Chinese===
可是，这个人难道就不会弄错么？

===Original===
Obviously the liability of humanity to err must be kept in view. At the same time it was difficult to see how a mistake could have occurred. The matter was not one of opinion, but of fact. Was Austin wearing the shoes when he went out and returned on the Wednesday evening? Were they clean before he started and muddy after he reached home? There did not seem to be any possibility of error on these points. More important still, were they worn at any other time? The butler had stated he always knew what shoes Austin was wearing, as all his master’s footwear was in his charge. It seemed to Tanner that if Austin was away from the house for so long as a journey to the Abbey would involve, in dirty weather, the butler would expect a pair of shoes to have been soiled, and would therefore be bound to know if those in question had been worn.

===Chinese===
显然，凡人皆有过失，这一层必须记在心上。但同时，也很难看出错处能从哪里钻出来。这不是个见解问题，而是个事实问题。星期三傍晚奥斯汀出门和回来时，脚上穿的是不是这双鞋？他动身前鞋子是干净的，到家后是沾泥的，是不是如此？这些地方似乎没有出错的余地。更要紧的是，这双鞋在旁的时候穿过没有？管家陈述说，主人的鞋履全归他掌管，奥斯汀脚上穿什么鞋他无有不知。在坦纳看来，倘若奥斯汀离家的时间长得够得上一趟修道院之行——又赶上脏天气——管家就该料到有一双鞋要弄脏，因而那双鞋穿没穿过，他必定心里有数。

===Original===
But there was corroborative evidence which vastly strengthened the man’s statement, and that was the apparent age of the footmarks. Tanner could not tell to an hour when prints were made, but he felt certain he could say to within twelve. And in the case of these particular marks at the Abbey their appearance told him unmistakably they must have been made on or about Wednesday night. That the shoes came in wet and muddy that night, and that on Thursday morning they had dried by just the amount that might reasonably have been expected, was also strongly corroborative.

===Chinese===
不过另有一层佐证，大大加固了管家的陈述，那就是脚印看得出的大致新旧。坦纳固然说不出脚印是几点钟留下的、精确到钟头，但他自信能说准到前后不出十二小时。至于修道院里这几枚印子，它们的模样明白无误地告诉他：必是星期三夜里前后留下的。那双鞋当晚湿着、沾着泥进的屋，到星期四早晨，干的程度恰如合理预期——这也从旁大加佐证。

===Original===
The more Tanner pondered over the matter, the more he felt himself forced once more to the conclusion that the footprints at the Abbey were made on that Wednesday evening between the hours of nine and eleven. If Austin was now proved to have been at the boathouse between these hours, who then had made them?

===Chinese===
这件事坦纳越掂量，越觉得自己不得不再度归于那个结论：修道院的脚印，是星期三傍晚九点到十一点之间留下的。既然如今证明奥斯汀在这几个钟头里身在船屋，那么脚印是谁踩下的呢？

===Original===
And again, if so, what shoes had Austin worn at Luce Manor? On that night the butler had gone over all his footwear, and all except the shoes in question were there in Austin’s room.

===Chinese===
再者，倘若如此，奥斯汀在卢斯庄园穿的又是哪双鞋？案发当晚，管家把他所有的鞋履清点了一遍，除了那双鞋，其余全都好好地在奥斯汀房里。

===Original===
Tanner was genuinely puzzled. This whole matter of the shoes seemed so clear and straightforward, and yet, if Potts and Miss Penrose were to be believed, it was all a fake. As he sat smoking after lunch in the corner of a quiet restaurant he kept racking his brains to find the flaw. But he could get no light, and he did not see just where to look for it.

===Chinese===
坦纳当真被难住了。这双鞋的公案看似如此清楚、如此直截，可倘若波茨和彭罗斯小姐可信，它又整个是一场骗局。午饭后，他坐在一家清静饭馆的角落里抽烟，绞尽脑汁要寻出那破绽。却始终不得一线光明，也看不出该往哪里去寻。

===Original===
At last he decided he would try to trace Austin’s movements, from the time of his visit to Luce Manor on the Sunday evening previous to the murder, right up to the time he handed over the shoes to him, Tanner, on the following Friday. If Austin had arranged for a confederate to make the tracks for him he must have had communications with him, and it was possible Tanner might thus learn his identity.

===Chinese===
最后他拿定主意，把奥斯汀的行踪从头追查一遍：从案发前那个星期日傍晚他去卢斯庄园起，一直追到下个星期五他把那双鞋交给坦纳本人止。倘若奥斯汀安排了个同党替他踩脚印，两人之间必有接洽，坦纳说不定便能借此查明那同党的身份。

===Original===
As he was in London, the Inspector thought he might as well begin with Austin’s visit to town on the Monday previous to the murder. Of that, the only thing of which he knew was the purchase of the shoes. He had noted the maker’s markings, “Glimax B 10735 789647S Hunt & Co.”

===Chinese===
他人既然在伦敦，探长心想，不妨就从案发前那个星期一奥斯汀进城查起。关于那一趟，他知道的唯有买鞋一桩。他记下了厂家的标记：「Glimax B 10735 789647S Hunt & Co.」。

===Original===
Messrs. Hunt’s was a very large firm, with perhaps a score or more of shops in the metropolis, and probably hundreds throughout the three kingdoms. “Glimax” was one of the three or four “lines” advertised in every paper. Tanner borrowed a directory and looked up their head office. Half an hour later he was seated with their manager.

===Chinese===
亨特公司（Messrs. Hunt’s）是家极大的商号，在首都怕就有二十来家铺面，英伦三岛各地合计恐怕有数百家。「格利马克」（Glimax）是他们登在每份报纸上做广告的三四种「看家货色」之一。坦纳借来一册行名录，查到他们的总行。半小时后，他已同他们的经理对坐了。

===Original===
Having introduced himself as an Inspector from Scotland Yard, he went on to business at once.

===Chinese===
他自报家门，说是苏格兰场来的探长，随即言归正传。

===Original===
“I am endeavouring,” he said, “to trace the movements of a man who, on Monday, the 5th, this day fortnight, purchased a pair of shoes from one of your shops﻿—probably a West End branch. The shoes were marked Glimax B 10735 over 789647S. Now, can you oblige me by suggesting how I might obtain a record of the sale?”

===Chinese===
「我正在追查一个人的行踪，」他说。「此人在两星期前的今天——星期一，五号——在贵号的一家铺子买了一双鞋，多半是西区的一家分号。鞋上标着 Glimax B 10735／789647S。可否请您指点一条路子，这笔销售记录我如何才能查到？」

===Original===
“With the best will in the world, I don’t know that we can give you that information,” the manager returned slowly. “We get weekly statements from all our branches which show the total sales of each class of shoe during the period. But, unfortunately for you, though fortunately for us,” the manager smiled deprecatingly, “many shoes of the fitting in question would almost certainly be sold at each of our branches during each week. If, therefore, you were to go through our returns you would find yourself no further on﻿—it would still mean inquiries at each individual branch. How do you propose to identify your man?”

===Chinese===
「哪怕十二分愿意，我也拿不准能给您这个消息，」经理慢吞吞地答道。「各分号每星期都送报表来，列出该时段各类鞋的销售总数。可惜——于您不幸，于我们倒是万幸，」经理歉然一笑，「那个尺码的鞋，每个星期、每家分号几乎都保准卖出去若干双。所以您纵然翻遍我们的报表，也还是原地踏步——到头来仍旧得挨家分号去查问。您预备怎么认出您要找的人？」

===Original===
“I have his photograph.”

===Chinese===
「我有他的相片。」

===Original===
“I am afraid you will have to depend on that. Some of the salesmen will probably remember him. Can I help you in any other way?”

===Chinese===
「恐怕您只有指望它了。有些店员大概还记得他。还有别的能为您效劳的么？」

===Original===
“Two things, if you will be so good; to give me, first, a list of your West End branches and second, a note to your managers, asking them to assist me.”

===Chinese===
「劳驾两件事：第一，给我一份贵号西区各分号的名单；第二，给各位经理写张便条，请他们协助我。」

===Original===
“I will do both with pleasure.”

===Chinese===
「两件都乐意照办。」

===Original===
Ten minutes later Tanner reached the first branch. Here he saw the manager, presented his note, and explained his business. The official was extremely civil and brought the Inspector to each of the salesmen in turn. All gave him their careful attention, but none could recall Austin Ponson nor recollect the sale in question. With courteous thanks Tanner took his leave.

===Chinese===
十分钟后，坦纳到了第一家分号。他见了经理，递上便条，说明来意。这位职员客气极了，领着探长挨个儿去见每位店员。人人听得用心，却没有一个记得奥斯汀·庞森，也想不起那笔买卖。坦纳客客气气道了谢，告辞出来。

===Original===
The second branch was not far away, and here the Inspector made similar inquiries. But here again without result.

===Chinese===
第二家分号离得不远，探长照样查问了一遍，仍旧毫无结果。

===Original===
Recognising that his quest was going to be tedious, he engaged a taxi and settled down to work systematically through the list. Progress was slow, and it was approaching six o’clock by the time he had reached the ninth branch. But here, just as he had decided he would visit no more that evening, he had some luck.

===Chinese===
他明白这趟查访注定又闷又长，便雇了辆出租汽车，照着名单按部就班地干起来。进展缓慢，查到第九家分号时已将近六点。可就在这一家——他刚拿定主意当晚不再跑了——来了运气。

===Original===
In this shop, the second salesman he spoke to instantly recognised Austin’s photograph, and recollected the purchase of the shoes.

===Chinese===
在这家铺子里，他问话问到的第二个店员，一眼就认出了奥斯汀的相片，也想起了买鞋那一桩。

===Original===
“Yes,” he said, “I remember the man perfectly. What drew my special attention to him was the very peculiar way he conducted the purchase. He came in and said he wanted a pair of Glimax B 10735 over 789647S. He did not look at the shoes I brought him, except to check the number. I remarked that few gentlemen knew what they wanted so precisely as that, and he said he had had a pair of the same before which had suited him, and he simply wanted to replace them.”

===Chinese===
「记得，」他说，「这个人我记得清清楚楚。格外引我留意他的，是他买鞋那副少见的做派。他进来就说要一双 Glimax B 10735／789647S。我取来鞋，他看也不看，只核了核号码。我说，少有先生把自己要的东西知道得这么一清二楚的；他说他从前有过一双一样的，正合脚，这回不过是照样再置一双。」

===Original===
“About what time was that?”

===Chinese===
「那是几点钟前后的事？」

===Original===
“Shortly after four, I should say.”

===Chinese===
「我看是四点刚过不久。」

===Original===
“And did he give his name?”

===Chinese===
「他留姓名了没有？」

===Original===
“Yes. I forget what it was, but I sent the shoes to the parcels office at St. Pancras.”

===Chinese===
「留了。是什么我忘了，只记得我把鞋送到圣潘克拉斯车站的包裹处去了。」

===Original===
In reply to a further question the man said he recalled the names of Ponson and Halford.

===Chinese===
再往下问，那人说他记得「庞森」和「哈尔福德」两个名字。

===Original===
The Inspector was considerably puzzled by what he had heard, and that evening he lit a cigar and settled down to consider it. In the first place, Austin’s statement that he had bought the shoes on that Monday was true. But how did he know their number? The butler, Tanner remembered, had said that his master had never had a similar pair. For a long time he pondered over the problem, but the only thing that seemed to him clear was that some trick had been played. But at last a possible solution occurred to him. What if there were two of them in it﻿—Austin and an accomplice? The accomplice buys a pair of shoes and sends Austin the number so that he may get a precisely similar pair. Then on the Wednesday night while Austin, wearing one pair, is at Luce Manor, the confederate, wearing the other, is making the tracks at the Abbey.

===Chinese===
探长让听到的话弄得大惑不解。当晚他点起一支雪茄，坐下来细细掂量。首先，奥斯汀说那双鞋是那个星期一买的，这话不假。可他怎么知道鞋的号码？坦纳记得，管家说过，主人从来不曾有过一双相仿的鞋。这道题他琢磨了许久，唯一显得明白的是：这里头使了花招。可最后他到底想出了一种可能的解答。倘若这桩勾当里有两个人呢——奥斯汀，外加一个同伙？同伙先买下一双鞋，把号码告知奥斯汀，好让他也买到一双分毫不差的。到了星期三晚上，奥斯汀脚上穿一双、身在卢斯庄园，那同伙穿另一双，在修道院踩脚印。

===Original===
At first this seemed to Tanner to account for the facts, but then he recollected that the dent on the sole of one shoe proved that the pair which made the tracks at the Abbey was Austin’s pair﻿—the pair which had been in the butler’s charge till he, Tanner, received it. Unless, therefore, Austin and his accomplice had exchanged shoes at the end of the excursion, this theory would not work.

===Chinese===
起初坦纳觉得这套说法能把事实都兜拢，可跟着便想起：一只鞋底上的凹痕表明，在修道院踩出脚印的那双正是奥斯汀那双——由管家保管、直到他坦纳亲手接过的一双。因此，除非奥斯汀和同伙在那趟夜行临了把鞋换了回来，这套理论便行不通。

===Original===
Suddenly another idea came into the Inspector’s mind, at which he slapped his thigh, and smiled to himself. “Guess I’m on to it this time,” he muttered, as he went up to bed, well pleased with his day’s work.

===Chinese===
忽然又有一个念头闯进探长的脑子，他一拍大腿，暗自笑了。「这回八成是让我摸着门道了，」他上楼安歇时咕哝道，对这一天的成绩颇为得意。

===Original===
To test the soundness of his new supposition, he continued next morning the inquiry he had been making on the previous afternoon﻿—interrogating the shoe shop salesmen for information as to Austin’s purchases. He began with the tenth branch, as if he had discovered nothing at the ninth. But here his efforts met with no success. Nor did they at the eleventh, twelfth, and thirteenth. But at the fourteenth, with a feeling of pleased triumph, he discovered what he had hoped to find.

===Chinese===
为检验这个新设想立不立得住，第二天早晨他接着前一天下午的查访继续——向鞋店店员盘问奥斯汀买鞋的情形。他从第十家分号问起，装作在第九家一无所获。可这一家毫无收获。第十一、十二、十三家也没有。可到了第十四家，他怀着一阵得胜的欢喜，找到了他巴望找到的东西。

===Original===
At this shop he inquired, as before, if any of the assistants recollected a man like that of the photo he showed having purchased a pair of shoes of the given number. At once he had an affirmative response. One salesman remembered Austin having called on the Monday in question, and after having been carefully fitted, having bought the shoes. The salesman had according to his usual custom handed Austin a card bearing the number of the shoes. He had offered to send the parcel, but Austin had said he was running for a train and would take it with him. The transaction had occurred about three o’clock.

===Chinese===
在这家铺子里，他照旧问起：有没有哪位店员记得一个像他出示的相片里那样的人，买过一双那个号码的鞋。立刻便得了肯定的答复。有位店员记得奥斯汀在所说的那个星期一来过，经过仔细试穿合脚之后把鞋买了下来。那店员按老规矩递给奥斯汀一张卡片，上头写着鞋的号码。他提出代客寄送，奥斯汀却说赶火车，随身带走。这笔买卖是在三点钟上成交的。

===Original===
“Bully for me!” thought Tanner as he drove to St. Pancras, en route for Halford. “See what a little imagination does!”

===Chinese===
「嘿，我真有两下子！」坦纳驱车前往圣潘克拉斯、取道回哈尔福德时想道。「瞧瞧，一点点想象力能顶多大的用场！」

===Original===
The theory he had evolved on the previous night now seemed not unlikely to be the truth. According to it, Austin had gone to town on the Monday and purchased two identical pairs of shoes. The first he had had fitted in the usual way in one shop; the second had been selected in another shop as being of the same number as the first. This had been rendered possible by carrying out the purchases in two different branch shops of the same firm. One pair he had bought openly giving his name and having the parcel sent to St. Pancras; the other transaction he intended to remain a secret.

===Chinese===
他前一夜推演出的那套理论，如今看来未必不是实情。照这套理论，奥斯汀那个星期一进了城，买了两双一模一样的鞋。头一双，他在一家铺子里照常试穿合脚买下；第二双，是在另一家铺子里照头一双的号码挑的。所以能办得到，是因为两笔买卖做在同一家商号的两家不同分号。一双他敞开来买，报了姓名，还叫人把包裹送到圣潘克拉斯；另一笔，他打算让它始终是个秘密。

===Original===
Arrived at his home, Austin had carried out the same tactics. One pair he had spoken of and given into his butler’s charge; the other he had locked away privately. No one was supposed to know, and no one did know, that he had purchased more than one pair.

===Chinese===
回到家中，奥斯汀如法炮制。一双说了出去，交给管家保管；另一双私下锁了起来。他买的鞋不止一双——这件事本不该有人知道，也确实没有一个人知道。

===Original===
Let us call these two the known pair and the secret pair. On the evening of the murder, then, Austin puts on the known pair which the butler had in his charge, goes to Luce Manor, commits the murder, walks home through some muddy ground, gets the shoes wet, changes them on returning home, where they dry during the night and are cleaned by the butler next day, all exactly as the latter had stated. But at some other time, probably in the dead of Wednesday night, Austin gets up, puts on the other pair﻿—the secret pair﻿—and slipping out of his house unnoticed, makes the tracks at the Abbey. To make the deception more convincing he has previously dinted the sole of one of these “secret” shoes, so that this dint will show on the prints at the Abbey. At some convenient opportunity when the butler is out of the way he himself cleans the secret pair, and then *changes them for the others*. The dinted pair which made the tracks at the Abbey thus become those in the butler’s charge; the others, in which the murder was committed, are locked away by Austin, who doubtless takes an early opportunity of destroying them.

===Chinese===
这两双鞋，我们不妨分别叫作「明的一双」和「暗的一双」。于是，案发当晚，奥斯汀穿上管家保管的那双明鞋，去卢斯庄园，行了凶，蹚着泥泞的路走回家，鞋也湿了；到家便换下，鞋在夜里阴干，次日由管家擦净——桩桩件件，与管家陈述的毫厘不爽。而在另一个时候，多半在星期三后半夜万籁俱寂之际，奥斯汀起身穿上另一双——那双暗鞋——神不知鬼不觉地溜出家门，到修道院踩下足迹。为叫骗局更逼真，他事先在其中一只「暗」鞋的鞋底上磕出一道凹痕，好让这道凹痕显现在修道院的脚印里。等管家不在跟前的某个方便时机，他亲手把暗鞋擦净，然后*把它们同另外那双调了个包*。在修道院踩出足迹的那双带凹痕的鞋，就这样变成了管家手上的那双；而穿着行凶的那另一双，则被奥斯汀锁了起来，随后无疑一得着机会便早早销毁了。

===Original===
Tanner had to admit the ingenuity of the plan. To anyone not knowing there were two pairs of shoes in question, the alibi would be overwhelming.

===Chinese===
坦纳不得不承认这套计划设计得精巧。在任何一个不知道此案实有两双鞋的人看来，这份不在场证明都将是压倒性的。

===Original===
But completely to prove this theory it would be necessary to show that Austin was at the Abbey at some time other than that he had stated. It was with this object Tanner was returning to Halford.

===Chinese===
但要把这套理论证明到十足，还须表明奥斯汀在自陈的时刻以外的某个时候到过修道院。坦纳重返哈尔福德，为的正是这一桩。

===Original===
He made most persistent inquiries, but was unable to find any evidence on this point. None of the cottagers nor farm hands in the vicinity of the Abbey had seen Austin, either on the Wednesday evening or at any other time. Nor had any other stranger been observed. If, however, Austin had been to the Abbey in the middle of the night, as Tanner suspected, the failure to see him was not surprising, and did not invalidate the main conclusion. On the contrary, Tanner believed he had solved his problem. Austin, he felt, was guilty beyond a shadow of doubt.

===Chinese===
他查得再执着不过，却在这上头找不出一丝证据。修道院左近的村民、雇工，无论星期三傍晚还是旁的什么时候，都没人见过奥斯汀。连别的生人也没有人瞧见。不过，倘若奥斯汀真如坦纳疑心的那样是深更半夜去的修道院，没人瞧见便不足为怪，也推翻不了主要的结论。相反，坦纳相信自己已经解开了这道题。他认定，奥斯汀有罪，已无可置疑。

===Original===
And then Tanner saw that this solution cleared up another point by which he had been somewhat puzzled, namely, Austin’s readiness, indeed almost eagerness, to tell of his visit to the Abbey. That, he now saw, had been a trap, and he, Tanner, had walked right into it. He saw Austin’s motive now. From the latter’s point of view it was necessary that Tanner should inspect the footprints while they were still fresh. If some days passed before suspicion was aroused, the marks would have become obliterated, and the alibi worthless. Austin was a cleverer man than the Inspector had given him credit for. By his manner he had deliberately roused the latter’s suspicions so that his alibi might be established while the footprints were clear.

===Chinese===
接着坦纳又看出，这个解答还澄清了另一处曾叫他略感纳闷的地方，那就是：奥斯汀谈起修道院之行时何以那样爽快，简直是急不可待。如今他明白了，那是个圈套，而他坦纳正一头栽了进去。奥斯汀的用意此刻也看清了：在后者看来，必须让坦纳趁着脚印还新鲜时就去查验。倘若疑心过了好几天才起，印子早已磨灭，不在场证明也就一文不值。奥斯汀比探长所推许的更精明。他故意用自己的做派撩起后者的疑心，好让他的不在场证明趁着脚印清晰时确立下来。

===Original===
