That evening Tanner made careful notes of the evidence he had accumulated against Austin Ponson. When the document was completed, it read:

===Chinese===
当晚，坦纳把手里积攒的控告奥斯汀·庞森的证据细细录成文书。文书写成，全文如下：

===Original===
- 
					

Austin never got on with Sir William.

- 
					

Though Sir William allowed him £1,000 a year, this was a small sum compared to what he might equally easily have paid.

- 
					

Austin could not be making more than two or three hundred a year, so his total income could not much exceed £1,200.

- 
					

He was living up to, or almost up to, this figure.

- 
					

Austin had become engaged to a girl to whom, as a daughter-in-law, there was every reason to believe Sir William objected. This girl had no dot.

- 
					

Unless he got an increased allowance Austin would find himself very pinched after marriage.

- 
					

Sir William had threatened that if the marriage came off, he would not only not increase the allowance, but might alter his will adversely to Austin.

- 
					

Austin would therefore be faced with the alternative of having his prospects ruined if his father lived, or, if he died, of receiving £150,000. Thus not only his own position and comfort were at stake, but that also of the girl he loved﻿—a terrible temptation.

- 
					

Austin had an interview with Sir William on the Sunday night previous to the murder, at which the two quarrelled about a lady﻿—presumably Miss Drew.

- 
					

Austin had the requisite knowledge of Luce Manor and Sir William’s ways to have accomplished the deed.

- 
					

Austin had on that Wednesday night rowed down the river and met Sir William at the Luce Manor boathouse.

- 
					

Austin had denied having been in the neighbourhood at the time.

- 
					

Austin had invented and carried out an elaborate plant with the object of proving an alibi. This alibi was a deliberate falsehood from beginning to end, and was prearranged.

===Chinese===
- 奥斯汀同威廉爵士素来不睦。

- 威廉爵士虽每年给他 1,000 英镑，但比起他同样轻而易举便能拿出的数目，这点钱实在微薄。

- 奥斯汀自己一年至多挣得二三百镑，总收入难以超过 1,200 英镑。

- 而他的用度已贴着、或几乎贴着这个数目。

- 奥斯汀已同一位姑娘订婚；有充分理由相信，威廉爵士反对她进门做儿媳。这位姑娘没有嫁资。

- 除非增加津贴，婚后奥斯汀的日子将十分拮据。

- 威廉爵士曾放话：这桩婚事若成，他非但不加津贴，还可能改立遗嘱，于奥斯汀不利。

- 于是奥斯汀面临两途：父亲活着，前程尽毁；父亲死了，坐收 150,000 英镑。押上赌桌的不但他本人的地位与安逸，还有他所爱的姑娘的——可怕的诱惑。

- 案发前的星期日夜间，奥斯汀曾与威廉爵士会面，二人为一位女子起了争执——想必就是德鲁小姐。

- 对卢斯庄园和威廉爵士的起居习性，奥斯汀具备作案所需的了解。

- 案发那个星期三夜里，奥斯汀确曾摇船下河，在卢斯庄园的船屋同威廉爵士碰了面。

- 奥斯汀否认案发时曾在左近一带。

- 奥斯汀为证明不在场，设计并实施了一套周密的栽赃布局。这份不在场证明自始至终是有意的谎言，且出于预谋。

===Original===
As Tanner read over his document he felt that seldom had he investigated a clearer case, or got together more utterly damning evidence.

===Chinese===
坦纳把文书从头读了一遍，只觉得自己经手的案子，鲜有比这更清楚的，也鲜有凑齐过比这更铁的罪证。

===Original===
“The man’s as good as hanged,” he said grimly to himself.

===Chinese===
「这人等于已经绞定了，」他冷冷地自语。

===Original===
Next morning he laid his conclusions before his chief, with the result that an hour later he was again on his way to Halford, armed with a warrant for Austin Ponson’s arrest.

===Chinese===
次日早晨，他把结论呈到上司面前；结果是，一小时后他已再度奔赴哈尔福德，随身带着逮捕奥斯汀·庞森的拘捕令。

===Original===
He took the sergeant and a constable with him to the house, but left them waiting in the hall while he was shown into Austin’s study. The latter was writing at his table.

===Chinese===
他带了警佐和一名警员同去宅邸，却让两人候在门厅里，自己被引进奥斯汀的书房。后者正在桌前写字。

===Original===
“Hallo! Inspector,” he cried cheerily. “And how are you getting on?”

===Chinese===
「哟！探长，」他欢快地招呼道，「案子办得顺不顺？」

===Original===
Tanner ignored his outstretched hand, and as the other saw his visitor’s face, his expression changed.

===Chinese===
坦纳没有去接他伸出的手；待对方看清来客的脸色，神情登时变了。

===Original===
“Mr. Austin Ponson, I am sorry to inform you I hold a warrant for your arrest on a charge of murdering your father, Sir William Ponson. I must also warn you that anything you may say may be used against you.”

===Chinese===
「奥斯汀·庞森先生，我很遗憾地通知您：我手中持有拘捕令，以谋杀您的父亲威廉·庞森爵士之罪逮捕您。我还要警告您：您所说的一切，都可能被用来作为对您不利的证据。」

===Original===
Austin shrank back and collapsed into his chair as if he had been struck. His face grew ghastly, and little drops of moisture formed on his forehead. For some moments he sat motionless, then slowly he seemed somewhat to recover himself.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀向后一缩，瘫倒在椅子里，像挨了一记闷棍。他的脸变得死灰，额头上渗出细小的汗珠。有那么片刻他一动不动地坐着，随后才慢慢缓过一些来。

===Original===
“All I can say, Inspector,” he answered earnestly, “is that, before God, I am innocent. I am ready to go with you.”

===Chinese===
「探长，我所能说的只有一句，」他恳切地答道，「在上帝面前，我是清白的。我跟您走。」

===Original===
The news spread like wildfire, and that evening the people of Halford had a fresh thrill and a new subject of conversation.


===Chinese===
消息像野火般传开，当晚，哈尔福德的镇民又得了新的刺激、新的谈资。

## IX · Lois Drew Takes a Hand / IX · 洛伊丝·德鲁出手

===Original===
Just about the time that the arrest of Austin Ponson was taking place, Miss Lois Drew entered her drawing-room, and sitting down at the old Sheraton desk near the window, became immersed in her household accounts.

===Chinese===
就在逮捕奥斯汀·庞森的行动正在进行之际，洛伊丝·德鲁小姐走进自家的客厅，在窗边那张谢拉顿（Sheraton）式旧书桌前坐下，埋头理起了家用账目。

===Original===
The low ceilinged, green-tinted room was pleasantly cool on this hot, dusty morning. The bow window faced west, and so was shaded from the glare of the sun. The casements stood invitingly open to the warm, scent-laden air, which streamed gently in over the fragrant masses of colour in the flower beds without. The faint hum of honey-seeking insects fell soothingly and companionably on the ear. Now and then a sudden crescendo marked the swift passage of a bee, busily intent on its own affairs, while butterflies flitted aimlessly by with erratic, dancing movements. Beyond the garden a meadow stretched down to the river, in which cattle, immersed to the knees, stood motionless, enjoying the cool of the water. The surface of the reach was still and unruffled, and reflected as in a mirror the tree-covered slopes of the opposite bank. Restful and pleasant was the prospect without, and within, the whole atmosphere of the room breathed an equal contentment and peace.

===Chinese===
在这个炎热多尘的早晨，这间天花板低矮、泛着绿色的屋子却凉爽宜人。弓形窗朝西开着，正好避开烈日的炫光。窗扉敞向温暖而饱含花香的空气，那空气徐徐漫进来，拂过窗外花坛里一片芬芳绚烂的花丛。寻蜜的虫豸发出隐约的嗡嗡声，听在耳里既安神又作伴。忽而一阵骤然拔高的鸣响，标出一只蜜蜂的疾掠而过——它正专心忙着自己的营生；蝴蝶则舞步零乱，飘飘忽忽地掠过。花园之外，一片草场直铺到河边，牛群浸到膝盖，一动不动地立在水中消受清凉。这一湾河面波澜不兴，对岸覆满树木的坡地映在水中，恍如镜里。屋外景致安宁悦目，屋内的整个氛围，也透着同等的满足与静谧。

===Original===
And no less pleasant and restful to the eye was the figure of the girl at the desk. Though hardly anyone would have called her beautiful, she had what was of much greater importance﻿—that elusive indefinable quality called charm. There was charm in her graceful movements, in the steady glance of her dark, lustrous eyes, in the ready smile which lit up her expressive face, and in the low, mellow tones of her musical voice. She bent over her task, working rapidly, carrying out the business of the moment as she did everything she undertook, quietly, unobtrusively, and efficiently.

===Chinese===
伏在书桌前的这位姑娘，身影映入眼帘，也同样悦目、同样安恬。虽说几乎没有人会称她为美人，她却拥有一样要紧得多的东西——那种难以捉摸、无从界定的资质，人们唤作魅力。她的魅力，在举手投足的优雅里，在深色明眸沉静的一瞥里，在倏然点亮生动脸庞的盈盈笑意里，也在乐音般的嗓音那低沉圆润的声调里。她俯身做着手头的事，动作敏捷，料理眼下这桩事务也正如她经手的一切一样：安静，不事张扬，干净利落。

===Original===
She had lived what most girls of her age would have called a dull and pleasureless life. Her mother, who had been the brains and the manager of the household, had died when she was fifteen, leaving the care of her kindly, idealistic, impractical father and her pretty, inconsequent younger sister on her hands. Almost unconsciously she had shouldered the burden, but a very real burden she soon found it. Though they lived comfortably, even well, they were far from being well off; indeed, before long the chronic strain of making ends meet became to her a veritable nightmare. But she carried on bravely with a smiling face, entirely forgetful of her own pleasure. If only her father could be spared worry, and her sister have as good an education and as much amusement as could be extracted from their circumstances. But if the strain had made her self-reliant and dependable, it had also somewhat aged her and given her a seriousness beyond her years. She had, however, her reward in the way her father, now a rapidly ageing man, idolised and leant on her, and in the exuberant affection of her sister.

===Chinese===
她过的日子，多半要被同龄的姑娘们称作枯燥乏味、毫无乐趣。她的母亲——原是这个家的主心骨兼当家人——在她十五岁那年去世，把慈祥然而好空想、不切实际的父亲，连同漂亮而无心计的妹妹，都撂在了她手上。她几乎是浑然不觉地挑起了这副担子，可她很快便发现，这是一副实实在在的重担。他们日子过得算舒适，甚至说得上体面，却远谈不上宽裕；的确，没过多久，那种为收支相抵而长年煎熬的紧张，在她这里成了一场不折不扣的噩梦。但她始终面带微笑，勇敢地撑持下去，把自己的欢愉忘得干干净净。但求父亲能免于忧烦，妹妹能从他们的境况里得到所能挤出的最好的教育、最多的欢乐。只是这份重负固然把她磨炼得自立可靠，也在某种程度上催她早熟，给了她一种超出年纪的凝重。不过她自有报偿：眼看迅速见老的父亲把她奉若至宝、倚为支柱，妹妹又对她怀着满腔的热忱深情。

===Original===
She had on this bright summer morning just completed her accounts, having with characteristic thoroughness at last run to earth a fugitive fivepence which for a considerable time had avoided capture. She was putting away her books when the elderly general servant brought in a note. It was addressed in Austin Ponson’s square, masculine hand. With a sudden irrational feeling of foreboding she waited till the woman had left the room, then tore it open, and with a clutching at the heart read:

===Chinese===
在这个晴朗的夏日早晨，她刚把账目理清——凭着她一贯的彻底，那笔潜逃多时的五便士，终于被她追查归案。她正把账本收起来，年迈的女仆送进来一封便函。信封上的字，是奥斯汀·庞森那手方正遒劲的男子笔迹。她心头没来由地涌起一阵不祥的预感，等那女仆退出房去，才把信撕开，揪着心读道：

===Original===
21st July.

===Chinese===
「7月21日。

===Original===
“Dear Lois: I am writing this by the courtesy of Inspector Tanner of Scotland Yard, though he has warned me he will have to read it before sending it on. He tells me that some evidence has been received which throws suspicion of my father’s murder on me, and that he is bound to arrest me. I do not yet know the details. This is just to say, dearest, that of course I see that under the circumstances there can be no question of an engagement between us. I will live in hope that if this trouble passes over, you will perhaps allow me once again to plead my cause, but if this should be goodbye, I can only say I am innocent of this awful charge, and beg you to think as kindly of me as you can.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的洛伊丝：我写这封信，是承蒙苏格兰场坦纳探长的通融，尽管他警告过我，信在转寄之前他必须过目。他告诉我，他们收到了一些证据，把杀害我父亲的嫌疑落到了我头上，他不得不逮捕我。细节情形我还不知道。亲爱的，我只想说：我自然明白，事到如今，你我之间已谈不上什么婚约。我会抱着希望活下去——倘若这场灾祸终会过去，你或许肯容我再一次恳求；可倘若这就是诀别，那我只能说，这项可怕的罪名加在我身上，我是清白的，只求你尽量怀着善意记挂我。

===Original===
“Always yours devotedly,

===Chinese===
「你至死不渝的，

===Original===
“Austin Ponson.”

===Chinese===
「奥斯汀·庞森。」

===Original===
For several minutes Lois Drew sat motionless, her white face hard and set, her horror-stricken eyes fastened on the fateful words. So the blow had fallen at last﻿—the terrible blow that ever since the Inspector’s visit she had feared day and night. Since the tragedy it had been as if some Dread Shade lurked, for the moment indeed in the background, but ready at any minute to step forward and intrude its baleful presence into Austin’s life and hers. For never for a moment had it occurred to her to break off the engagement, and now, though Austin had written of its cancellation as an obvious and indeed an accomplished fact, she never really allowed her mind to harbour the idea. Had she not promised to marry him? Very well, that was enough. If he had wanted her when all things were well, would he not want her tenfold now when trouble had come? But though she did not seriously consider his proposal, subconsciously she was pleased that he had made it so promptly. And she did not resent the cold tone of his note. She realised his feelings were sacred to him. Neither he nor she could have them weighed and sifted by an Inspector of police.

===Chinese===
洛伊丝·德鲁一动不动地坐了好几分钟，苍白的脸绷得僵直，惊骇的双眼死死钉在那几行决定命运的字句上。打击终于落下来了——正是自打探长来访那天起，她日夜恐惧的那记可怕打击。惨案发生之后，仿佛始终有一道「骇人的暗影」潜藏在一旁，眼下固然还缩在幕后，却随时会跨步上前，把它不祥的存在硬挤进奥斯汀的生活和她的生活里来。因为她从未有过一念要解除婚约；而此刻，尽管奥斯汀在信里把退婚写成一件不言自明、甚至木已成舟的事，她也从不曾真正容许这个念头在心里落脚。她不是答应过要嫁给他吗？好，这就够了。万事顺遂时他要的是她，如今大祸临头，他难道不会十倍地要她吗？不过，虽说她并没有认真把他的提议当回事，潜意识里却为他提得这般爽快而欢喜。她也不怪他信里的冷淡口吻。她明白，他的感情于他是神圣的。无论他还是她，都不能容人把这份感情摆到一位警察探长的天平上去称量、去筛拣。

===Original===
As she sat, motionless in body and almost numb in mind, waiting unconsciously till the first rude shock should have somewhat passed over, she tried to consider the matter dispassionately. She knew that many persons arrested on suspicion were afterwards set at liberty, the charge against them breaking down on further investigation, and that of those actually brought to trial, a respectable percentage were acquitted. But she also knew that in a case like the present Scotland Yard would not order an arrest unless there were pretty strong grounds to go on. And this was particularly so where the accused had been under suspicion for some time, as she guessed from Tanner’s visit to herself Austin had been. But she was a good friend. In all her cogitations never once did the possibility of her lover’s guilt enter her thoughts. Of his innocence she was as certain as of her own existence.

===Chinese===
她这样坐着，身子纹丝不动，心神几乎麻木，不自觉地等着那当胸一击最初的震痛稍稍平复，一面设法不动感情地考量这件事。她知道，许多因涉嫌被捕的人后来都获了释，罪名经进一步侦查便站不住脚；而真正解上法庭的那些人里，洗脱罪名的也占了可观的比例。可她也知道，眼下这样的案子，苏格兰场若不是握着相当过硬的根据，是不会下令逮捕的。何况被告已然涉嫌了些时日——她从坦纳登门找她这一点猜得出，奥斯汀早被列入了嫌疑——那就更是如此了。但她是个忠诚的朋友。千思万想之中，恋人有罪这个可能，一次也不曾闯进过她的念头。他的清白，她确信得如同确信自己的存在。

===Original===
As time passed, her mind became clearer, and her practical common sense reasserted itself. What, she wondered, was she to do? She was so ignorant of the law, so unversed in its usages, so unaware of its possibilities. But she felt one thing demanded her immediate care. That impossible idea of breaking the engagement must be put out of Austin’s head without further delay. He must not be allowed to think for one moment that his trouble could make any difference to her. He must be made to feel she was not leaving him in the lurch.

===Chinese===
随着时间过去，她的头脑清楚了些，务实的常识又占了上风。她该怎么办呢？她暗自思量。她对法律何其无知，对法律的路数何其生疏，对法律能有怎样的作为何其没有概念。但她觉得有一桩事刻不容缓。退婚那个万万要不得的念头，必须毫不耽搁地从奥斯汀脑子里赶出去。绝不能容他有一瞬想到，他的祸事能让她有半分改易。必须让他知道，她没有在他危难时弃他而去。

===Original===
But how was she to communicate with him?

===Chinese===
可是，她该怎么把话传给他呢？

===Original===
She did not know whether he would be allowed to receive letters, but she fancied not. At all events there might be a delay in their delivery. She thought for a few moments, and then she took a sheet of paper and wrote as follows:

===Chinese===
她不知道会不会准他收信，想来多半不会。无论如何，信送到他手里也可能耽搁。她想了几分钟，随即取过一张信纸，写道：

===Original===
“Dear Mr. Tanner: I have received the letter from Mr. Ponson which you were good enough to permit him to write and I wish to convey to you my grateful thanks for your consideration. In case it should be contrary to the rules for him to receive my reply direct, I write to ask whether you could possibly see your way to convey to him this message﻿—first, that I will not hear of our engagement being broken off; on the contrary, I am going to announce it immediately, and second, that though I know it is unnecessary to assure him of my absolute belief in him and in his innocence, I still wish to do so in the strongest manner possible.

===Chinese===
「亲爱的坦纳先生：庞森先生蒙您好意准许写来的那封信，我已收到，谨向您这份体恤致以衷心的感谢。倘因规章所限，他不能直接收到我的复信，我写信是想请问：您可否设法向他转达下面的话——第一，解除婚约一事，我断乎不能听之任之；恰恰相反，我打算立即宣布订婚；第二，虽说我明白，我对他本人、对他的清白所怀的绝对信念，原不待我来担保，我仍然愿意以最恳切的方式向他言明。

===Original===
“I thought you were exceedingly kind on the day you called here, and I am sure that if you can do me this great favour you will.

===Chinese===
「那天您光临舍间时，我深感您极为仁厚；我确信，只要这个大忙您帮得上，您是一定肯帮的。

===Original===
“Yours sincerely,

===Chinese===
「您真诚的，

===Original===
“Lois Drew.”

===Chinese===
「洛伊丝·德鲁。」

===Original===
She addressed the envelope to “Inspector Tanner, Criminal Investigation Department, New Scotland Yard, London,” marking it “Personal.” Then putting on her hat, she walked quickly to the post office and dropped the letter into the receiver. She thought that many of the passersby looked at her curiously, but she was too much absorbed in her own thoughts to care.

===Chinese===
她在信封上写「伦敦，新苏格兰场，刑事调查处（Criminal Investigation Department），坦纳探长」，又注明「亲启」。随后她戴上帽子，快步走到邮局，把信投进了收信口。她觉得过路人中有不少好奇地打量她，可她一心沉浸在自己的思绪里，全不在意。

===Original===
As she turned homewards it suddenly occurred to her that her cousin Jimmy might help her. Mr. James Daunt was junior partner of a London firm of solicitors, a clever, and she believed, a rising man, and who would have all the knowledge of the possibilities of the case which she lacked. Moreover, Jimmy was a decent soul, and a good friend of her own. They had seen a lot of each other as children, and she felt he would help her if he could.

===Chinese===
往家走的路上，她忽然想起表哥吉米（Jimmy）也许能帮她。詹姆斯·多恩特（James Daunt）先生是伦敦一家律师事务所的初级合伙人，人聪明——她相信——还是个前程看涨的人；她所欠缺的、关于这类案子能有怎样的种种作为的学问，他一概具备。何况吉米是个正派厚道的人，又是她自己的好朋友。两人打小来往不断，她觉得只要办得到，他一定肯帮她。

===Original===
She turned into the local telephone office and put through a call. Mr. James Daunt was in his office. If she came to town he could see her at five o’clock.

===Chinese===
她转进本地电话局，接通了一个电话。詹姆斯·多恩特先生在事务所里。她若进城，他五点钟可以见她。

===Original===
She travelled up by the 3:30 train, and at the hour named mounted the steps of the old house in Lincoln’s Inn. She was soon in her cousin’s room.

===Chinese===
她乘 3:30 的火车进了城，到了约定的钟点，登上林肯律师学院那座老宅前的台阶。不多时，她已坐在表哥的房间里。

===Original===
“Hallo old girl!” he greeted her when the door had closed. “Jolly to see you. It’s not often you take pity on a lonely old bachelor like this. Sit down, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
「喂，老伙计！」门一关上，他便招呼道。「见到你真高兴。你可不常这样可怜可怜我这么个孤零零的老单身汉。快坐，好不好？」

===Original===
She sank back into a deep, leather-lined armchair. They talked commonplaces for a few moments, and then Lois referred to the object of her call. She found it much harder to begin than she had expected, but when her cousin understood she was really in trouble he dropped his somewhat breezy manner and became serious and sympathetic.

===Chinese===
她深深陷进那把衬皮面的扶手椅里。两人先闲谈了几句，随后洛伊丝便提到此来的正事。她发觉开口远比预想的艰难，可等表哥明白她当真遇上了难处，他便收起了那副随随便便的爽利做派，变得又郑重又体贴。

===Original===
“Have you had tea?” he asked, interrupting her story.

===Chinese===
「用过茶点了吗？」他打断她的话头问。

===Original===
“Not yet. I came direct from the station.”

===Chinese===
「还没有。我从车站直接过来的。」

===Original===
“Then not a word till you’ve had it,” he declared. “Come out to a quiet little place I know. We can talk there without interruption.”

===Chinese===
「那就一个字也不许再说，吃完了才许讲，」他宣布道。「跟我去一家我知道的清静小馆子。在那儿说话没人打搅。」

===Original===
Though she had not realised it, Lois was almost fainting for food. She had not eaten any lunch, and now the hot stimulant and the fresh rolls and butter did her more good than she could have thought possible. She smiled across at him.

===Chinese===
洛伊丝自己虽没察觉，其实已饿得几欲昏厥。她午饭粒米未进，此刻这杯热腾腾的提神饮品，加上新鲜的小圆面包和黄油，给她补的元气之多，连她自己都想不到。她隔着桌子朝他嫣然一笑。

===Original===
“I believe I was dying of hunger,” she announced.

===Chinese===
「我看我方才真是快饿死了，」她宣布。

===Original===
“Just like you,” he retorted, “at your old games again. Always thinking of somebody else, and forgetting your own much more important self.”

===Chinese===
「你就是这脾气，」他回敬道，「又犯老毛病了。老惦记着别人，倒把自己这个要紧得多的人忘个精光。」

===Original===
“Well, you’ve probably saved my life. And now, Jimmy, I do want your help.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，你这怕是救了我一条命。吉米，这回我可真要你帮忙了。」

===Original===
“I know, old girl. I don’t need to tell you I’ll do everything I can. Just start in and let’s have the whole story. You don’t mind if I smoke?”

===Chinese===
「我知道，老伙计。不用我说，能办的我都会办。这就从头讲起，把事情原原本本说给我听。我抽支烟，你不介意吧？」

===Original===
“Of course not. The first thing I have to tell you is﻿—that I have become engaged to be married.”

===Chinese===
「当然不介意。我要告诉你的头一件事就是——我订婚了。」

===Original===
“By Jove, you have!” cried Jimmy, jumping up and holding out his hand. “I hadn’t an inkling there was anything in the wind. Best possible congratulations, old girl. I think a cousinly salutation﻿—” they were alone in an alcove, and he kissed her lightly on the cheek. “And who’s the lucky man, if it’s not a secret?”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，真的！」吉米叫起来，跳起身伸出手去。「事前一点儿风声都没漏到我耳朵里。老伙计，最诚挚的祝贺。我想该行一个表兄妹的贺礼——」两人正独坐在一处凹室雅座里，他便在她脸颊上轻轻吻了一下。「那位幸运的男士是谁呀——若不是秘密的话？」

===Original===
“Ah, there’s no luck in it, Jimmy. That’s what I’ve come about. It’s Austin Ponson.”

===Chinese===
「唉，哪里有什么幸运，吉米。我正是为这个来的。是奥斯汀·庞森。」

===Original===
“Austin Ponson? How do you mean no luck? Why, dear girl, I congratulate you again. I have heard of him, and always that he was a white man through and through.”

===Chinese===
「奥斯汀·庞森？怎么说没有幸运？嗳，亲爱的姑娘，我更要再贺你一次了。这人我听说过，听到的无一例外都是：他是个彻头彻尾的正人君子。」

===Original===
Tears trembled on Lois’s long eyelashes. It took all her strength of will to speak in her normal tone.

===Chinese===
泪珠悬在洛伊丝长长的睫毛上打颤。她费尽全部的意志，才用平常的声调开了口。

===Original===
“Dear Jimmy, you can’t think what it means to me to hear you say that. You evidently don’t know what has happened. He’s in frightful trouble.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的吉米，你不知道，听你这么说对我意味着什么。你显然还不知道出了什么事。他遇上了可怕的祸事。」

===Original===
Jimmy looked his question.

===Chinese===
吉米用目光问出了他的疑问。

===Original===
“He’s just been arrested on the charge”﻿—Lois’s lip quivered in spite of herself﻿—“of murdering his father.”

===Chinese===
「他刚刚被捕了，罪名是」——洛伊丝的嘴唇不由自主地发颤——「谋杀他的父亲。」

===Original===
Her cousin whistled.

===Chinese===
表哥吹了声口哨。

===Original===
“Good Lord!” he cried, “you don’t say so? Poor old girl.”

===Chinese===
「我的天哪！」他叫道，「竟有这种事？可怜的老伙计。」

===Original===
“It’s terrible. Oh, Jimmy, what are we to do?”

===Chinese===
「太可怕了。唉，吉米，我们可怎么办呢？」

===Original===
“It’s damnable. But you musn’t be downhearted. Many and many a man has been arrested for a crime he has known nothing about. Don’t get upset till we’re sure there is something to be upset about. Tell me the details.”

===Chinese===
「这事儿可恶透顶。可你千万别灰心。多少人莫名其妙地被捕，罪名与他们毫不相干。在确知有值得愁的事之前，先别自乱阵脚。把详细经过讲给我听。」

===Original===
She told him all she knew; of Austin’s visit at eleven on the fatal night, of the hoax that had been played on him, of their learning of Sir William’s disappearance the next day, of the inquest and its adjournment, of Tanner’s visit to her, of the adjourned inquest, and of Austin’s note, and her reply to it. He listened in silence till she had finished.

===Chinese===
她把知道的一切都讲给他听：奥斯汀在出事当晚十一点到访，别人对他使下的骗局，次日他们获悉威廉爵士失踪，验尸审讯及其休会，坦纳对她的造访，休会后复讯的审讯，还有奥斯汀的便函以及她对那封信的答复。他一声不响地听着，直到她讲完。

===Original===
“And you have no idea what this new evidence is?” he asked at last.

===Chinese===
「那么这份新证据是什么，你一点都不知道？」他终于问道。

===Original===
“None whatever.”

===Chinese===
「一点儿也不知道。」

===Original===
“Our first step will be to find that out. I think I can do that. Then if you give me authority to act for you, I’ll see him and hear what he has to say about it. That would probably be the quickest way to learn our defence.”

===Chinese===
「我们的第一步就是把这件事查个水落石出。这个我想我有办法。然后，你若委托我代你行事，我就去见他，听听他对这事怎么说。这大概是摸清我们辩护方略的最快法子。」

===Original===
“Could I see him, Jimmy?”

===Chinese===
「我能去见他吗，吉米？」

===Original===
Her cousin hesitated.

===Chinese===
表哥迟疑了。

===Original===
“I hardly think so,” he said slowly, “at present. Later on if the case really goes to trial there should be no difficulty. Just for the immediate present I shouldn’t make a move. You have done enough for him, writing that letter to the Inspector.”

===Chinese===
「我看怕是不行，」他缓缓说道，「眼下还不见。往后案子若真开庭审理，见一面应当不难。就眼前这一刻，你还是按兵不动的好。你给探长写那封信，为他做的已经够多了。」

===Original===
Lois looked at him searchingly.

===Chinese===
洛伊丝用审视的目光望着他。

===Original===
“Don’t try to keep me away from him, Jimmy,” she pleaded.

===Chinese===
「吉米，别想把我从他身边隔开，」她恳求道。

===Original===
“Of course not. But it would be difficult for you to get an order, and you better wait till we see how things go on. You probably wouldn’t get two orders under any circumstances.”

===Chinese===
「当然不会。可你要拿到探视许可谈何容易，还是先等一等，看看事情怎么走。无论如何，两张许可你多半是拿不到的。」

===Original===
“If you keep me from him, I’ll never forgive you.”

===Chinese===
「你要是拦着不让我见他，我一辈子也不原谅你。」

===Original===
“Dear girl, don’t get notions. I wouldn’t attempt it. But we’re hardly so far on as that yet. There is his family to consider. Have you seen them?”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的姑娘，别胡思乱想。我不会那么做的。只是事情还远没到那一步。还得顾及他的家人。你见过他们吗？」

===Original===
“No. Why should I?”

===Chinese===
「没有。我为什么要见？」

===Original===
“Just that if they’re going to undertake the defence I don’t quite see where we come in. But we’ll find that out.” He paused and then went on: “Now, old girl, you’ve come to me for professional advice and you’re going to get it. Don’t think I’m trying to keep you away from Austin Ponson, but my advice to you is, don’t make any announcement of your engagement for a day or two yet. You will only distract and worry Ponson, and you won’t do any good. The thing to concentrate on is to get him out of the hands of the police. He’ll want all his wits to help with that. He knows you’re not going to drop him, and that will be enough to comfort him. Take my advice, Lois, and let things alone for the present.”

===Chinese===
「不外乎是：倘若他们要出面主持辩护，我就不大看得出我们还有什么插手的余地。不过这个总能打听明白。」他顿了顿，又接着说：「好了，老伙计，你是来向我讨行家忠告的，我这忠告你且听好。别以为我要把你跟奥斯汀·庞森隔开，可我劝你的是：这一两天，先别宣布你们的婚约。你只会搅得庞森心烦意乱，好处一点没有。眼下要聚精会神去办的，是把他从警察手里捞出来。办这件事，他的心智一分一毫都用得着。他知道你不会撇下他，这便尽够安慰他了。听我的劝，洛伊丝，眼下且随它去。」

===Original===
“But how can I see him or do anything for him if the engagement is not known?”

===Chinese===
「可婚约要是无人知晓，我又怎么见他、怎么为他出力呢？」

===Original===
“What could you do if it was?”

===Chinese===
「就算人人皆知，你又能做什么呢？」

===Original===
“Well, I could at least take my share of arranging for his defence.”

===Chinese===
「至少，安排辩护的事，我也能尽我一份力。」

===Original===
“Aren’t you doing that now? What more do you think you could do?”

===Chinese===
「你现在不正在尽这份力吗？你还觉得自己能做什么？」

===Original===
“But how can you approach the family, acting for me, if it’s not known that we’re engaged?”

===Chinese===
「可要是没人知道我们订了婚，你代我去见他家人，名分上又怎么讲得通？」

===Original===
“Perfectly simply. I’ll see him, explain the facts, and get him to employ me. That will give me ten times the authority that you could, and it will leave his mind at rest about you. Believe me, Lois, that’s the thing to be done.”

===Chinese===
「再简单不过。我去见他，把实情说明白，让他聘请我。这样我得到的权限，比你能给的大上十倍，又能让他对你把心放下。相信我，洛伊丝，这才是眼下该办的事。」

===Original===
“Oh, Jimmy, if you’ll do that it would make just all the difference. How good you are!”

===Chinese===
「噢，吉米，你若肯这么做，那就一切都不一样了。你人真好！」

===Original===
“Rubbish. But remember you’ve got to do your part﻿—to sit tight and say nothing. I’ll see all these people and arrange matters.”

===Chinese===
「哪里的话。可记住，你也有你的一份要尽——稳坐不动，一字不提。这些人我都去见，事情都由我来安排。」

===Original===
“But you’ll let me know how you get on?”

===Chinese===
「可进展如何，你总该让我知道吧？」

===Original===
“Sure. I’ll keep nothing back from you.”

===Chinese===
「那自然。我什么都不瞒你。」

===Original===
He asked her some more questions, finally seeing her to St. Pancras and putting her into the Halford train.

===Chinese===
他又问了她几个问题，末了送她到圣潘克拉斯车站，把她送上开往哈尔福德的火车。

===Original===
“By Jove,” he soliloquised as he left the station, “here’s a mess! Whatever happens”﻿—he swore great oaths under his breath﻿—“Lois must be kept out of it. As decent a girl as lives! That’s the way with these extra good women﻿—they *will* throw themselves away on rotters of all kinds. Heavens, what an idea! To announce an engagement with a man arrested for murder! And now first of all to nip that madness in the bud﻿—*if* possible.” He hailed a taxi and was driven to Scotland Yard.

===Chinese===
「我的天，」他走出车站时自言自语道，「这可真是一团糟！无论如何」——他压着嗓子狠狠咒了几句——「决不能把洛伊丝牵扯进去。天底下再找不出这么好的姑娘了！这些格外善良的女人就是这个样——她们*偏偏*要把自己糟蹋给形形色色的无赖。老天爷，这算什么主意！跟一个因谋杀被捕的人宣布订婚！眼下头一件事，就是把这股疯劲儿掐灭在芽里——*如果*掐得灭的话。」他招了一辆出租汽车，坐车去了苏格兰场。

===Original===
“Is Inspector Tanner in?” he asked. “Private business.”

===Chinese===
「坦纳探长在吗？」他问。「私事。」

===Original===
Tanner, it appeared, was just going home, but had not yet left. He returned to his room.

===Chinese===
原来坦纳正要回家，却还没有走。他又转身回了办公室。

===Original===
“My name,” began Jimmy, “is Daunt﻿—James Daunt, junior partner of Willington, Daunt & Daunt, Solicitors, of Lincoln’s Inn.”

===Chinese===
「敝姓多恩特，」吉米开腔道，「詹姆斯·多恩特，威灵顿、多恩特与多恩特律师事务所（Willington, Daunt & Daunt）的初级合伙人，事务所设在林肯律师学院。」

===Original===
Tanner bowed.

===Chinese===
坦纳躬身还礼。

===Original===
“I know your firm, sir,” he said quietly.

===Chinese===
「贵事务所我知道，先生。」他平静地说。

===Original===
“I called on behalf of my cousin, Miss Lois Drew, of Halford. You can no doubt guess my business?”

===Chinese===
「我代舍表妹、哈尔福德的洛伊丝·德鲁小姐来访。我来意如何，您想必猜得到？」

===Original===
“You are going to act for Mr. Ponson?”

===Chinese===
「您是要代庞森先生行事？」

===Original===
“Not quite. At least I am not sure. It is my cousin I am really interested in. I want to ask your help, Mr. Tanner. My cousin, who is a little﻿—well, fond of her own way, has written you a letter, a compromising letter, one which should never have been written. You probably haven’t received it yet, but in it she asks you to inform Mr. Ponson that she holds him to the engagement that they had just fixed up, and is going to announce it at once. Now, Mr. Tanner, you will see that this is quixotic and absurd, and it musn’t be allowed. I have succeeded in getting her to promise to say nothing for the present, and what I want to ask you is to be so good as to respect her confidence, and keep her name out of it.”

===Chinese===
「不尽然。至少我还拿不准。我真正挂心的是舍表妹。坦纳先生，我想请您帮个忙。舍表妹这个人有点儿——呃，自作主张惯了，给您写了一封信，一封授人以柄的信，一封根本不该写的信。您多半还没有收到；她在信里请求您转告庞森先生：她信守他们刚定下的婚约，并且要立即宣布。坦纳先生，您看得出，这纯属堂吉诃德式的荒唐，断不能任其行事。我已劝得她答应眼下暂不声张，我想求您的是：劳驾体恤她这份信托，别让她牵扯进来，别让她的名字沾上这案子。」

===Original===
Tanner looked somewhat distressed.

===Chinese===
坦纳的神色有些为难。

===Original===
“I should be only too glad,” he answered, “to do as you say, especially as I so greatly respected and admired what I saw of Miss Drew, if only it were possible. But I fear it won’t be. I am afraid the engagement will be an integral part of the Crown case. But I may say that I shall not use Miss Drew’s letter. Both she herself and Mr. Ponson told me of the engagement.”

===Chinese===
「若办得到，」他答道，「我巴不得照您说的办，尤其德鲁小姐的为人我见识过几分，实在敬重钦佩。只怕是办不到。恐怕这桩婚约将成为控方（the Crown）案情的组成部分。不过我可以这样说：德鲁小姐那封信，我不会动用。婚约的事，她本人和庞森先生都亲口对我说过。」

===Original===
“You don’t say so? And have you mentioned it to anyone else?”

===Chinese===
「竟是这样？那您对别人提起过吗？」

===Original===
“Only to my chief.”

===Chinese===
「只对上司说过。」

===Original===
“Well, you know the case against Mr. Ponson, and I don’t. I can only ask that you don’t make the fact public until it is absolutely necessary.”

===Chinese===
「这个嘛，控庞森先生的案子，您知底细，我不知。我只能求您：不到万不得已，别把这件事公之于众。」

===Original===
“That I will promise you with pleasure.”

===Chinese===
「这一点，我乐意应承您。」

===Original===
“I am very grateful. And now there is another thing,” and Jimmy explained that he wanted to see Austin, and for what reason.

===Chinese===
「非常感激。还有一件事，」吉米接着说明，他想见奥斯汀，以及为着什么缘由。

===Original===
“I shall certainly raise no objection,” Tanner answered, “but I’m afraid your application will have to go through the usual channel.”

===Chinese===
「我自然毫无异议，」坦纳答道，「不过您的申请，恐怕还得循惯常的程序去办。」

===Original===
“Of course. When is he to be brought before the magistrate?”

===Chinese===
「那是自然。他什么时候被解送法庭？」

===Original===
“Tomorrow at eleven.”

===Chinese===
「明天十一点。」

===Original===
Two hours later the door of Austin’s cell opened to admit the junior partner of Messrs. Willington, Daunt & Daunt. Austin was sitting with his head on his hands and an expression of deep gloom on his face, but he rose with a look of inquiry as Jimmy entered.

===Chinese===
两小时后，奥斯汀牢房的门打开，迎进了威灵顿、多恩特与多恩特律师事务所的那位初级合伙人。奥斯汀正双手抱头坐着，一脸深重的阴郁，见吉米进来，带着询问的神色站起身。

===Original===
“Mr. Ponson?” said the latter as he introduced himself. “I come on rather delicate business. It is on behalf of my cousin, Miss Lois Drew,” and he told of the interview which he had just had. Austin was much moved.

===Chinese===
「是庞森先生吧？」来人一面自我介绍一面说。「我为一件相当微妙的事而来。是受舍表妹洛伊丝·德鲁小姐之托。」接着他把方才那番会面讲了一遍。奥斯汀大为动容。

===Original===
“God bless her!” he cried hoarsely. “Isn’t it unbelievable, even for her! But it must be stopped, Mr. Daunt, at all costs it must be stopped. Her name must be kept out of it no matter what happens.”

===Chinese===
「愿上帝保佑她！」他嗓音沙哑地喊道。「即便是对她来说，这也叫人难以置信，是不是！可这件事必须拦下，多恩特先生，不惜一切代价也必须拦下。无论出什么事，她的名字绝不能沾进来。」

===Original===
Jimmy did not repeat what Tanner had told him.

===Chinese===
吉米没有把坦纳的话复述出来。

===Original===
“I’m glad you agree, Mr. Ponson,” he said. “Now another point. May I ask who is acting for you in this matter?”

===Chinese===
「您能这样看，我很欣慰，庞森先生，」他说。「现在说另一件事。请问这桩案子里，眼下是谁在替您主事？」

===Original===
Austin passed his hand, wearily over his forehead.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀疲惫地抬手抹过额头。

===Original===
“I’m afraid I haven’t arranged anything yet. You see, I only arrived here a few hours ago.”

===Chinese===
「恐怕我什么都还没安排。您知道，我几个钟头前才到这儿。」

===Original===
“But who is your usual man of business?”

===Chinese===
「那您平素的律师是谁？」

===Original===
“Any little thing I have wanted done up to the present I have taken to Mr. Hopkins, of Halford, and he was present at the adjourned inquest on my behalf. He is as straight as a die, but he is elderly, and I dare say out of date. I’m afraid he wouldn’t be much use at this kind of thing. Wills and deeds are more in his line. I wonder, Mr. Daunt, if I asked you to take it on would it draw attention to Lois?”

===Chinese===
「迄今我有什么小事要办，都是找哈尔福德的霍普金斯先生（Mr. Hopkins），休会后复讯那一次，也是他代表我到场的。他为人正派得没话说，只是上了年纪，我怕也早过了时。这种事，他怕帮不上什么忙。遗嘱和契据才是他的本行。多恩特先生，我在想，若是我请您接手这案子，会不会把人的目光引到洛伊丝身上？」

===Original===
“It’s rather a delicate matter as it looks like cadging for business, but for Lois’s sake I should be glad to act for you. As for dragging in her name, I don’t think half a dozen people in the world know we are cousins, and at Halford probably no one. Besides, you would not employ me, but the firm.”

===Chinese===
「说起来这事有点儿微妙，像是上门兜揽生意似的，可看在洛伊丝的份上，我乐意代您行事。至于牵扯她的名字——这世上知道我们是表兄妹的，我想不过寥寥几人，哈尔福德那边恐怕一个也没有。何况，您聘的也不是我这个人，而是事务所。」

===Original===
“Then I do employ your firm﻿—if you will be good enough to act. What is the first step?”

===Chinese===
「那我就聘贵事务所——若蒙您肯屈尊出面的话。第一步是什么？」

===Original===
“The first legal step is to be present in court on your behalf tomorrow. Then I shall get hold of the case against you, after which we will put our heads together over your defence. But there is also your own family to consider. Have you any near relatives other than your mother, sister, and cousin?”

===Chinese===
「第一步，在法律上是明天代表您出庭。随后我要把控方的案卷拿到手，然后我们凑在一起细商您的辩护。不过，您自己家里人也得顾及。除了母亲、妹妹和堂兄，您还有别的近亲吗？」

===Original===
“None.”

===Chinese===
「没有了。」

===Original===
“I presume you would like me to take them into our confidence?”

===Chinese===
「想必您愿意让我把实情也告诉他们吧？」

===Original===
Austin agreed and they discussed the matter further, arranging terms and other details.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀同意了，两人又就此事进一步商议，把委托条件和其他细节一一议定。

===Original===
“Now, Mr. Ponson,” said Jimmy when these were settled, “I have to give you a very solemn warning. Your very life may depend on how you heed it. It is this. You must tell me the truth. I don’t mean refrain from falsehood only, but tell me everything﻿—*everything* you know. It is only fair to say that if you hold anything back I can no longer help you, and you may be signing your own death warrant. Do you realise that?”

===Chinese===
「好了，庞森先生，」这些谈妥之后，吉米说，「我必须向您进一句极郑重的忠告。您这条性命，或许就取决于您如何记取它。忠告就是这一句：您必须对我说实话。我不只是说不许说假话，而是要您把一切都告诉我——您知道的*一切*。有句话先说在明处：您若隐瞒半分，我便无法再帮您，而您也许就是在亲手签下自己的死刑判决书。这一点您明白吗？」

===Original===
“I certainly do. You need not be afraid. I’ll be only too thankful to tell you. Now ask your questions.”

===Chinese===
「我当然明白。您尽管放心。能向您和盘托出，我求之不得。这就请问吧。」

===Original===
“You forget I know nothing of the case as yet. Just tell me the whole business from beginning to end and with all the details you can.”

===Chinese===
「您忘了，这案子我眼下还一无所知。就把事情从头到尾讲一遍，细节能给的都给我。」

===Original===
Austin sat motionless for a moment as if collecting his thoughts, then, settling himself more comfortably, he began to speak.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀一动不动地坐了片刻，像是在收拢思绪，随后把身子安顿得舒服些，开口讲了起来。

===Original===
He opened by explaining his relations with his father, and his mode of life in Halford, and then described his friendship with Lois Drew, culminating in his proposal and the engagement. He told of the note he had received on that fatal Wednesday evening, his taking the boat to the Old Ferry, the self-styled servant of Mrs. Franklyn, her message, his walk to the Abbey, his visit to the Franklyns’ house, and his call at the Drews. Then he recounted the circumstances of Sir William’s death, the call from Innes, the inquiries at Dr. Graham’s, the search for and discovery of the body, the subsequent inquest, the interview with Tanner, the latter’s questions, and the demand for the shoes, and finally, the medical evidence at the adjourned inquest, and his arrest. He stated he had no idea what the discovery was which led to this culmination.

===Chinese===
他先讲明自己与父亲的关系、在哈尔福德的生活情形，继而讲到他与洛伊丝·德鲁的情谊，直到求婚与订婚。他讲到出事那个星期三傍晚收到的那张便条，他驾船前往老渡口，那位自称弗兰克林太太仆人的女子，她口传的信儿，他步行去修道院，走访弗兰克林家，以及到德鲁家的拜访。然后他叙述了威廉爵士之死的种种情节：英尼斯来报信，到格雷厄姆医生处的问询，搜寻与发现尸体，其后的验尸审讯，与坦纳的晤谈，坦纳的种种盘问和对鞋子的索取，最后是复讯时的医方证词和他的被捕。至于究竟是哪一项发现引出了这个结局，他说自己毫无所知。

===Original===
It was clear to Jimmy Daunt that he must hear Tanner’s side of it before he knew where they stood. Nothing more could be done that night. He therefore told his new client to keep his heart up, took his leave and went home.

===Chinese===
吉米·多恩特心里明白：不听听坦纳那边的说法，他就摸不清他们的处境。当夜能做的也就到此为止了。于是他嘱咐这位新委托人打起精神来，然后告辞回家。

===Original===
The proceedings next morning were purely formal, evidence of arrest only being given. Austin was remanded for a week, bail being refused.

===Chinese===
次日上午的开庭纯属例行公事，只听取了执行逮捕的证词。奥斯汀被还押一周，保释遭到拒绝。

===Original===
Daunt made it his business to see Lady Ponson, Enid, and Cosgrove, all of whom expressed themselves as being heartily in agreement with Austin’s selection of advice. It was decided that if the case went on to trial, Sir Mortimer Byecroft, K.C., one of the most eminent criminal experts at the bar, should be retained for the defence.

===Chinese===
多恩特又专程去见了庞森夫人、伊妮德和科斯格罗夫，三人无不表示，由衷赞成奥斯汀择定的这位顾问。大家议定：案子若真进入审理，便延请莫蒂默·拜克罗夫特爵士（Sir Mortimer Byecroft，K.C.）主持辩护——他是律师界最负盛名的刑事名宿之一。

===Original===
When Daunt received the depositions of the crown witnesses, he saw at a glance that he was up against something very much stiffer than he had anticipated. The motive suggested for the crime was horribly adequate. In the face of Austin’s story of his visit to the Abbey ruins, the evidence of Lucy Penrose and young Potts was almost overwhelmingly damaging. But when Daunt read of the purchase of the two identical pairs of shoes, and grasped the theory of the faked alibi which this seemed to support he was genuinely aghast. “Heavens!” he thought, “if the fellow hasn’t an explanation of this, he’s as guilty as sin!”

===Chinese===
等多恩特拿到控方证人的书面证词，一眼便看出，自己面对的东西远比预想的棘手。控方为这桩罪行设定的动机，充分得叫人心头发冷。对照奥斯汀自述的那趟修道院废墟之行，露西·彭罗斯和小波茨的证词几乎构成了压倒性的杀伤。而读到买下两双一模一样的鞋、明白了这似乎支撑起的伪造不在场证明的推论时，多恩特是当真感到了惊骇。「天哪！」他心想，「这家伙若是拿不出个说法，那他就是铁板钉钉的罪人！」

===Original===
Accordingly, Daunt lost no time in again seeing his client, and at his interview he did not mince matters.

===Chinese===
于是多恩特毫不耽搁，再次去见委托人，这一回，他毫不隐讳。

===Original===
“Look here, Mr. Ponson,” he said. “There are two bad bits of evidence against you and I want to hear what you have to say about them. About 9:30 that night you tell me you were halfway to the Abbey. Is that so?”

===Chinese===
「听我说，庞森先生，」他开腔道。「有两件对您不利的坏证据，我要听听您怎么说。那天晚上 9:30 前后，您对我说，您正在去修道院的半路上。是这么回事吧？」

===Original===
“Certainly. I was on the path between the Abbey and the road.”

===Chinese===
「当然。我当时正在修道院与大道之间的那条小路上。」

===Original===
Daunt leant forward and watched the other keenly, as he spoke slowly and deliberately.

===Chinese===
多恩特俯身向前，一面缓缓地、一字一顿地说，一面锐利地注视着对方。

===Original===
“Then how do you account for the fact that you were seen rowing a boat up to the Luce Manor boathouse at just that hour?”

===Chinese===
「那么，有人恰在那个钟点看见您划着一条船，到了卢斯庄园船屋跟前，这件事您作何解释？」

===Original===
Austin Ponson started and his face grew dead white. He sat motionless for several seconds gazing with terrified eyes, at his questioner, and apparently unable to speak.

===Chinese===
奥斯汀·庞森猛地一惊，脸色变得煞白。他一动不动地坐了好几秒，惊恐地瞪着发问的人，看样子连话都说不出来。

===Original===
“What’s that you say?” he gasped at last, licking his dry lips. “Impossible! I﻿—I wasn’t there.” He dropped his head into his hands while the sweat stood in drops on his forehead. Daunt waited silently. His doubts were becoming confirmed. The man surely was guilty.

===Chinese===
「你……你说什么？」他终于喘着气吐出话来，舔了舔发干的嘴唇。「不可能！我——我根本不在那儿。」他双手抱住头，汗珠一颗颗立在额头上。多恩特默默地等着。他心头的疑云正越凝越重。这人只怕真是有罪的。

===Original===
Presently Austin raised his head.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，奥斯汀抬起头来。

===Original===
“This is awful news,” he said hoarsely. “I can only say I wasn’t there. I swear it. There is a mistake. There must be. Who is supposed to have seen me? And from where?”

===Chinese===
「这消息太可怕了，」他声音嘶哑地说。「我只能说，我不在那儿。我发誓。这里面有差错。一定有。据说是谁看见了我？又是从哪里看见的？」

===Original===
In Daunt’s opinion the answer was unconvincing. The man’s manner was shifty. Unless Jimmy was greatly mistaken he was lying. He replied somewhat coldly:

===Chinese===
在多恩特看来，这个回答毫无说服力。这人的神色躲躲闪闪。除非吉米大错特错，此人就是在撒谎。他回话时，语气有些冷淡：

===Original===
“Two people in Dr. Graham’s wood at the other side of the river are prepared to swear to it.”

===Chinese===
「河对岸格雷厄姆医生的林子里，有两个人准备对此宣誓作证。」

===Original===
“Two?” Austin groaned. “My God! How can they? They must have seen someone else, and mistaken him for me.”

===Chinese===
「两个人？」奥斯汀呻吟了一声。「我的上帝！他们怎么可能？他们看见的必定是别人，把他错认成了我。」

===Original===
“Suppose it was proved there was no other boat down the river that night but yours. What will you say then?”

===Chinese===
「倘若查明，那天夜里河下游除了您的船，再没有第二条船。到那时您怎么说？」

===Original===
“What can I say? I don’t understand it. That could not be proved﻿—unless someone took my boat from the Old Ferry.” He sat up eagerly and a gleam came into his eye. “Could that be it, Mr. Daunt? Could whoever worked the trick on me have been watching at the Old Ferry, and have taken my boat when I went ashore? What do you think?”

===Chinese===
「我能说什么？我弄不明白。这不可能被证实——除非有人从老渡口取走了我的船。」他急切地坐直身子，眼里闪起一丝亮光。「会不会就是这个，多恩特先生？在我身上设这个骗局的人，会不会一直守在老渡口，等我上了岸，就把我的船取走了？您觉得呢？」

===Original===
This was an idea which had not occurred to Daunt, and he instantly saw that it might account for the whole thing. Suppose the real murderer, knowing of Austin’s financial relations with his father, had seen how that fact could be used as the basis of a case against the son, and had added details to strengthen it. Suppose he had forged notes, getting Austin to bring a boat to the Old Ferry, and, leaving it there, go to the Abbey. Meanwhile he himself, made up to represent Austin, might have taken the boat to the boathouse, committed the murder and returned the boat to the Old Ferry before Austin arrived back? Daunt felt that this was a possibility which must not be overlooked. It might at any rate be a line of defence.

===Chinese===
这个念头，多恩特自己倒没有想到，他当即看出，它或许能把全局都解释得通。设想真凶知道奥斯汀与他父亲之间的金钱关系，看出这一事实可以拿来充当指控儿子的根基，又添上种种细节把它做实。设想他伪造了字条，诱使奥斯汀把一条船带到老渡口，船撂在那里，人去了修道院。与此同时，他自己装扮成奥斯汀的模样，把船划到船屋，行凶杀人，再赶在奥斯汀回来之前，把船送回老渡口？多恩特感到，这是一个不容忽视的可能。至少，它可以算一条辩护的路子。

===Original===
Then he remembered the shoes. No. If Austin had deliberately made a fake with the shoes, he must be guilty. He spoke again.

===Chinese===
随即他想起了那两双鞋。不。倘若奥斯汀当真蓄意用鞋做了假，那他必定有罪。他又开了口：

===Original===
“Unfortunately, there is another very serious point. Your alibi depends on the fact that the prints made at the Abbey were made by shoes which it can be proved you were wearing on that Wednesday night and at no other time. Isn’t that so?”

===Chinese===
「不幸的是，还有一处极其严重的关口。您的不在场证明，全系于这样一个事实：修道院里留下的鞋印，是那双鞋所留——而那双鞋，可以证明您恰恰在那个星期三晚上穿着，且除那一晚之外再没有穿过。是这样吧？」

===Original===
“That is so.”

===Chinese===
「是这样。」

===Original===
“How many pairs of that kind of shoe had you?”

===Chinese===
「那种鞋，您一共有几双？」

===Original===
“One pair.”

===Chinese===
「一双。」

===Original===
“Then how do you explain the fact that you bought two pairs on the Monday before the murder?”

===Chinese===
「那么，案发前的那个星期一，您一买就买了两双，这件事您作何解释？」

===Original===
This time Austin showed no signs of embarrassment.

===Chinese===
这一次，奥斯汀没有露出半点窘态。

===Original===
“I bought two﻿—yes,” he answered readily, “but I only brought one home. I lost the other.”

===Chinese===
「我是买了两双——是的，」他爽快地答道，「可我只带回家一双。另一双丢了。」

===Original===
“Lost the other? Just how?”

===Chinese===
「丢了另一双？怎么丢的？」

===Original===
“Very simply. I went that Monday to Hunt’s shoe shop in Piccadilly and there I bought a pair of shoes. I had them carefully fitted, and was pleased with them. The shopman gave me a card with their number, in case I should want to replace them. I took them with me, as I was hurrying to catch the 3:25 train from St. Pancras. I had to call at a shop in Regent Street, and I walked there. But as I stepped off the footpath to cross the street, a lorry I hadn’t seen came quickly up, and I had to jump back out of its way. I was startled, and I unfortunately dropped the shoes. As luck would have it they were run over by the lorry. A hawker picked them up and returned them, but one was badly torn, so, as they were no further use to me, I made him a present of them. That left me without any, so I decided to replace them. I noticed another of Messrs. Hunt’s shops close by, and I went in and asked for shoes of the number on the card. That saved me from a troublesome refitting. By the time my purchase was complete I was late for my train. I therefore waited till the 5:15. Does that make the matter clear?”

===Chinese===
「说来简单。那个星期一，我去皮卡迪利的亨特鞋店，在那里买了一双鞋。我仔细试得合脚，穿在脚上很满意。店员给了我一张卡片，上面写着那双鞋的号码，说我若想再配同款，凭它就行。我拎着鞋就走，因为急着赶圣潘克拉斯 3:25 的火车。我还得去摄政街一家铺子办事，便步行前往。可我刚跨下人行道要过街，一辆我没瞧见的卡车疾冲上来，我只好猛地缩脚避让。心里一惊，不巧手里的鞋掉了下去。偏偏不巧，鞋正好叫卡车碾过。一个小贩把鞋拾起来还我，可有一只已经碾得稀烂，对我再无用处，我便干脆送了他。这么一来我手上没了鞋，便决定再买一双补上。我注意到近旁还有一家亨特公司的分店，就进去按卡片上的号码要了同款。这倒省了重新试鞋的麻烦。等这双买妥，火车已经赶不上了。我便一直等到 5:15 那班。这么说，事情清楚了吗？」

===Original===
Daunt was relieved, but somewhat puzzled by what he had heard. Unquestionably, Austin’s explanation was plausible, and he could see no reason why it should not be true. If the hawker who got the shoes could be found it would set this part of the matter at rest, but Daunt feared he would be untraceable. He felt doubtful and dissatisfied in his mind about the whole affair, but he saw that Austin’s statements provided a line of defence, though whether the best available he was not yet certain.

===Chinese===
多恩特松了口气，可听了这番话，心里又有些犯疑。无可否认，奥斯汀的解释说得通，他也看不出有什么理由断定它不是真的。倘若那位得了鞋的小贩能够找到，这一节便可彻底了结，可多恩特只怕他已无从寻访。整桩事在他心里留下的，既有疑虑，又有不痛快，不过他看得出，奥斯汀的说法总算撑起了一条辩护的路子——只是这是不是眼下能有的最佳路数，他还没有把握。

===Original===
Still turning the idea over in his mind he went down the next Saturday to Halford, to spend, on the earnest invitation of Lois, the weekend with the Drews.

===Chinese===
心里还盘算着这个念头，下一个星期六，他应洛伊丝的恳切邀请北上哈尔福德，到德鲁家去度周末。

## X · A Woman’s Wit / X · 女人的机智

===Original===
After dinner on that Saturday evening, Lois Drew had a long conversation with her cousin, James Daunt. She waited until he was seated in the most comfortable chair in the drawing-room with his cigar well under way, and then she spoke of the subject next her heart.

===Chinese===
那个星期六的晚上，晚饭过后，洛伊丝·德鲁同表哥詹姆斯·多恩特作了长谈。她一直等到他在客厅里那张最舒服的椅子上坐定，雪茄也抽得顺畅了，才把心中最挂念的事说了出来。

===Original===
“Tell me, Jimmy,” she begged, “just what you *really* think. I want to understand exactly what we have to meet.”

===Chinese===
「告诉我，吉米，」她恳求道，「你*到底*是怎么想的。我想确切弄明白，我们要面对的是什么。」

===Original===
He told her. Directly and without any attempt to gloss over the uglier facts, he told her all he knew. She listened in silence for the most part, but occasionally interjected shrewd, pertinent questions. Jimmy, who knew and respected his cousin’s intellect, yet marvelled at her grip, her power of letting go irrelevant details, and the unhesitating way in which she went straight to the essential heart of the various points. When he had finished she remained silent for a considerable time.

===Chinese===
他讲了。他把知道的全部直截了当地讲给她听，丝毫不去粉饰那些最难堪的事实。她大半时间默默听着，只是偶尔插进几句犀利而中肯的问话。吉米素来知道并敬重表妹的头脑，却仍惊叹于她的领悟力，惊叹她撇开无关细节的本领，惊叹她一针见血、直捣各项要点要害的果断。他讲完之后，她沉默了好一阵子。

===Original===
“It seems to me then,” she said at last, “that Austin’s suggestion must be the truth﻿—that the murderer forged the notes purporting to be from me, and which brought Austin to the Abbey that night, that he waited for Austin’s arrival at the Old Ferry, that either he had an accomplice there or he himself was disguised as Mrs. Franklyn’s servant, that on Austin’s leaving for the Abbey he made himself up to look like Austin, that he rowed to the boathouse, committed the murder, and returned the boat to the Old Ferry before Austin got back. What do you think, Jimmy?”

===Chinese===
「那么在我看来，」她终于开口道，「奥斯汀的推测必定就是实情——凶手伪造了那几封冒我之名的字条，就在那晚把奥斯汀引到了修道院；他在老渡口候着奥斯汀到来；在那里，他要么有个同伙，要么就亲自扮作了弗兰克林太太的女仆；待奥斯汀动身去修道院，他便把自己妆扮成奥斯汀的模样，划船到船屋，行凶杀人，再赶在奥斯汀回来之前把船送回老渡口。你觉得呢，吉米？」

===Original===
“It seems a possible defence.”

===Chinese===
「这看上去像是一种说得通的辩词。」

===Original===
“It seems more than that; it seems to be what happened. If so, let us consider what that teaches us about the murderer. Several things, I think. Tell me if I go wrong. Firstly, he must have had a strong motive for Sir William’s death. Secondly, he must have known all about the family﻿—Sir William’s habits, the lie of Luce Manor, the household arrangements, and that sort of thing. Thirdly, he must have been acquainted with Austin, and *his* house and habits, and fourthly, he must not only have been aware of my existence and friendship with Austin, but he must have had my handwriting to copy. Surely there can’t be many persons in the world to whom all these conditions apply?”

===Chinese===
「不止如此；这看来就是实情。倘若这样，那我们来推想一下，这能让我们对凶手知道些什么。我想有好几条。有说错的地方你指出来。第一，他必定有置威廉爵士于死地的重大动机。第二，他必定对这一家了如指掌——威廉爵士的起居习惯、卢斯庄园的方位格局、家务上的种种安排，诸如此类。第三，他必定与奥斯汀相熟，熟悉*他的*宅子和习惯；第四，他不但必知道有我这个人、知道我与奥斯汀的交情，还必定拿到过我的笔迹去仿写。天底下能同时满足这些条件的人，想必不会多吧？」

===Original===
“One would say not,” Daunt returned slowly. “It’s very unfortunate, of course, but you must see how the prosecution will use all these points you bring up﻿—every one of them can be turned against Austin.”

===Chinese===
「照说不会多，」多恩特缓缓答道。「这当然非常不幸，可你必须看到，控方会怎样利用你提出的这各条——每一条都能反过来对付奥斯汀。」

===Original===
“I know, but that’s only wasting time. The fact that Austin’s innocence rules him out surely makes the search for the real murderer easier?”

===Chinese===
「这我知道，可那只是白费工夫。奥斯汀既属清白、可以排除，岂不正好让追查真凶变得容易了些？」

===Original===
“Why, that is so, I suppose.” Daunt tried to make his voice cheery and sanguine.

===Chinese===
「呃，这么说倒也是。」多恩特竭力让口气显得开朗乐观。

===Original===
“Very well. I came to that conclusion days ago. Now Jimmy, it’s a horrible thing to say, but who is the only other person we know of that fits the conditions?”

===Chinese===
「很好。这个结论我几天前就得出了。那么吉米，这话听着可怕——在我们晓得的人当中，除他之外，唯一符合条件的还有谁？」

===Original===
Daunt looked up swiftly. It was suddenly evident to him that Lois was speaking with a more direct object than he had thought.

===Chinese===
多恩特猛地抬起头来。他骤然明白，洛伊丝说这些话，怀着比他所想更直接的目的。

===Original===
“I don’t know, Lois,” he answered. “Who?”

===Chinese===
「我不知道，洛伊丝，」他答道。「是谁？」

===Original===
“Who but the cousin﻿—Mr. Cosgrove Ponson?”

===Chinese===
「除了那位堂兄——科斯格罗夫·庞森先生，还能是谁？」

===Original===
“Good Heavens! That never occurred to me. But does he fill the bill?”

===Chinese===
「天哪！这个我可从没想到过。可他符合这些条件吗？」

===Original===
“I have thought so for some time, but it’s a matter for you to find out. But just consider. Mr. Cosgrove benefits by the will﻿—Austin told me so. He knew Sir William and all about Luce Manor; he knew Austin and all about *him*; he was like Austin in appearance; and lastly he knew me﻿—he has dined here with Austin.”

===Chinese===
「我这么想有些日子了，可这事得由你去查明。你想想看。科斯格罗夫先生从遗嘱里得了好处——奥斯汀这么告诉我的。他认识威廉爵士，对卢斯庄园知根知底；他认识奥斯汀，对*他*也一清二楚；他相貌生得像奥斯汀；末了，他还认识我——他同奥斯汀一道在这儿吃过饭。」

===Original===
“Your handwriting?”

===Chinese===
「你的笔迹呢？」

===Original===
“I wrote to thank him for sending me the name of an English *pension* at Cannes﻿—a friend of mine wanted to know.”

===Chinese===
「我给他写信致过谢，谢他寄给我戛纳（Cannes）一家英国人经营的膳宿公寓（*pension*）的名字——我有位朋友想打听。」

===Original===
“When was that?”

===Chinese===
「那是什么时候的事？」

===Original===
“About two months ago.”

===Chinese===
「约莫两个月前。」

===Original===
“Seems rather a long time. And when did he dine?”

===Chinese===
「隔得好像久了些。他来吃饭又是什么时候？」

===Original===
“About a week before that. I happened to mention about the *pension*, and he said he had some addresses and would look them up.”

===Chinese===
「比那早一个星期上下。我碰巧提起了那家膳宿公寓，他说他手头存着几个地址，愿意替我翻找出来。」

===Original===
“And what kind of man is he personally?”

===Chinese===
「那么他这个人本身怎么样？」

===Original===
Lois did not reply for some moments.

===Chinese===
洛伊丝半晌没有作声。

===Original===
“That’s hardly a fair question,” she said at last. “I have to admit taking a dislike to him. But it’s not a question of my likes or dislikes. I think it is essential that you should find out something about him. Find out where he was on that Wednesday evening.”

===Chinese===
「这样的问题可难答得公允，」她末了说。「我不得不承认，我是看他不顺眼。但这不在我喜欢不喜欢。我认为要紧的是，你应当去把他的底细摸一摸。查一查案发那个星期三的晚上，他人在哪儿。」

===Original===
Daunt smoked in silence. He was thinking that if Austin were out of the way as well as Sir William, Cosgrove’s gains would not improbably be considerably increased. There might be something in this idea of Lois’s after all. A few inquiries would do no harm at any rate.

===Chinese===
多恩特默默抽着烟。他心里盘算，倘若奥斯汀也同威廉爵士一样没了，科斯格罗夫所得的好处，多半还会大大增加。洛伊丝这个想法，说不定真有几分道理。反正，打听打听总没坏处。

===Original===
“Well,” he said at last, “I’ll do as you say. I’ll find out something about him.”

===Chinese===
「好吧，」他终于说，「就依你。我去把他的底细查一查。」

===Original===
They continued the discussion, and it was arranged that as soon as any information was forthcoming, Lois would go to town, and they would have another talk.

===Chinese===
两人接着又谈了一阵，并约定：一旦打听到什么消息，洛伊丝便进城来，两人再作一次商谈。

===Original===
As Sir William Ponson’s will was to be one of the factors in the Crown case, Daunt had no difficulty in obtaining a copy. That, and a few judicious inquiries convinced him of the importance of Lois’s suggestion. There seemed no question that Cosgrove’s motive for the deed was at least as strong as Austin’s.

===Chinese===
由于威廉·庞森爵士的遗嘱将是控方案情的一个要件，多恩特毫不费力地弄到了一份副本。这份遗嘱，加上几处得体的打听，使他确信洛伊丝的提议非同小可。毫无疑问，科斯格罗夫作案的动机，至少与奥斯汀的一般大。

===Original===
For some time Daunt puzzled over the best way to get hold of his information. Then it occurred to him that so wide-awake an official as an Inspector of Scotland Yard would certainly have foreseen and considered all that he and Lois had discussed. As Cosgrove had not been arrested, there must be some flaw in the case he was trying to make. He decided to see Tanner once more, in the hope of gaining some information.

===Chinese===
有好一阵子，多恩特为打听消息的最好路数大费踌躇。继而他想道，像苏格兰场探长这样机警的官员，必定早已料到并掂量过他和洛伊丝讨论的这一切。科斯格罗夫既未被捕，他正设法织成的那张案网里必有某个破绽。他决计再去见坦纳一面，指望讨得一点消息。

===Original===
With a man like Tanner there was nothing to be gained by any but the most direct methods. Daunt could ask for what he wanted, and either get it or be refused, but he felt he could not obtain it by a trick. To try his luck he called at the Yard and inquired for the Inspector.

===Chinese===
跟坦纳这样的人打交道，除非最直截了当的法子，旁的都讨不到便宜。多恩特尽可以开口讨他要的东西，要么到手，要么被回绝，可他自觉总不能靠圈套弄到手。为碰碰运气，他到苏格兰场求见探长。

===Original===
“I want to get some information, Mr. Tanner,” he said, when they had conversed for a few moments. “I’m going to ask you for it in confidence, but you may not consider it proper to give it to me, and if so, there is of course no more to be said. It’s not directly about the case.”

===Chinese===
「我想打听一件事，坦纳先生，」寒暄几句之后，他说道。「我要私下里向您讨教，不过您也许觉得不便告诉我；若当真如此，那自然不必多言。这不算直接关乎本案。」

===Original===
“What is it, sir?”

===Chinese===
「什么事，先生？」

===Original===
“It’s this. In going into this matter it has struck me that the nephew, Cosgrove Ponson, had as much to gain by his uncle’s death as the accused. It is obvious that that must have struck you also. I wondered if you would tell me why you acquitted him in your mind?”

===Chinese===
「是这样。深入研究这桩案子的过程中，我注意到，侄子科斯格罗夫·庞森从叔父之死所得的好处，同被告一样多。这一点想必也早入过您的法眼。不知您可肯告诉我，您心里为何给他开了脱？」

===Original===
“Now don’t you get astray on that notion, Mr. Daunt. It won’t wash. I went into that, and I may tell you for your private information Cosgrove is as innocent as you are.”

===Chinese===
「我说，多恩特先生，您可别在这个念头上走岔了。那站不住脚。那一层我查过了，不妨私底下告诉您：科斯格罗夫跟您一样清白。」

===Original===
“So I gathered from your action, in the matter, but if you could see your way to give me particulars, I should be greatly obliged. You see, it’s Miss Drew. She’s got it into her head Cosgrove was the man, and I’d like to be able to clear the thing up to her.”

===Chinese===
「您在这事上的处置，我也大致猜到了几分，不过您若肯把详情见告，我将感激不尽。是这样，德鲁小姐认准了科斯格罗夫就是凶手，我想把这件事向她说个明白。」

===Original===
Tanner thought for some moments.

===Chinese===
坦纳沉吟了片刻。

===Original===
“I’d like to oblige both you and Miss Drew,” he said, “but I’m not just sure that I ought. However, as you say, it’s not exactly on the case, and if you give me your word to keep the thing to yourself I’ll tell you.”

===Chinese===
「您和德鲁小姐的面子，我都想给，」他说，「只是拿不准该不该。不过，诚如您所言，这不算正式案情；您若向我担保绝不外传，我就告诉您。」

===Original===
“I promise most gratefully.”

===Chinese===
「我担保，多谢您了。」

===Original===
“Very well. The man has an alibi,” and Tanner repeated Cosgrove’s story of the visit to Montrose, the missing of the 7:15, the return to his rooms, the call at the theatre on Miss Belcher, and the final journey north by the 10:30. Then he explained how he had checked Cosgrove’s statements, and produced his calculation of times and distances, showing that Cosgrove could not have motored to Luce Manor.

===Chinese===
「那好。此人有不在场证明。」坦纳于是把科斯格罗夫的供述复述了一遍：去蒙特罗斯相马，误了 7:15 的车，回到寓所，去剧院拜访贝尔彻小姐，末了乘 10:30 的车北上。接着他讲了自己如何逐条核实科斯格罗夫的陈述，又拿出那份时间与路程的推算，证明科斯格罗夫绝无可能坐汽车赶去卢斯庄园。

===Original===
To Daunt the whole thing seemed utterly conclusive. Apart from the mere fact that it had satisfied Tanner﻿—no mean test﻿—he could not himself see any possibility of a flaw. With considerable apprehension of the disappointment Lois would feel, he telephoned to her and arranged their consultation for that evening.

===Chinese===
在多恩特听来，这一切铁案如山。姑且不说它连坦纳都说服了——这绝非小可的检验——他本人也寻不出半点破绽的可能。他料想洛伊丝必将大失所望，怀着这份不安，他给她挂了电话，约好当晚会商。

===Original===
She heard his story almost in silence. But she did not show the chagrin he expected.

===Chinese===
她几乎一言不发地听完了他的叙述，却没有露出一丝他料想中的懊丧。

===Original===
“If the thing was obvious,” she said in answer to his comment, “Cosgrove would have been arrested and not Austin. But I feel absolutely certain that that alibi of Cosgrove’s is a fraud. He has tricked Inspector Tanner. How he has done it is what you’ve got to find out.”

===Chinese===
「这事情若当真一目了然，」她回应他的议论道，「被捕的就该是科斯格罗夫，而不是奥斯汀。可我绝对确信，科斯格罗夫那份不在场证明是个骗局。他骗过了坦纳探长。他是怎么做到的，就要靠你去查出来了。」

===Original===
“My dear girl,” Daunt remonstrated, “it’s all very well to talk like that, and I’ll do my best of course, but you know, if Tanner with all his opportunities was taken in, it’s not too likely I shall find the flaw.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的姑娘，」多恩特劝道，「话说得倒轻巧。我当然会尽力，可你想，坦纳手里握着那么多门路尚且上了当，我找到破绽的希望实在不大。」

===Original===
“It’s quite likely,” she declared. “Inspector Tanner was not specially looking for a flaw; you are. Don’t you see﻿—there must be a flaw. Look at it like this. A man resembling Austin was met by Sir William at the boathouse on that Wednesday night. It *must* have been Cosgrove, because no one else is sufficiently like Austin to be mistaken for him. No kind of facial makeup will meet the case, because Sir William himself evidently was satisfied. Therefore Cosgrove’s alibi must be false. Don’t you agree with me?”

===Chinese===
「希望很大，」她断言。「坦纳探长当初并非专为寻破绽而去；你是。你难道不明白——那里面*必定*有个破绽。这样来看：案发那个星期三晚上，威廉爵士在船屋见到的是一个酷肖奥斯汀的人。那人*必定*是科斯格罗夫，因为再没有第二个人像奥斯汀像到会被认错。无论怎样的化装都圆不过这一节，因为显然连威廉爵士本人都深信不疑。因此，科斯格罗夫的不在场证明必是假的。你不同意我吗？」

===Original===
“It seems reasonable,” Jimmy admitted. “But the alibi certainly looks right enough too.”

===Chinese===
「听着有理，」吉米承认。「可那份不在场证明瞧上去也确实滴水不漏。」

===Original===
“I admit that. It may be so good that we’ll never find the flaw. But we must try. Oh, Jimmy,” she turned to him beseechingly, “remember what is at stake﻿—his life﻿—both our lives. You will try, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
「这我承认。它也许严密得叫我们永远找不出破绽。可我们必须试一试。噢，吉米，」她转向他，满眼恳求，「想想这干系着什么——他的性命——我们两个人的性命。你会尽力的，对不对？」

===Original===
“Of course I’ll try, and what’s more, I expect to succeed,” Jimmy lied bravely. And he spoke in the same confident tone as, after dinner, he went to the station with her, and saw her off by the 9:30 to Halford. But his secret feelings were very different.

===Chinese===
「我当然会尽力，而且不瞒你说，我料定能成。」吉米硬着头皮撒了个谎。饭后他送她去车站，用同样自信的口吻，看她上了 9:30 开往哈尔福德的车。可他心底的滋味，全然是另一回事。

===Original===
Two days later he had another call from Lois.

===Chinese===
两天后，洛伊丝又来了电话。

===Original===
“You needn’t be frightened,” she smiled at him; “I am not going to haunt the office and make your life a burden to you. But I have been thinking over our problem. I want you, Jimmy, to begin an investigation. Will you?”

===Chinese===
「你不必害怕，」她含笑对他说；「我不打算赖在事务所里，把你的日子搅得不得安生。可我一直在琢磨咱们那个难题。吉米，我要你着手一项侦查。你肯吗？」

===Original===
“Why certainly, if I can. What is it?”

===Chinese===
「那还用说，只要办得到。是什么侦查？」

===Original===
“If you haven’t time yourself, and I don’t expect you will have, employ a private detective. But get a good man who will do the work thoroughly.”

===Chinese===
「你要是自己没工夫——我想你多半没有——就雇一位私家侦探。不过要雇个好的，肯把事情办到底的人。」

===Original===
“Yes, yes. But what exactly is to be done?”

===Chinese===
「好，好。可具体要办什么？」

===Original===
“This. The evidence seems to me overwhelming that Mr. Cosgrove missed the 7:15 at King’s Cross on that Wednesday night, and went to Montrose by the 10:30. But what did he do in between?”

===Chinese===
「是这样。依我看，铁证如山：科斯格罗夫先生案发那个星期三晚上在国王十字误了 7:15 的车，改乘 10:30 去了蒙特罗斯。可这中间他都干了些什么？」

===Original===
Daunt was puzzled.

===Chinese===
多恩特被弄糊涂了。

===Original===
“I don’t understand,” he said. “We have the butler’s and Miss Belcher’s evidence to corroborate Cosgrove’s own story there. I don’t see that we can reasonably doubt he did what he said.”

===Chinese===
「我不明白，」他说。「这一节有管家的证词和贝尔彻小姐的证词，佐证了科斯格罗夫本人的说法。我看不出我们凭什么去合理地怀疑他没有照他说的做。」

===Original===
“Don’t you? But I do. Probably both these people were interested. Miss Belcher we know was﻿—she was on too friendly terms not to be. And the butler may have been well paid to tell his story. Another thing makes me doubt Miss Belcher. You remember the conversation Mr. Tanner overheard between her and Mr. Cosgrove in the restaurant? They mentioned an alibi. Mr. Tanner thought they were talking about Austin. I don’t believe it. It was Mr. Cosgrove’s own alibi they were discussing. What do you think?”

===Chinese===
「你看不出，我看得出来。这两个人多半都利害攸关。贝尔彻小姐这一层我们已经知道了——她同他过从太密，想不攸关也难。至于那管家，讲那套话保不定拿了一大笔钱。还有一桩叫我疑心贝尔彻小姐。你记得坦纳先生在饭馆里偷听到她同科斯格罗夫先生的谈话吗？他们提到了不在场证明。坦纳先生以为他们说的是奥斯汀。我不信。他们商量的，正是科斯格罗夫先生自己的不在场证明。你怎么看？」

===Original===
“It’s possible, of course,” Daunt answered slowly, “but I question if we can be sure of it.” He began to think Lois had got an obsession.

===Chinese===
「这当然有可能，」多恩特缓缓答道，「可我怀疑我们能否拿得准。」他开始觉得，洛伊丝是染上执念了。

===Original===
“Well, whether or not is immaterial. What matters is that Mr. Cosgrove’s whereabouts after 7:30 has not been proved.”

===Chinese===
「也罢，是不是执念无关紧要。要紧的是，7:30 以后科斯格罗夫先生身在何处，至今没有查实。」

===Original===
“But you forget Tanner’s time table. Cosgrove wouldn’t have had time to go to Luce Manor between the trains.”

===Chinese===
「可你忘了坦纳那份时间推算。两班车之间，科斯格罗夫来不及去卢斯庄园。」

===Original===
“Yes!” cried Lois excitedly, “he would! That’s the point I’ve been coming to. According to Mr. Tanner’s calculations he would have had time or almost time, to go to Luce Manor and back, but he wouldn’t have had time to commit the murder. But Mr. Tanner assumed he had first driven to his rooms. If he had gone direct from King’s Cross he would have had time for all.”

===Chinese===
「不对！」洛伊丝激动地叫道，「来得及！我正要说到这一步。照坦纳先生的推算，他来回卢斯庄园是来得及的，或者差不多来得及，只是来不及行凶。可坦纳先生是先假定他先回了趟寓所。他若从国王十字直接动身，全部时间都够了。」

===Original===
This was a new idea to Daunt, and he had to admit its possibility.

===Chinese===
这对多恩特是个新想法，他不得不承认有此可能。

===Original===
“It may be so certainly,” he answered, “but how are we to prove it? That butler won’t give himself away if he has lied.”

===Chinese===
「这当然也许是真的，」他答道，「可我们怎么证明？那管家若撒了谎，是不会自己露馅的。」

===Original===
“I’ve thought of that, too, and there seems to be one way we might get at it. Mr. Cosgrove said he took three drives during that time﻿—first, from King’s Cross to Knightsbridge; second, from Knightsbridge to the Empire; and third, from the Follies’, to King’s Cross. Could we not find the cabmen, or at least one of them?”

===Chinese===
「这一层我也想到了，倒似乎有一条路走得通。科斯格罗夫先生说，那段时间他坐了三趟车——头一趟，从国王十字到骑士桥；第二趟，从骑士桥到帝国剧院；第三趟，从「福利斯」回国王十字。我们何不去找找那些车夫，哪怕只找到其中一个也好？」

===Original===
“By Jove, Lois, you should have been a detective,” Daunt said with half-unwilling admiration. “I believe it’s a good notion.”

===Chinese===
「老天，洛伊丝，你本该去当侦探，」多恩特半带不甘地叹服道。「我看这是个好主意。」

===Original===
“You see, if we were to search for these men thoroughly and fail to find them, it would greatly strengthen my theory. On the other hand, if we found them we would be sure of what we are up against, for if Mr. Cosgrove took any one of the drives, his statement must be true. Will you try to find them, Jimmy? Or”﻿—her eyes brightened eagerly﻿—“let’s do it together. Would you mind?”

===Chinese===
「你想想，我们若把这些车夫彻头彻尾找一遍却一个也找不到，我的推断就会大大加分。反过来，倘若找到了，我们也就摸清了对手的底——因为科斯格罗夫先生只要坐过其中任何一趟，他的话就必是真的。你肯去找吗，吉米？或者」——她的眼睛热切地亮了起来——「咱们一起干。你介意吗？」

===Original===
“Mind?” he echoed. “Dear girl, what do you take me for? I’m ready to begin now﻿—in collaboration with you, that is.”

===Chinese===
「介意？」他应声道。「亲爱的姑娘，你把我当成什么人了？我这就准备开工——确切说，是与你通力合作。」

===Original===
“Dear Jimmy, you are so good. I can never thank you enough.”

===Chinese===
「好吉米，你人真好。我感激不尽。」

===Original===
“What utter rubbish. Now let’s get to work. Have you made any plans?”

===Chinese===
「瞧你说的什么话。好了，干活吧。你有什么章程没有？」

===Original===
“I thought perhaps we could get lists from the taxi companies of the men on the various stands. Then I thought we could see all those on the stands nearest the three starting points. If it was none of these could we not send a letter to each driver in London﻿—get some office supplies place to do it, you know? If this failed we might try newspaper advertisements.”

===Chinese===
「我想，我们兴许能从出租车行弄到各候客点上司机的名单。然后，把三个起点附近候客点上的司机挨个见一见。若还找不着，能不能给伦敦每个司机发一封信——你懂吧，找家文具行去代办？这再不成，还可以试登报纸广告。」

===Original===
“Excellent. We could get the lists of the men through Tanner, if he would give them. I’ll ring him up now.”

===Chinese===
「好极了。名单可以通过坦纳去弄，只要他肯给。我这就给他打电话。」

===Original===
Inspector Tanner was at the Yard. On Daunt satisfying him as to the reason of the demand, he promised to have the information looked up and supplied.

===Chinese===
坦纳探长正在苏格兰场。多恩特向他说明索要名单的缘由，他听罢应承下来，答应着人查好送来。

===Original===
The next day the lists came and the cousins set off to commence their investigations. They saw a number of the men on the stands in question, others at the depots and still others at their homes. After three strenuous days they had gone over them all. But they learnt nothing. None of the men had driven Cosgrove.

===Chinese===
第二天名单到了，表兄妹俩便动手开始查访。他们见了那些候客点上的一部分司机，又到车场里见了另一些，再到各人家里去见其余的。辛辛苦苦查了三天，全部过了一遍，却一无所获。没有一个司机拉过科斯格罗夫。

===Original===
“It’s hopeful,” Lois announced as they dined together that evening, “but we must now try the circular.”

===Chinese===
「大有希望，」那晚两人一起吃饭时，洛伊丝宣告道，「不过眼下得试试发通函了。」

===Original===
They drafted a letter offering a reward of £5 for information as to the identity of the man who had driven a fare answering to Cosgrove’s description on any of the three trips in question. With this Daunt called to see Tanner on the following morning. He told him what they had done, and what they proposed, and asked for a complete list of the taxi and cabdrivers of London. Tanner, nothing loath to have his own conclusions verified, had the information made out. Then Daunt went to an office supplies firm in New Oxford Street, and arranged for a circular to be sent to each man on the list﻿—several thousand.

===Chinese===
他们起草了一封信，悬赏五镑征求线索：谁曾在上述三趟车程中载过一名与科斯格罗夫相貌相符的乘客，请报告此人身份。第二天早晨，多恩特揣着信去见坦纳，讲了他们已做的事和下一步的打算，请他开一份伦敦出租汽车与马车车夫的完整名单。坦纳巴不得有人来验证自己的结论，当即着人把资料备齐。多恩特随后去了新牛津街（New Oxford Street）一家文具行，安排按名单给每人寄发一份通函——足有几千份。

===Original===
Two days passed and there was no answer, but on the third day a taxi-driver giving his name as John Hoskins called at Daunt’s office. Jimmy saw him at once.

===Chinese===
两天过去，杳无音信；到第三天，一个自称约翰·霍斯金斯（John Hoskins）的出租汽车司机来到多恩特的事务所。吉米立刻见了他。

===Original===
“It was abaht this ’ere letter,” said the man. “I guess I’m the man you want.”

===Chinese===
「就是为这封信的事儿，」那人说。「我寻思着，你们要找的就是我。」

===Original===
“Yes? You drove a fare on one of those trips on that Wednesday evening?”

===Chinese===
「哦？案发那个星期三晚上，那三趟车里你载过一位客人？」

===Original===
“Yes, sir. I were just passing out of King’s Cross after setting down a lady and gent, when the gent ’e hails me, ‘Engaged?’ ’e asks. ‘No, sir,’ I says. ‘174B Knightsbridge,’ ’e says. I drove ’im there, and that’s all I knows abaht it.”

===Chinese===
「是，先生。那晚我拉了一男一女到国王十字，刚空着车出来，那位先生就招呼我。『有空儿吗？』他问。『有，先生，』我说。『骑士桥 174B，』他说。我就把他送到了那儿。别的事儿，我压根儿不知道。」

===Original===
Daunt opened a drawer and took out half a dozen cabinet photographs, which, in unconscious imitation of Tanner, he had procured. One was of Cosgrove, the others of men as like Cosgrove as Jimmy could find.

===Chinese===
多恩特拉开抽屉，取出半打六寸相片——不知不觉学起了坦纳的样，这些相片是他事先备下的。一张是科斯格罗夫，其余几张的人，是吉米尽量找来同科斯格罗夫相像的。

===Original===
“Is the man you drove among those?” he asked, handing them over.

===Chinese===
「你拉的人在这些里头吗？」他边问边把相片递过去。

===Original===
The driver glanced over them and unhesitatingly picked out Cosgrove’s.

===Chinese===
司机扫了几眼，毫不迟疑地挑出了科斯格罗夫那张。

===Original===
“That’s ’im, mister,” he said decisively.

===Chinese===
「就是他，先生。」他说得斩钉截铁。

===Original===
“And what time did you pick him up?”

===Chinese===
「你几点钟拉上他的？」

===Original===
“Abaht 7:30. I left ’im in Knightsbridge at a quarter to eight.”

===Chinese===
「7:30 上下。八点差一刻，我把他撂在了骑士桥。」

===Original===
“That’s all right, my man. It’s what I wanted to know. If you give me your name and address I’ll give you the five pounds.”

===Chinese===
「这就成了，老弟。我要打听的就是这个。你把姓名住址留下，我把那五镑给你。」

===Original===
Jimmy telegraphed the news to Lois at Halford, using for the benefit of the postal officials a code on which they had previously agreed. The information, he feared, would be a heavy blow to her. She had so confidently made up her mind that Cosgrove was the guilty man, and here was proof﻿—to Daunt it seemed final and conclusive proof﻿—of his innocence. Even Lois herself had admitted that if Cosgrove had indeed returned to his rooms after leaving King’s Cross, it would have been impossible for him to have visited Luce Manor﻿—times and distances made that certain. Miss Belcher’s confirmation of his visit to the Follies, and the discovery of the other two taxi drivers were therefore not required. As matters stood, Cosgrove’s innocence was demonstrated.

===Chinese===
吉米拍电报把消息告知哈尔福德的洛伊丝，用的是两人事先约定的暗语，好叫电报局的人看不明白。他担心这消息对她是沉重一击。她曾那样笃定地认定科斯格罗夫就是凶手，而如今——在多恩特看来是最终也最确凿的证明——却证明了他的清白。就连洛伊丝自己也承认过：科斯格罗夫若真是离开国王十字后回了寓所，便绝无可能到过卢斯庄园——时间和路程摆在那里，错不了。如此一来，贝尔彻小姐对他去「福利斯」的佐证、另外两名司机的下落，都用不着再查了。事已至此，科斯格罗夫的清白已成定论。

===Original===
So Daunt reasoned, but not so Lois Drew. As she tossed sleepless on her bed that night she racked her brains for some flaw in the case, some loophole that might save her lover. But the more she thought it over, the more hopeless it seemed. As dawn brightened slowly into day she had to admit herself beaten. And then, just as a delightful drowsiness began to creep over her restless, wearied body, an idea flashed into her mind. She remained motionless, hardly daring to breathe as its full significance gradually dawned upon her. When it did so all chance of sleep vanished. Her eyes became very bright, and she laughed contentedly to herself.

===Chinese===
多恩特是这么推断的，洛伊丝·德鲁却不是。那天夜里，她辗转床榻，久不能寐，为案情搜肠刮肚，想寻出一线破绽、一个能救她恋人的口子。可她越想越觉无望。晨光渐渐亮成白昼，她只得认了输。恰恰在那时，一阵惬意的睡意悄悄爬上她躁动疲惫的身子，一个念头却倏地闪进脑海。她一动不动躺着，几乎不敢喘气，任那念头的全部含义慢慢显豁出来。待到豁然尽显，睡意登时荡然无存。她双眼灼灼发亮，心满意足地独自笑了。

===Original===
She travelled to town by an early train and was in Daunt’s office soon after it opened. She received his condolences quietly, then startled him by saying demurely:

===Chinese===
她乘早班火车进了城，事务所一开门便到了多恩特那里。她安然领受了他的慰问，随即却文文静静地抛出一句话，惊得他一怔：

===Original===
“I want you to send out another circular to the taxi-men, Jimmy. I have it here.”

===Chinese===
「吉米，我要你再给司机们发一份通函。稿子在这儿。」

===Original===
The paper she handed him read:

===Chinese===
她递给他的那张纸上写道：

===Original===
“Ten Pounds Reward

===Chinese===
「悬赏十镑

===Original===
“The above reward will be paid to the taxi driver who picked up a fare”﻿—here followed a description of Cosgrove﻿—“about 7:50 on Wednesday evening, 7th July, in or near Knightsbridge, on his identifying the man picked up from a photograph, and saying where he was set down. Apply﻿—” and here followed Jimmy’s address.

===Chinese===
「上述赏金，将付给这样一位出租汽车司机：7月7日星期三晚上约莫 7:50，他在骑士桥城内或近郊载过一位乘客」——此处列有科斯格罗夫的相貌特征——「只要他从相片中认出所载之人，并说出让他下车的地方，即可领取。应征请至——」后面便是吉米的地址。

===Original===
“But my dear girl,” the latter objected, “we have already sent that out, or practically that.”

===Chinese===
「可是亲爱的姑娘，」后者反对道，「这个我们已经发过了，至少差不多发过了。」

===Original===
“Never mind, Jimmy,” she said, with one of the brilliant smiles that lit up her face and made it momentarily beautiful. “Do this for me, and don’t ask questions.” Before he realized what she was going to do, she had kissed him lightly on the forehead, and with a whirl of skirts was gone.

===Chinese===
「别管那些，吉米，」她说着，脸上绽出一个明艳的微笑，一时间容光焕发。「替我把这事办了，别问为什么。」不等他明白过来她要做什么，她已在他额上轻轻一吻，裙裾一旋，人已经走了。

===Original===
“By Jove!” said Jimmy weakly to himself as the door closed. “What bee has she got in her bonnet now? At any rate she might have waited and explained.” But he did what he had been asked, and two days later the new circular was in the hands of the taxi-men.

===Chinese===
「老天！」门关上后，吉米有气无力地自言自语。「她这回又犯了什么痴念？不管怎样，总该等一等、说明白呀。」但他还是照她的吩咐办了。两天后，新的通函发到了众司机手里。

===Original===
And it bore early fruit. Only a few hours after its distribution there was an answer. A small, sallow, rat-faced man in a peaked cap and leather coat called to see Daunt.

===Chinese===
而且很快结了果。分发出去才几个钟头，就有了回音。一个面色蜡黄、尖嘴猴腮的小个子男人，戴鸭舌帽，穿皮外套，上门来见多恩特。

===Original===
“You think you picked up the man described in the letter?” asked Jimmy, as he produced his six photographs. “Was he one of these?”

===Chinese===
「你说你载过信里描述的那个人？」吉米问，一面摆出他那六张相片。「是这里头的一个吗？」

===Original===
Like his confrère of a day or two earlier, the man glanced over the cards and unhesitatingly drew out Cosgrove’s portrait.

===Chinese===
同一两天前那位同行一样，这人扫了几眼相片，毫不迟疑地抽出了科斯格罗夫的画像。

===Original===
“That’s ’im, mister,” he also said decisively.

===Chinese===
「就是他，先生。」他也说得斩钉截铁。

===Original===
“Where exactly did you pick him up?”

===Chinese===
「你到底在哪儿拉上他的？」

===Original===
“In Knightsbridge, not far from Piccadilly.”

===Chinese===
「在骑士桥，离皮卡迪利不远。」

===Original===
“At what time?”

===Chinese===
「几点钟？」

===Original===
“About ten minutes to eight.”

===Chinese===
「八点差十分。」

===Original===
“And where did you set him down?”

===Chinese===
「你让他在哪儿下的车？」

===Original===
“Over thirty miles away﻿—at a crossroads away beyond Luton.”

===Chinese===
「三十多英里外——过了卢顿还老远的一个十字路口。」

===Original===
James Daunt sprang excitedly to his feet.

===Chinese===
詹姆斯·多恩特激动得一跃而起。

===Original===
“What?” he roared. “Where did you say?” Here, surely, was the impossible! All were agreed that Cosgrove could not have made the run in the time, and yet, it now seemed, he had done so. And then the thought of the tremendous consequences of this discovery overwhelmed every other consideration. If Cosgrove had really been to Luce Manor, particularly after his own denial, he must unquestionably be guilty. And if he were guilty, Austin was innocent. Jimmy believed he now held the evidence which would save his client.

===Chinese===
「什么？」他吼道。「你说在哪儿？」瞧，这不正是那桩不可能的事么！众口一词，科斯格罗夫决计来不及跑完这一趟，可如今看来，他偏偏跑成了。紧接着，这一发现的千钧分量压倒了其他一切念头。科斯格罗夫若真到过卢斯庄园——尤其在他亲口否认之后——那就毫无疑问有罪。他有罪，奥斯汀便清白。吉米相信，能救他当事人的证据，此刻已握在自己手里。

===Original===
He thought rapidly for a few seconds.

===Chinese===
他急速地想了几秒钟。

===Original===
“At what hour did you reach this crossroads?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「你们几点钟到的那个十字路口？」他问。

===Original===
“About quarter-past nine, sir. I remember noticing when I was making up the money.”

===Chinese===
「九点一刻上下，先生。我记得清楚，那会儿我正算车钱来着。」

===Original===
And the bogus servant met Austin at the Old Ferry about then! Truly this was a great find!

===Chinese===
而那个假女仆，正是在那前后在老渡口见的奥斯汀！这可真是一大发现！

===Original===
“Are you engaged within the next hour or two?” Jimmy went on.

===Chinese===
「这一两个钟头，你有空吗？」吉米接着问。

===Original===
“No, sir.”

===Chinese===
「没有，先生。」

===Original===
“Very good. Will you drive me to this crossroads? I’ll make it worth your while.”

===Chinese===
「那好。你肯拉我去那个十字路口吗？亏待不了你。」

===Original===
They stopped at a post office and Jimmy sent a telegram to Lois asking her to be at the end of the Old Ferry lane at midday. Then the run began.

===Chinese===
途中在一处邮局停了停，吉米发电报给洛伊丝，请她正午时分到老渡口小道的路口等候。随后，车子便出发了。

===Original===
As the vehicle slipped quickly through the traffic, Daunt chuckled with delight. Though he did not in the least understand how Cosgrove had managed it, it was at least evident that he had visited Luce Manor before taking the 10:30 to Montrose. And that, Daunt felt more than ever certain, meant his guilt and the breakdown of the case against Austin. Though, to do him justice, Jimmy’s chief joy was the thought of the happiness this would bring to Lois, yet he was human enough to realise the kudos which must come to him personally from the skilful way he had unravelled the mystery. And yet, had he unravelled it? As he looked back he had to admit that every particle of credit must go to the girl. She it was who had suggested the steps which had led to success﻿—she who had evidently guessed the solution which even now still eluded him.

===Chinese===
汽车在车流中轻快地穿行，多恩特喜得暗自窃笑。他虽然全然不明白科斯格罗夫是怎么做到的，但至少可以断定：他在乘 10:30 去蒙特罗斯之前，到过卢斯庄园。而这——多恩特比任何时候都确信——就意味着他的罪行，意味着加在奥斯汀身上那桩案子的崩塌。不过说句公道话，吉米最大的欢喜，还是想到这事将带给洛伊丝的幸福；可他毕竟也是凡人，并非意识不到，自己如此巧妙地解开这团乱麻，随之而来的美名必归于他个人。然而，当真是他解开的吗？回想起来，他不得不承认，每一分功劳都得记在那姑娘名下。是她提议了通向成功的那些步骤——是她，显然早已猜中了那个直到此刻仍与他捉迷藏的谜底。

===Original===
In about an hour and a half they reached their destination, and Jimmy, who knew the district from his visits to the Drews, saw with satisfaction that the point was where the Halford-London road crossed that which passed over the river bridge above the Cranshaw Falls. From there to the Old Ferry was about ten minutes’ smart walk, and, if the taxi-man’s statement was correct, Cosgrove could therefore have reached Austin’s boat about 9:25. It would take him ten minutes or more to row to the Luce Manor boathouse, so that he would arrive there, say, between 9:30 and 9:40. This was quite sufficiently in accordance with the statement of Lucy Penrose and young Potts that they had seen the boat arriving about half-past nine. Jimmy recognised delightedly that the whole thing was working in.

===Chinese===
约莫一个半钟头后，他们到了目的地。吉米因常去德鲁家做客，认得这一带，遂满意地看出，这个路口正是哈尔福德—伦敦干道与另一条道路相交之处——后一条路经由克兰肖瀑布上游的河桥。从那里到老渡口，快步走约十分钟；倘若司机所言不虚，科斯格罗夫当能在 9:25 前后赶到奥斯汀的船边。再划十分钟或十分钟出头，到卢斯庄园的船屋，那么他到达的时刻，姑且算作 9:30 到 9:40 之间。这与露西·彭罗斯和小波茨的陈述——他们说看见那条船在九点半前后到达——相当吻合。吉米欣喜地意识到，整件事正一环扣一环地对上榫。

===Original===
It was still about quarter to twelve, and Jimmy had the taxi run slowly on to the Old Ferry lane. Lois was already there, and he lost no time in putting her in possession of the facts.

===Chinese===
眼下还只十二点差一刻，吉米让出租汽车缓缓开到老渡口小道的路口。洛伊丝已在等候，他立即把一应事实讲给了她。

===Original===
“You guessed that this happened?” he queried.

===Chinese===
「你料到会有这一出了？」他问。

===Original===
“Yes. I suddenly thought of it in bed, the night before I came up to see you.”

===Chinese===
「料到了。来看你的前一晚，我躺在床上突然想起了这一层。」

===Original===
“But I can’t make head nor tail of it,” Jimmy confessed. “Now it seems that Cosgrove must have been at the boathouse between half-past nine and ten, and yet he caught the 10:30 from King’s Cross, where he couldn’t have arrived till at least 11:30. The thing’s an absolute puzzle to me. Can you see light?”

===Chinese===
「可我实在摸不着头脑，」吉米坦白道。「现在看来，九点半到十点之间科斯格罗夫必在船屋，可他又赶上了国王十字 10:30 的车——而国王十字，他怎么也要 11:30 以后才到得了。这事对我来说是个十足的谜。你能看出门道来吗？」

===Original===
“Of course. He never caught the 10:30 at all.”

===Chinese===
「当然。他压根儿就没赶上 10:30 那班车。」

===Original===
“But, my dear girl, he did﻿—he must have. You forget the porter at Grantham and the dealer at Montrose.”

===Chinese===
「可是亲爱的姑娘，他赶上了——必定赶上了。你忘了格兰瑟姆的搬运工和蒙特罗斯的马贩子。」

===Original===
“Not at all. He travelled no doubt by the 10:30 from Grantham to Montrose. But that’s a very different thing. I’ll tell you, Jimmy, what’s puzzling you. You haven’t studied your Great Northern time table as I have. The 10:30 is not the only train in the day from London.”
