===Chinese===
「一点没忘。从格兰瑟姆到蒙特罗斯，他无疑坐的正是 10:30 那班。可那是两码事。吉米，我来告诉你你的谜结在哪儿。你不曾像我这样，把大北方铁路（Great Northern）的时刻表研习透彻。一天之内从伦敦发出的车，可不是只有 10:30 那一班。」

===Original===
Daunt waited.

===Chinese===
多恩特等着她说下去。

===Original===
“Well?” he said impatiently.

===Chinese===
「怎么样？」他不耐烦地说。

===Original===
“Before that 10:30, as you might have known from Mr. Tanner’s story of his own movements, there runs a pickup train. It leaves King’s Cross at 10:00 and reaches Grantham at 12:28, ten minutes before the 10:30. And that train, Jimmy, stops at three or four stations. It stops”﻿—she leant forward and whispered in his ear with an air of triumph﻿—“*it stops at Hitchin at 10:45!*”

===Chinese===
「在 10:30 那班之前，还开着一班沿途收客的慢车——这一点，你本可以从坦纳先生讲他自己行程的话里推知的。它 10:00 从国王十字发车，12:28 到格兰瑟姆，比 10:30 那班早十分钟。而那班车，吉米，沿途要停三四个站。它停——」她探身向前，带着胜利的神气凑到他耳边低声道——「*它 10:45 停靠希钦！*」

===Original===
“And Hitchin is only six miles from here! Good Heavens, how stupid not to have seen that! Of course that’s what he did! After the murder he motored to Hitchin and caught the relief train. Well, Lois, you deserve all you’re going to get for thinking of it!”

===Chinese===
「而希钦离这儿只有六英里！天哪，我竟没看出这一点，多蠢哪！他当然就是这么干的！行凶之后，他坐汽车赶到希钦，搭上了那班接应的慢车。好啊，洛伊丝，想得出这一层，日后有什么报偿，你都受之无愧！」

===Original===
“But we’re not quite out of the wood yet,” the girl reminded him. “We have to find out how he went from here to Hitchin.”

===Chinese===
「可我们还没全然脱险呢，」姑娘提醒他。「还得查明，他从这儿到希钦是怎么走的。」

===Original===
“Probably by the taxi,” suggested Daunt, and they returned to where the vehicle was waiting.

===Chinese===
「多半还是那辆出租汽车，」多恩特揣测道。两人回到车子等候的地方。

===Original===
But his guess was incorrect. The driver assured them that on reaching the crossroads Cosgrove had paid him off and he had returned at once to London.

===Chinese===
然而他猜错了。司机一口咬定：到了十字路口，科斯格罗夫付清车钱打发了他，他便当即折回伦敦去了。

===Original===
“I thought of that first,” said Lois, “then I thought not, that he would never have let any one man have so much information about his movements. Then I wondered if he wouldn’t have arranged for a vehicle from Halford to pick him up, but I saw that wouldn’t do either. At last I thought the most obvious and indeed the least suspicious plan would be to engage a car in Hitchin to run out for him. What do you think?”

===Chinese===
「这个我先想到过，」洛伊丝说，「跟着又想不对——他绝不会让单独一个人摸到他行踪的这么多底。后来我又琢磨，他会不会从哈尔福德约好一辆车来接，可我看这也不成。末了我想，最不惹眼、也确实最无可怀疑的法子，就是在希钦雇好一辆汽车，让它开出城来接他。你觉得呢？」

===Original===
“I believe you are right. Let’s run over to Hitchin now and make inquiries.”

===Chinese===
「我相信你说得对。咱们这就赶去希钦打听。」

===Original===
They reached the town in about quarter of an hour. There they paid off their taxi, having noted the man’s name and address. Then after a hurried lunch they got to work.

===Chinese===
约莫一刻钟后，他们到了希钦镇。他们在那里付了车钱，记下了司机的姓名住址，匆匆吃过午饭，便动手查访。

===Original===
At the first and second garages they drew blank, but at the third they had success. It appeared that late on the Monday evening before the murder, a man, whom the proprietor instantly identified from the photograph as Cosgrove, called and asked for a car to be sent to the same crossroads near Luce Manor. It was to be there on the following Wednesday evening at 10:15, and was to wait for him and run him into Hitchin. He gave no explanation of his movements, but he turned up at the place a few minutes after the hour named, and was duly brought to Hitchin and set down near the George Hotel. Daunt had the driver sent for, and he stated he had seen his fare’s face in the light of a street lamp when he was being paid, and he also unhesitatingly selected Cosgrove’s photograph as that of the man.

===Chinese===
头两家车行都扑了空，第三家却有了收获。据称，案发前的那个星期一晚上，夜深时分，有位客人来访——车行老板一眼就从相片里认出是科斯格罗夫——要一辆车开到卢斯庄园近旁同一个十字路口去，在次日即星期三晚上 10:15 到达那里，候着他，再把他载回希钦。他对自己的行踪未作任何解释，不过在约定的钟点过后几分钟，他果然在约定地点现身，被如约载回希钦，在「乔治」旅馆附近下了车。多恩特叫人把那位司机找来，司机说付钱时他曾借路灯看清了乘客的脸，也毫不迟疑地选中了科斯格罗夫的相片，认定就是此人。

===Original===
“How far is it from the George Hotel to the station?” asked Daunt.

===Chinese===
「从『乔治』旅馆到车站有多远？」多恩特问。

===Original===
“Three minutes’ walk, sir.”

===Chinese===
「步行三分钟，先生。」

===Original===
Here at last was proof﻿—utter and final proof. As the cousins left the garage Jimmy once more congratulated his companion on her success.

===Chinese===
到这里，证据终于到手了——彻底的、一锤定音的证据。表兄妹俩走出车行时，吉米再次向同伴道贺，庆她的成功。

===Original===
“But we’re not finished yet, Jimmy,” she answered him. “We have to find the woman﻿—the false servant.”

===Chinese===
「可我们还没大功告成呢，吉米，」她答道。「我们还得找出那个女人——那个假女仆。」

===Original===
“You think it couldn’t have been Cosgrove?”

===Chinese===
「你觉得那不可能是科斯格罗夫干的？」

===Original===
Lois shook her head.

===Chinese===
洛伊丝摇了摇头。

===Original===
“He could never have deceived Austin. Besides, the hours don’t work. Cosgrove could hardly have reached the Old Ferry till after Austin had left. In any case Cosgrove would never have had time to make up so well.”

===Chinese===
「他绝骗不过奥斯汀。再说，钟点也对不上。科斯格罗夫怎么也要等奥斯汀离开之后，才到得了老渡口。无论如何，他也来不及妆扮得那么像。」

===Original===
“I agree with you. Then our next job is, cherchez la femme. Have you any ideas as to how we should start?”

===Chinese===
「我同意。那么咱们的下一桩差事，就是找出那个女人（cherchez la femme）。从哪儿下手，你有主意吗？」

===Original===
“I don’t know if you will agree with me,” Lois answered slowly, “but I wonder if we should not take Mr. Tanner into our confidence. He has been very straight and very kind all through, and I’m sure if he knew what we have learned he would take over the finding of the woman.”

===Chinese===
「不知你赞不赞成，」洛伊丝缓缓答道，「可我在想，咱们是不是该把事情向坦纳先生和盘托出。他自始至终光明磊落、待我们极好；我确信，他一旦知道我们查到的一切，追查那女人的事他就会接过去。」

===Original===
“By Jove, Lois, I believe you are right in this as in everything else. I’ll go and see him now. Would you care to come too?”

===Chinese===
「老天，洛伊丝，这件事同其余每件事一样，我相信你都说对了。我这就去见他。你要不要同去？」

===Original===
“No. I think you could do that best alone. I’ll come to town with you and hear your report.”

===Chinese===
「不去。这事你独自去办最好。我陪你进城，听你的回报。」

===Original===
They went up by the Great Northern, and Daunt drove to Scotland Yard. Inspector Tanner was out.

===Chinese===
两人由大北方铁路进了城，多恩特驱车前往苏格兰场。坦纳探长外出了。

===Original===
“I am the solicitor who is acting for Mr. Austin Ponson﻿—you know, the Halford murder﻿—and I bring some very material information about the case. I should like to see someone in authority.”

===Chinese===
「我是奥斯汀·庞森先生聘任的律师——您知道，就是哈尔福德那桩谋杀案——我带来了一些对案情至关重要的消息。我想见一位主事的长官。」

===Original===
He was asked to wait and presently was ushered into the presence of Chief Inspector Edgar. This official had followed the case with Tanner, and he heard Daunt’s story with thinly veiled amazement.

===Chinese===
人家请他稍候，不多时便被引到埃德加总探长（Chief Inspector Edgar）面前。这位长官一直同坦纳一并跟进此案，听多恩特讲罢来龙去脉，那份惊诧几乎掩藏不住。

===Original===
“It’s the most extraordinary case I have come across for many a year,” he exclaimed. “That makes two suspects, and Tanner’s off to Portugal after a third.”

===Chinese===
「这是多年来我经手的最离奇的案子，」他叫道。「这一来已有两名嫌疑犯了，坦纳又跑去葡萄牙追第三个。」

===Original===
“Good Lord! To Portugal?”

===Chinese===
「我的天！去葡萄牙？」

===Original===
“Yes, with an extradition warrant and all complete. Well, Mr. Daunt, I needn’t say how grateful we are to you and Miss Drew for what you have done, and you may count on your information receiving the fullest and most careful attention. When Tanner gets back, perhaps you wouldn’t mind calling in and having a chat over the matter with him?”

===Chinese===
「是啊，带着引渡令，一切都齐备了。多恩特先生，不必说您和德鲁小姐所做的一切让我们多么感激。您尽可放心，您带来的消息必将得到最充分、最审慎的对待。等坦纳回来，可否劳驾您再来一趟，同他把这事谈一谈？」

===Original===
When Jimmy returned to Lois and told her of the Portuguese expedition she was as utterly amazed as he was. But there was no way of satisfying their curiosity, and they had unwillingly to content themselves to wait till Tanner’s return.

===Chinese===
吉米回到洛伊丝身边，把葡萄牙之行一说，她惊得目瞪口呆，不亚于他。可这份好奇心无从满足，两人只得按捺着，等坦纳回来再见分晓。

## XI · A Fresh Start / XI · 崭新的开端

===Original===
While Lois Drew and her cousin, James Daunt, were pursuing their researches into the movements of Cosgrove Ponson, Inspector Tanner had been far from idle. We may retrace our steps to the day of Austin’s arrest, and follow the detective as he endeavours to complete his case against the accused.

===Chinese===
当洛伊丝·德鲁和表哥詹姆斯·多恩特还在追查科斯格罗夫·庞森的行踪时，坦纳探长也一直没有闲着。让我们回溯到奥斯汀被捕的那一天，跟着这位侦探，看他如何设法把加在被告身上的案由办得周全。

===Original===
Having reached London with his prisoner, and handed him over to the proper authorities, Tanner returned to the Yard and set to work on a statement of the evidence he was prepared to supply to the Crown Prosecutor.

===Chinese===
坦纳把押解的犯人带到伦敦，移交给有关当局之后，便回到苏格兰场，着手起草一份证据陈述，准备提供给控方检察官（Crown Prosecutor）。

===Original===
But the more he considered this evidence, the less satisfied he became with it. Tanner was an ambitious as well as a naturally efficient man, and he hated giving over a case which was not complete in every detail. Here, though the facts he had learned undoubtedly made a powerful arraignment of Austin, they just stopped short of being conclusive. Always there was the possibility of a plant, with the accused as the innocent victim. At all events Tanner was sure a defence on these lines would be attempted, and he was not quite certain that he could meet it.

===Chinese===
可这份证据，他越琢磨越不满意。坦纳其人，既有天生的干才，又有勃勃的野心，最恨把一桩细节上还不完备的案子交出去。就本案而论，他查明的种种事实固然足以对奥斯汀构成一纸有力的控状，却偏偏差着一口气，算不上板上钉钉。栽赃的可能始终存在，被告兴许只是个无辜的受害人。无论如何，坦纳确信辩方必定会循着这条路子抗辩，而他并无十分把握能够应付。

===Original===
Another difficulty was his failure to discover the maker of the fifth set of tracks on the river bank. Until this man was identified, and his business there known, the affair would remain unsatisfactory.

===Chinese===
另一桩难处，是他始终没能查出河岸上第五趟脚印是谁留下的。这个人一日不明、来意一日不清，这案子在他眼里就一日不够圆满。

===Original===
Tanner determined he could not rest on his oars, but must continue his inquiries in the hope of making his case overwhelming.

===Chinese===
坦纳打定主意，此案决不能就此歇桨，必须继续查访，务求把案由办得无可辩驳。

===Original===
He recalled the fact that he had intended to follow up Sir William Ponson’s visits to London in the hope of finding that the latter’s business in town had some connection with his death. His attention had been diverted into other channels by the unexpected information given by Lucy Penrose and young Potts of Austin’s movements on the night of the murder. But now his mind reverted to the point, and he decided it remained his most promising clue. Without loss of time, therefore, he began to work on it.

===Chinese===
他想起自己原打算追查威廉·庞森爵士生前进伦敦的每一趟行程，指望查出他进城办的事同他的死有某种干系。后来露西·彭罗斯和小波茨意外提供了奥斯汀案发当晚的行踪，他的注意力才被引上了别的道。可如今他的心思又回到这一点上，并且认定这仍是最有希望的线索。于是他一刻也不耽搁，动手查了起来。

===Original===
He remembered that he had already learnt the trains by which the murdered man had travelled to town. He could thus start with the practical certainty that Sir William had arrived at St. Pancras at 11:40 on the Saturday and the Monday before the crime.

===Chinese===
他记得，死者生前进城搭乘的几班火车已经查明了。因此他差不多可以断定：案发前的那个星期六和星期一，威廉爵士都是 11:40 抵达圣潘克拉斯的。

===Original===
By what means would the deceased leave the station? Tanner did not think a man of his position would walk or go by bus or tube. No, as his private car was not available he would take a taxi. “I must find the man who drove him,” the Inspector thought.

===Chinese===
死者出站之后，会怎样代步？坦纳想，像他这样身份的人，断不至于步行，也不会去乘公共汽车或地铁。不，自备汽车既然用不上，他就会坐出租汽车。「我得找出载过他的那个司机。」探长心想。

===Original===
He gave his bell a code ring, and instructed the assistant who answered to undertake the inquiry. From the constables on station duty the numbers of the vehicles which left on the arrival of the train in question could be obtained, and it would be a simple matter to find the drivers and learn by means of a photograph which of them had driven Sir William, and to what point.

===Chinese===
他打了个暗号铃，吩咐应铃而来的助手去承办这项查访。从车站值勤的警员那里，可以查到那班列车进站后驶离车站的各车号码；循着号码找到司机，拿相片一认，便不难查明他们当中是谁载过威廉爵士，又送到了什么地方。

===Original===
But Tanner was by no means sanguine that such an inquiry would bear fruit. He believed that the deceased had not used his own car because he wished to cover his traces. And if so he would probably have avoided taking a taxi at the station. It would have been safer for him to have picked one up in the street outside. Tanner therefore felt he should if possible have another string to his bow. Where could such be found?

===Chinese===
不过，这番查访能不能结出果来，坦纳实在不敢乐观。他认定死者那天不用自备汽车，正是为了隐匿行踪。倘若如此，他多半也不会在车站雇车——到站外的街面上另叫一辆，岂不更稳妥。坦纳因此觉得，最好还得另备一条弓弦。可这样的弦，又上哪儿找去？

===Original===
A second line of inquiry soon suggested itself. Sir William would not have passed the day without food. If Tanner could find where he lunched, it would give him another point of attack.

===Chinese===
很快，第二条查访的路子自己浮上心头。威廉爵士总不至于一整天不进饮食。坦纳若能查明他那天在哪儿用的午饭，便又多了一处下手的地方。

===Original===
The Inspector had learned from Innes his master’s usual restaurants, as well as the names of his two clubs. As all these were extremely expensive and exclusive, Tanner felt he might confine his researches to places of the same type.

===Chinese===
探长早从英尼斯口中问出了主人平素光顾的几家餐馆，还有他那两家俱乐部的名字。既然这些去处无一不是花销高昂、门槛森严，坦纳觉得，查访尽可以只限于同一类地方。

===Original===
He began at once. Driving to the first club, he made exhaustive inquiries. Sir William was a well-known figure there, and his death had caused some of the attendants to recall in conversation the occasion of his last visit. But this had been three weeks before the murder. The men were positive he had not been there either the Saturday or Monday in question.

===Chinese===
他立刻动手。驱车来到第一家俱乐部，作了穷根究底的查问。威廉爵士在那里是个知名人物，他死后，有些侍者闲谈中还谈起过他最后一次到访的情形。可那已是案发前三个星期的事了。那几个人一口咬定，无论所说的星期六还是星期一，他都没有来过。

===Original===
At the second club Tanner received similar information. Here Sir William had not been seen for over two months prior to his death.

===Chinese===
在第二家俱乐部，坦纳得到的是同样的消息。这一家，威廉爵士死前两个多月就没人见过了。

===Original===
The Inspector then began on the restaurants. By the time he had visited the Carlton, the Savoy, and one or two others it was after eight o’clock. He therefore gave up for the night and, going home, busied himself in making out a list of other possible places at which he would inquire on the following day.

===Chinese===
探长接着开始查餐馆。卡尔顿（Carlton）、萨伏伊，再加另外一两家跑下来，已过了八点钟。他当晚就此收工，回家忙着开列一张单子，把第二天要去查问的其他可能去处逐一记上。

===Original===
Next morning he was early at work. He was very thorough and painstaking, leaving no restaurant till he had interviewed everyone who might conceivably help him, from the manager down to the cloakroom attendant. For a long time he had no luck. But at last in the late afternoon, when he had worked half down his list and visited no less than seventeen restaurants, he found what he wanted.

===Chinese===
第二天一早他又上工了。他办得极其周密扎实，每家餐馆，不把一切可能帮上忙的人问遍——从经理直到衣帽间侍者——决不离开。好半天他都运气不佳。直到下午较晚时分，单子查过了一半，跑过的餐馆已不下十七家，他才找到了要找的东西。

===Original===
It was a small but expensive French place on the border of Soho, with an unobtrusive exterior, and a quiet, excellent service﻿—a place frequented by a well-to-do but, Tanner somehow imagined, rather disreputable clientéle. Here Sir William’s photograph received instant recognition.

===Chinese===
那是索霍区（Soho）边上的一家法国馆子，地方不大，价钱却贵，门面毫不招摇，服务安静而周到——光顾的主顾个个手头殷实，只是——也不知怎么，坦纳总觉得——名声不大干净。在这里，威廉爵士的相片一拿出来就被认了出来。

===Original===
“But yes, monsieur,” the polite manager assured him. “I remember this gentleman distinctly. He come here﻿—let me see﻿—about three weeks ago, I think. He come early and he ask for me. He wish a private room and lunch for three. Presently two other gentlemen join him. They lunch. After coffee he give orders that they be not disturbed. They stay there for ver’ long time. Then they leave and this gentleman”﻿—the manager tapped the photograph﻿—“he pay for all.”

===Chinese===
「可不是嘛，先生，」彬彬有礼的经理向他担保。「这位先生我记得清清楚楚。他来这儿——让我想想——大概是三个礼拜以前，我想。他来得早，一来了就找我。他要一个包间，三个人的午饭。过不多时，另外两位先生来同他会齐。他们用午饭。用过咖啡，他吩咐下去，不许有人去搅扰。他们在里头待了很长很长的时间。后来他们走了，这一位」——经理点了点相片——「他把账全付了。」

===Original===
“Can you tell me what day that was?”

===Chinese===
「您能告诉我是哪一天吗？」

===Original===
By looking up his records of the hire of the room the manager could. It was Monday the 5th July. Further inquiries elicited the information that Sir William had reached the restaurant about twelve, and had remained till three, when he left with his friends.

===Chinese===
经理翻出包间租用的账簿，还真查了出来。那是 7 月 5 日星期一。进一步打听又得知，威廉爵士十二点前后到的馆子，一直待到三点，才同他的朋友们一同离去。

===Original===
“Together?” asked Tanner.

===Chinese===
「一道走的？」坦纳问。

===Original===
“At the same time, monsieur, yes; but not in company. The old gentleman”﻿—again the manager indicated the photograph﻿—“he drive off in a taxi. The other two walk.”

===Chinese===
「同一个时间，是的，先生；可不是结伴。那位老先生」——经理又指了指相片——「他坐上一辆出租汽车走了。另外两位是步行。」

===Original===
“Now those other two. Would you kindly describe them.”

===Chinese===
「再说说那另外两位。可否请您把他们的模样讲一讲。」

===Original===
As he listened to the manager’s reply, the Inspector got a sudden idea. He took from his pocket the half-dozen photographs he had used when tracing Cosgrove’s movements, and asked the other if the two friends were among them. The manager glanced over them, then bowed and smiled.

===Chinese===
探长一面听经理描述，心里忽然闪过一个念头。他从口袋里取出追查科斯格罗夫行踪时用过的那半打相片，问对方那两位朋友可在其列。经理扫了几眼，随即躬身微笑。

===Original===
“These are the gentlemen,” he declared, picking out those of Austin and Cosgrove.

===Chinese===
「就是这两位先生。」他说着，把奥斯汀和科斯格罗夫的两张挑了出来。

===Original===
Inspector Tanner was greatly surprised at the news. What, he wondered, could have been the business between these three, which was so secret that it could only be discussed in a private room of a somewhat shady foreign restaurant in Soho? Something dark and sinister, he feared. It was evident that all three had desired to keep the meeting a secret. Sir William had taken steps to cover his traces on the journey, and so probably had the other two. At least if they had not, they had practically denied being there. Both Austin and Cosgrove had stated explicitly that they had not seen Sir William on the day in question. Further, it must have been complicated business. Austin had been alone with his father for at least two hours on the previous evening﻿—Tanner recollected that after dinner at Luce Manor on the Sunday the men had not joined the ladies in the drawing-room﻿—and here, the very next day, an interview of three hours had been necessary. Or say two hours, excluding lunch. A lot of business could be put through in two hours. Tanner began to fear the whole affair was deeper and more complicated than he had at first supposed.

===Chinese===
坦纳探长听到这个消息，大为吃惊。这三个人之间究竟有什么勾当，竟机密到只能躲进索霍区一家不大干净的外国馆子的包间里去商量？只怕是阴晦见不得人的事。显而易见，三方面都想瞒住这次会面。威廉爵士在路上已着意掩去行踪，那两位多半也一样。退一步说，就算没有，他们也实际上等于否认到过那里——奥斯汀和科斯格罗夫都曾明确声明，案发当天没有见过威廉爵士。再者，这桩勾当想必还不简单。头一天晚上，奥斯汀已同他父亲单独相处了至少两个钟头——坦纳记得，那个星期日在卢斯庄园吃过晚饭后，男客们没有到客厅去陪女眷——可第二天，竟还须密谈三个钟头。就算除去午饭，只算两个钟头吧。两个钟头能办成多少事啊。坦纳开始担心，这整桩案子比他起初设想的更深、更缠杂。

===Original===
He questioned all of the staff who had come in contact with the three men, without result, until he came to the restaurant porter who had called the taxi. Here he had more luck than is usual in such inquiries. The taxi had been taken from a neighbouring rank, and the porter recollected the driver, whom he had called several times previously. He was an elderly, wizened man, clean-shaven and with white hair.

===Chinese===
他把同那三位有过接触的店员挨个问了一遍，一无所获，直到问起替那趟车叫车的餐馆门房，才交了好运——这样的查访里算是难得的运气。车是从近旁一个候客点叫的，门房还记得那位司机，先前已叫他过好几回车。那是位上了年纪的干瘦汉子，脸刮得精光，头发全白了。

===Original===
After slipping a coin into the porter’s eager hand, Tanner walked to the rank. The driver of the third car answered the description. Tanner accosted him civilly, explaining who he was.

===Chinese===
坦纳把一枚硬币塞进门房巴望着的手心，便朝候客点走去。第三辆车的那位司机，正合描述的模样。坦纳客客气气地上前搭话，说明了自己是谁。

===Original===
“On Monday the 5th instant, just three weeks ago,” he went on, “about three in the afternoon, you were hailed by the porter at the Étoile over there. Your fare was this gentleman”﻿—he showed Sir William’s photograph. “Do you remember it?”

===Chinese===
「本月 5 日星期一，也就是整整三个星期以前，」他接着说，「下午三点前后，那边『埃托瓦尔』（the Étoile）的门房招呼过你。你载的客人就是这位先生」——他出示威廉爵士的相片。「记得起来吗？」

===Original===
The man looked at the card.

===Chinese===
那人看了看相片。

===Original===
“Why yes, sir, I remembers ’im all right.”

===Chinese===
「呃，记得，先生，这人我记得真真的。」

===Original===
“Where did you drive him to?”

===Chinese===
「你把他拉到哪儿去了？」

===Original===
“I’m blessed if I can remember the name, sir,” the driver answered slowly, “It was to a little narrow street back of Gower Street. I ’adn’t ever been there before. The old gent, ’e directs me there, and tells me to set ’im down at the corner, and so I does. ’E was standing there when I saw ’im last.”

===Chinese===
「那个地名，先生，您就是打死我，我也想不起来了，」司机慢吞吞地答道。「是高尔街（Gower Street）背后一条又窄又小的街。那地方我从前压根儿没去过。老先生，他一路指点着我，叫我把他撂在街口，我就照办了。我末了瞧见他，他就站在那儿。」

===Original===
“Could you find the place again?”

===Chinese===
「那地方你还能再找着吗？」

===Original===
“I believe I could, sir.”

===Chinese===
「我想我找得着，先生。」

===Original===
“Then drive me there,” said Tanner, entering the vehicle.

===Chinese===
「那就拉我去。」坦纳说着，上了车。

===Original===
The district they reached was a miserable, decaying part of town. The streets were narrow﻿—mere lanes, and the buildings high and unusually drab and grimy even for a London backwater. The houses had been good at one time, but the place had now degenerated into a slum. “What in the name of wonder,” thought Tanner, as he stood looking round at the depressing prospect, “could have brought Sir William here?”

===Chinese===
他们到的那片地方，是城里一处穷困破败的地界。街道窄得很——不过是些小巷；房屋高耸，即便按伦敦僻壤的标准，也灰暗腌臜得出奇。这些宅子早年想必也曾体面，如今整片地方已沦为贫民窟。「到底是什么蹊跷事，」坦纳站在那里，环顾着四周叫人丧气的景象，暗自想道，「会把威廉爵士引到这种地方来？」

===Original===
He paid off his driver and began to investigate his surroundings. He was at a crossroads, the broader street being labelled Dunlop Street, the other Pate’s Lane. In Dunlop Street were a few shops﻿—a bar at the corner, a tobacconist’s, a grocer’s﻿—all small, mean, and dirty. Pate’s Lane appeared entirely given up to tenement houses.

===Chinese===
他付钱打发了司机，开始察看四周的环境。他正站在一个十字路口，宽些的那条街标着邓禄普街（Dunlop Street），另一条标着佩茨巷（Pate’s Lane）。邓禄普街上有几家铺子——街角一家酒馆，一家烟店，一家杂货铺——全都又小又寒碜又肮脏。佩茨巷看上去则整条巷子都是分间出租的住屋。

===Original===
Tanner felt utterly at a loss. He could form no conception of Sir William’s possible objective. Nor could he envisage any line of inquiry which might lead him to his goal. He seemed to be up against a blank wall, through which he could see no means of penetrating.

===Chinese===
坦纳全然摸不着头脑。威廉爵士到这里来可能图什么，他想象不出；哪一条查访的路子能引他达到目的，他也设想不来。他仿佛撞上了一堵死墙，看不出任何穿透过去的法子。

===Original===
He wondered if a former servant or mill worker might not live in the neighbourhood, with whom the manufacturer might have had business. But if so, and if by some incredible chance Tanner were to hit on the person in question, he felt he would be no further on, and that all knowledge of Sir William’s visit would almost certainly be concealed.

===Chinese===
他揣想，这一带会不会住着一位从前的仆人或者厂里的老工人，同这位厂主有过什么往来。可就算真有，就算坦纳凭着某种难以置信的运气撞见了正主，他自觉也不会因此前进一步——有关威廉爵士此行的一切，几乎必定会被瞒得严严实实。

===Original===
However, he would learn nothing by standing in the street, and he walked to the bar at the nearest corner and entered.

===Chinese===
不过，站在街心里终究问不出什么，他便走到最近的街角那家酒馆，推门进去了。

===Original===
The landlord was a big, red-faced man with a bluff manner. Tanner, after ordering some ale, engaged him in conversation, deftly pumping him. But he learned nothing. The man had not seen Sir William, nor did the manufacturer’s name convey anything to him.

===Chinese===
店主人是个红脸膛的大个子，一副爽直的做派。坦纳要了些麦酒，同他攀谈起来，不着痕迹地套他的话。结果一无所获。那人没见过威廉爵士，厂主的名字在他听来也不意味着什么。

===Original===
Tanner tried each of the other three corner shops of the crossroads, but again without result. Then, thinking that small tobacconists and newsagents sometimes act as mediums between persons who do not wish their connection to be known, he called at all the shops of these kinds in the immediate neighbourhood. Again he was disappointed, as he was also when he visited the pawnbroker’s. By this time it was getting late, and he turned his steps homeward, intending to return on the next morning and begin a house-to-house visitation in the vicinity of the cross streets.

===Chinese===
十字路口其余三家角店，坦纳也都一一试过，仍无结果。转念又想，小烟店、报刊店之类，有时会给那些不愿暴露彼此关系的人充当传话的中人，于是他把左近一带这类铺子挨门走了一遍。又一次失望了，再跑当铺，还是一样。这时天色渐晚，他转身往回走，打算第二天早晨再来，在几条横街一带挨家挨户地访查。

===Original===
As he walked down the road towards Gower Street, he noticed three buildings which he thought looked more the kind of place for a rendezvous than any he had yet seen. Two were shabby and rather squalid looking restaurants, the third a building slightly larger and more pretentious than its neighbours. In faded letters it bore the legend “Judd’s Family & Commercial Hotel.” Tanner decided that, before beginning on the houses of Pate’s Lane, he would try these three.

===Chinese===
顺着大路朝高尔街走去时，他注意到三座房子，觉得比他见过的哪一处都更像约会的地点。两座是寒碜而颇显腌臜的餐馆，第三座比左邻右舍略大些、也稍气派些，褪了色的字迹标着「贾德家庭兼商务旅馆」（Judd’s Family & Commercial Hotel）几个字。坦纳决定，在着手查访佩茨巷的人家之前，先把这三处试一遍。

===Original===
Next morning he drew blank in the first of the restaurants. A visit to the second was equally fruitless. But when he reached the hotel he had a stroke of luck.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，头一家餐馆他扑了个空。第二家跑下来同样一无所获。可到了那家旅馆，他交上了好运。

===Original===
A rather untidy porter was polishing the brass bell-push, and Tanner engaged him in conversation. Yes, he remembered a well-dressed old gentleman calling about half-past three on that Monday, three weeks earlier. He would recognise him if he saw him. Yes, he was sure that was his photograph. The gentleman had asked was a Mr. Douglas staying in the hotel, and on being answered in the affirmative he had gone up to the latter’s bedroom and remained with him for about half an hour. Then he had left, and that was all the porter knew.

===Chinese===
一个颇不修边幅的门房正在擦拭黄铜门铃按钮，坦纳便同他搭上了话。是的，他记得三个星期前的那个星期一，三点半上下，有位穿着体面的老先生来过。再见面他也认得出。是的，他敢肯定，相片上就是那位。那位先生打听过旅馆里可住着一位道格拉斯（Douglas）先生，得到肯定的答复后，便上那人的卧室去了，同他待了约莫半个钟头。随后他就走了——门房知道的就这么多。

===Original===
“And what sort of a man was Mr. Douglas?” Tanner asked.

===Chinese===
「那么，道格拉斯先生是个什么样的人？」坦纳问。

===Original===
“A small man, very small and thin,” the porter returned. “Looked as ’ow a breath of wind would ’ave blown ’im away. Sort of scared too, I thought.”

===Chinese===
「小个子，小得很，瘦得很，」门房答道。「瞧那样子，一口气就能把他刮跑。我还觉得，他有点儿胆战心惊的。」

===Original===
Tanner pricked up his ears.

===Chinese===
坦纳竖起了耳朵。

===Original===
“What was he like in face?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「他长相如何？」他问。

===Original===
“ ’E was getting on in years﻿—maybe sixty or more, and ’e ’ad a small, grey goatee beard, and a moustache, and wore spectacles. ’E spoke like an American man and was a bit free with ’is langwidge, damning and cursing about everything.”

===Chinese===
「他上了年纪——兴许六十开外，留一撮灰白的小山羊胡，唇上两撇小胡子，戴一副眼镜。说话是美国人的腔调，嘴上不大干净，张口闭口咒天骂地，什么都骂。」

===Original===
At last! Was this the man for whom he and Sergeant Longwell had been searching﻿—the man who had made the fifth set of tracks at Luce Manor, and who had travelled from St. Albans to London on the morning after the crime? With thinly veiled eagerness Tanner continued his questions.

===Chinese===
终于有了眉目！这莫非就是他同朗威尔警佐一直在寻的那个人——在卢斯庄园留下第五趟脚印、案发次日清晨从圣奥尔本斯乘车来伦敦的那个人？坦纳按捺着几乎掩不住的急切，继续发问。

===Original===
“Who cleans the boots here?”

===Chinese===
「这儿擦靴子的活儿谁干？」

===Original===
The porter looked interested.

===Chinese===
门房露出了有兴趣的神色。

===Original===
“I do,” he replied, “and why that?”

===Chinese===
「我干，」他答道，「可问这做什么？」

===Original===
“Only this. Can you remember what sort of boots this man Douglas wore?”

===Chinese===
「就问这么一句。这个人道格拉斯穿的什么样的靴子，你还记得吗？」

===Original===
“Well, they were small, like himself. Small boots like you’d expect a boy to wear.”

===Chinese===
「呃，小小的，跟他人一样。那种小靴子，你会当是给半大孩子穿的。」

===Original===
“With nails in the soles?”

===Chinese===
「鞋底带钉？」

===Original===
“Just that. Guess, mister, you’re a ’tec?”

===Chinese===
「正是。我猜，先生，您是个侦探？」

===Original===
Tanner nodded.

===Chinese===
坦纳点了点头。

===Original===
“From Scotland Yard,” he answered. “I’m after that man Douglas, and if you can tell me anything about him, I’ll make it worth your while.”

===Chinese===
「苏格兰场的，」他答道。「我正在追查那个道格拉斯。你要能说出他的一星半点，亏待不了你。」

===Original===
The man whistled.

===Chinese===
那人吹了声口哨。

===Original===
“Gosh! but I just thought he was a wrong ’un. Wot’s ’e been up to, mister?”

===Chinese===
「乖乖！我还只当他不是个好东西呢。他干了什么坏事，先生？」

===Original===
“Never mind now. Why did you think he was a wrong ’un?”

===Chinese===
「这个先不忙说。你为什么觉得他不是好东西？」

===Original===
“W’y, ’e looked scared fit to die. ’E ’ad something worrying ’im, ’e ’ad.”

===Chinese===
「嘿，他吓得魂儿都要没了。他心里有桩事折磨着他，有桩事。」

===Original===
“All the time he was here?”

===Chinese===
「他住在这儿的时候，一直这样？」

===Original===
“No, the morning ’e left. ’E got ’is tea the night before a bit early﻿—about ’alf five or thereabouts﻿—and then went out, and ’e didn’t come back till seven o’clock the next morning; walks in at seven o’clock all shaking and grey about the face and calls for brandy. Swallowed two large brandies nearly neat, ’e did. They pulled ’im together some, and then ’e pays ’is bill and ’ooks it.”

===Chinese===
「不是，是他走的那个早上。头天晚上他茶点用得早了点儿——大概五点半前后——就出门了，直到第二天早上七点才回来；七点钟晃进大门，浑身发抖，脸色灰败，开口就要白兰地。两大杯几乎是纯的白兰地，他灌了下去。这才把他撑起来几分，随后他结了账，开溜了。」

===Original===
“Hooked it?”

===Chinese===
「开溜了？」

===Original===
“Yes, we never saw ’im no more after that.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，打那以后我们再没见过他。」

===Original===
“And do you remember what day that was?”

===Chinese===
「那是哪一天，你还记得吗？」

===Original===
“No, but I can get it for you at the office.”

===Chinese===
「记不得，可我上账房能给您查出来。」

===Original===
The porter vanished for a moment to a room at the back.

===Chinese===
门房转身进了后边一间屋，片刻即回。

===Original===
“ ’E left on Thursday morning, the 8th.”

===Chinese===
「他是星期四早上走的，8 号。」

===Original===
“Did you notice anything about his boots that morning?”

===Chinese===
「那天早上他的靴子，你可注意到什么？」

===Original===
“Yes. They were covered with mud﻿—just covered. You couldn’t but notice them. And the ends of ’is trousers too. ’E looked as if ’e ’ad been up to the knees in muddy water.”

===Chinese===
「注意到啦。靴子上全是泥——简直糊满了。你想看不见都难。还有他裤脚管也是。瞧那样子，像是在泥水里蹚到了膝盖那么深。」

===Original===
Tanner was as nearly excited as his dignity would allow. There could be little doubt, he felt, that this Douglas was indeed the man of whom he was in search. The man’s size﻿—a small man would take short steps﻿—the little, hobnailed boots, the wet and muddy trousers, the goatee beard﻿—these points considered cumulatively, made the evidence of identity almost overwhelming. And in addition he had been out all the night of the tragedy, and had returned in the morning shaken and clamouring for brandy. Enough evidence to hang a man, Tanner thought with satisfaction. He turned again to the porter.

===Chinese===
坦纳兴奋得几乎到了持重的身份所能容许的限度。他感到，这位道格拉斯八成确乎就是他要寻的人。那人的个头——小个子步子自然短——那双钉掌的小靴子，湿泥的裤脚，那撮山羊胡——这几条攒在一处合计，身份上的证据几乎压倒一切。何况案发当夜他整宿在外，清早回来时魂不附体，嚷着要白兰地。凭这些就够把人送上绞架了，坦纳满意地想道。他又转向门房。

===Original===
“What day did he come here first?”

===Chinese===
「他起初是哪天住进来的？」

===Original===
“I looked that up when I went to the office. It was on the Friday evening before.”

===Chinese===
「方才上账房，我把这个也查了。是前一个星期五的晚上。」

===Original===
“He left no address, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
「他没留什么地址吧，我猜？」

===Original===
“Not ’im,” said the porter with a wink.

===Chinese===
「他才不留呢。」门房说着挤了挤眼。

===Original===
“How did he go away? Did he get a cab?”

===Chinese===
「他怎么走的？叫车了吗？」

===Original===
“Keb?” returned the other disgustedly. “Not ’im. ’E took ’is bag in ’is hand and just ’ooked it on ’is blooming feet.”

===Chinese===
「叫车？」对方一脸嫌恶地应道。「他才不呢。他手里拎着提包，凭他自己那两条宝贝腿，走了个没影儿。」

===Original===
Tanner replied absently. He was thinking that a man who departed on foot from an hotel without leaving an address might not be so easy to trace. And the best description of his appearance he could get was too vague to be of much use. The porter had not noticed the colour of the man’s eyes, if there was any scar or mark on his face or hands, the shape of his ears, any peculiarity in his gait﻿—none of the matters on which identification depends. Tanner could only remind himself that a general hazy notion was better than no notion at all.

===Chinese===
坦纳心不在焉地应了一声。他心里想的是：一个人从旅馆步行离开，又不留地址，查起来怕不容易。而他能弄到手的关于此人相貌的最佳描述，又太含糊，派不上大用场。那人眼睛是什么颜色，脸上手上有没有疤痕印记，耳朵生得什么形状，走路有没有特异之处——凡是辨认身份所系的种种事项，门房一概没有留意。坦纳只好自我宽解：有个囫囵模糊的印象，总比全无线索强。

===Original===
He went to the office and saw the proprietor. The latter was a tall weedy individual, dilapidated looking as his own hotel. But he spoke civilly, and exerted himself to answer the Inspector’s questions, calling in various maids, and an untidy waiter for the latter’s interrogations. Unfortunately, none of them could tell anything Tanner had not already learned.

===Chinese===
他到账房见了老板。老板是个高挑干瘦的家伙，同他这家旅馆一样一副破败相。好在谈吐还算客气，又尽心竭力地回答探长的问话，还把几名女仆和一个邋遢侍者先后叫来受盘问。不巧的是，他们谁也说不出坦纳尚未掌握的半点情况。

===Original===
The man had registered “William Douglas, Fulham Street, Birmingham,” and with the proprietor’s permission Tanner cut out and pocketed the leaf. Then he asked to see the room the visitor had occupied.

===Chinese===
那人在登记簿上写的是「威廉·道格拉斯（William Douglas），富勒姆街（Fulham Street），伯明翰（Birmingham）」。经老板许可，坦纳把那一页裁了下来，揣进衣袋。随后他请人带他去看看那位客人住过的房间。

===Original===
It was a small apartment on the fourth floor, supplied with the minimum of cheap, rickety furniture. The bed was not made up, and dust lay thick everywhere.

===Chinese===
那是四楼上的一个小房间，陈设简到不能再简，只有几件廉价摇晃的破家具。床没有铺整，厚厚的积尘四处都是。

===Original===
“It hasn’t been occupied recently?”

===Chinese===
「近来没人住过？」

===Original===
“Not since Mr. Douglas had it,” the proprietor admitted.

===Chinese===
「道格拉斯先生走后就没人住过。」老板承认道。

===Original===
“I’ll just take a look round, if you don’t mind,” said Tanner. “Don’t let me keep you. I’ll follow you to the office in a minute or two.”

===Chinese===
「您不介意的话，我就在这儿四下看看。」坦纳说。「别让我绊住您。过一两分钟我上账房找您。」

===Original===
Left to himself Inspector Tanner began one of his careful, painstaking examinations. The entire contents of the room were minutely inspected. Every inch of the carpet and the cracks between the floor boards were examined in the hope of finding some small object which might have been dropped. The drawers of the small wardrobe, the bedclothes, the dressing-table and washstand, all were gone through with the utmost care, but with no result. At last to complete his task the searcher turned his attention to the fireplace.

===Chinese===
屋里只剩他一人之后，坦纳探长便开始了他那种细心周密的勘查。房间里的全部什物都一一检视过。地毯的每一寸、地板缝的每一道都查遍了，指望找到什么可能掉落的小物件。小衣橱的抽屉、铺盖、梳妆台和洗脸架，无不翻检得仔仔细细，却毫无所得。末了，为把事情办全，勘查人把注意力转向了壁炉。

===Original===
A broken Japanese fan was stuck in the old-fashioned grate, and only partially concealed a litter of matches, scraps of paper, bits of cord and other debris. Tanner lifted out the fan and began to go through the rubbish with the same scrupulous care. And then his perseverance was rewarded.

===Chinese===
旧式炉格里插着一把破日本折扇，半遮半掩地盖着一堆火柴、纸片、绳头和别的碎屑。坦纳取出扇子，以同样一丝不苟的仔细，动手翻检那堆废物。这一回，他的坚韧得了报偿。

===Original===
Among the papers he found the charred remains of an envelope which at once interested him. It was more than half burnt, a triangular portion with the stamp on one corner only remaining. As he picked it up it struck him it was of unusually good quality to find in a room of that description﻿—thick, cream-laid paper, which only a well-to-do person would have used. A few letters at the end of each line of the address remained visible. But at these he hardly glanced at first, his attention being riveted on the postmark. The letters were slightly blurred, but still he could read it clearly﻿—“**Halford 4 p.m. 2 Jy 20**.”

===Chinese===
在那堆纸片中，他发现了一只信封的焦残，顿时来了兴致。信封已烧掉大半，只剩一个小三角角，邮票还留在角上。他把它拿起来，这才觉出这纸好得出奇，与那样的房间全不相称——厚实的奶白色直纹纸，只有家境殷实的人才会用。地址每一行的行尾还留着几个字母。可起初他几乎没有看这些，注意力全叫邮戳攫住了。字迹略微晕开，但他仍能清清楚楚地读出——「**Halford 4 p.m. 2 Jy 20**」（即：哈尔福德，1920年7月2日下午4时）。

===Original===
As he looked at the large, firm calligraphy of the address, the suggestion this name conveyed to him was confirmed﻿—the envelope had been written by Sir William Ponson. He examined the mutilated address. It contained four lines, and from three to five letters remained at the end of each. It was clearly not that of the hotel, and it therefore might, if decipherable, lead to the discovery of the man in question.

===Chinese===
端详着地址上那手大而遒劲的字，这名字给他的暗示得到了印证——信封是威廉·庞森爵士亲笔写的。他细看那残缺的地址。一共四行，每行行尾留有三到五个字母。显然不是旅馆的地址，因而倘若能够辨识，或许就能顺藤摸到要找的那个人。

===Original===
Tanner put the bit of burnt paper away in his pocketbook, and continued his search. But he could find nothing else of interest, and presently he took his leave.

===Chinese===
坦纳把那片烧焦的纸收进记事本，继续搜检。但再没有找到什么值得注意的东西，不多时他便告辞了。

===Original===
From the nearest telephone call office he spoke to the chief of police at Birmingham, asking him to try to trace a William Douglas who lived in Fulham Street in that city, and he was not greatly surprised when that officer, after asking him to hold on for a moment, informed him that there was no street of that name in the town.

===Chinese===
他在最近的一处公用电话间里接通了伯明翰警察局长，请对方设法查找一个住在该市富勒姆街的威廉·道格拉斯。那位长官请他别挂断，稍候片刻，随后告知：本市根本没有这么一条街。他听了并不怎么意外。

===Original===
On reaching his office at the Yard the Inspector sat down at his desk, and taking the burnt fragment of the envelope from his pocket, set himself to try and puzzle out the address. The four lines ended in “﻿—﻿—﻿glas,” “﻿—﻿—﻿ttage,” “﻿—﻿—﻿rton,” and “﻿—﻿—﻿von” respectively. Of these, the “﻿—﻿—﻿glas” at the end of the first line seemed unquestionably to be part of the name “Douglas,” and the Inspector was therefore not without hope that this was the man’s real name.

===Chinese===
回到苏格兰场他的办公室，探长在桌前坐下，从口袋里取出信封的焦残，潜心去破解那个地址。四行的末尾分别是「———glas」「———ttage」「———rton」「———von」。其中第一行末尾的「———glas」，看来无可置疑是道格拉斯（Douglas）一名的一部分，探长于是不无希望地想，这或许就是那人的真名。

===Original===
Procuring another envelope of the same size, Tanner laid the charred fragment on the top, and endeavoured to estimate from the spacing of the “﻿—﻿—﻿glas,” what had preceded it. It was probable that, as there was no “Esq.,” the line had commenced with “Mr.” But “Mr.” alone, or even “Mr. W.” or “Mr. Wm.” would not fill the line. Tanner tried to write “Mr. William Dou﻿—” in Sir William’s hand, and, as this seemed exactly the size of the required space, he assumed this had been the first line.

===Chinese===
坦纳另寻来一只同样大小的信封，把焦残覆在上面，设法从「———glas」的间距推想它前面写的是什么。既然没有「Esq.」（先生）字样，这一行多半以「Mr.」开头。可单是一个「Mr.」，甚至「Mr. W.」或「Mr. Wm.」，都填不满整行。坦纳试着用威廉爵士的笔迹写「Mr. William Dou—」，写出来恰好占满所需的空当，于是他断定第一行便是如此。

===Original===
The “﻿—﻿—﻿ttage” at the end of the second line immediately suggested the word “Cottage.” Writing in the “Co﻿—” in the same manner as the “Mr. William Dou﻿—” he found there would be left before it space for a word of six or seven letters. Though his conclusions on this second line were admittedly only guesswork, he still felt fairly sure of his ground so far.

===Chinese===
第二行末尾的「———ttage」立刻使人想到「Cottage」（小屋）一词。他照「Mr. William Dou—」的办法补出「Co—」，发现它前面还留有能写一个六七个字母单词的空当。尽管他也承认，第二行上的结论不过是猜谜，但到目前为止，他自觉脚跟还站得相当稳。

===Original===
So many towns and villages ended with the letters “﻿—﻿—﻿rton,” Tanner felt it useless to work at the third line. He therefore transferred his attention to the fourth, merely noting that as the third did not end in “Street” or “Road,” the balance of probability was against the fourth line containing the name of a town. But this, of course, was by no means certain.

===Chinese===
以「———rton」收尾的城镇村庄多得数不清，坦纳觉得第三行一时无从下手。于是他把注意力转到第四行，只顺手记下一点：第三行既不以「Street」（街）也不以「Road」（路）收尾，那么第四行是城镇名的可能便较小。当然，这也远非定论。

===Original===
The fourth line ended in “﻿—﻿—﻿von,” and, considering the suggestion made by the third line, Tanner determined to begin, by assuming this was the name of a county. He got down an atlas and went over all the counties in the three kingdoms. Two only ended in “﻿—﻿—﻿von”﻿—Carnarvon and Devon. He tried spacing these in and found neither would suit. “Devon” was out of the question, as the *D* came after the *n* of the third line, and “Carnarvon,” which fitted better, was still obviously too short.

===Chinese===
第四行以「———von」收尾。考虑到第三行的提示，坦纳决定先从「这是个郡名」的假定入手。他取下一册地图集，把三个王国的所有郡名逐一过了一遍。以「———von」收尾的只有两个——卡那封（Carnarvon）与德文（Devon）。他把这两个词试着排进去，发现都不合式。「Devon」根本不可能，因为其中的 *D* 排在第三行那个 *n* 的后面；「Carnarvon」虽然合式些，却显然还是太短。

===Original===
He set to work then upon the towns. It was clear that as the name was the last in the address, the town must either be of considerable importance, or else lie close to Halford. Tanner chose the latter alternative first, and went over all the towns near Sir William’s residence. He could find none to fit.

===Chinese===
接着他着手查城镇。显而易见，既然这个名字排在地址的最末一行，那么这城镇要么相当要紧，要么就在哈尔福德左近。坦纳先按后一设想来查，把威廉爵士住处附近的城镇逐一过了一遍，一个也对不上。

===Original===
Sending for a Post Office Directory, in which towns with head offices﻿—and therefore important﻿—are printed in capitals, the Inspector laboriously ran his eye down the closely printed pages, searching for names in capital letters ending in “﻿—﻿—﻿von.” There were scores in “﻿—﻿—﻿ton,” some in “﻿—﻿—﻿don,” some in “﻿—﻿—﻿ven,” and “﻿—﻿—﻿van,” but he was amazed to find only two in “﻿—﻿—﻿von”﻿—“Carnarvon” and “Stratford-on-Avon.” Of these, while “Carnarvon,” as he had already found, seemed too short, “Stratford-on-Avon” was clearly a good deal too long.

===Chinese===
他又差人取来一册《邮局名录》（Post Office Directory）——凡设有邮政总局、因而地位重要的城镇，书中皆以大写字母印出——探长费眼费力地顺着印得密密麻麻的书页一路找下去，搜寻以大写字母排成、以「———von」收尾的地名。以「———ton」收尾的有几十个，「———don」「———ven」「———van」的也各有若干，可令他惊讶的是，以「———von」收尾的竟只有两个——「Carnarvon」和「Stratford-on-Avon」（埃文河畔斯特拉特福德）。二者之中，「Carnarvon」如前所见似乎太短，「Stratford-on-Avon」则显然长得太多。

===Original===
As he slowly pondered the matter, another idea occurred to him. “Devon,” he recollected, was rarely written alone, “North” or “South” was usually prefixed. To space out “North De﻿—” was the work of a moment. And then he felt more satisfied, for these letters seemed exactly to fill the required space.

===Chinese===
他缓缓思忖着，忽然又生出一计。他记起来，「Devon」很少单独书写，前面通常冠有「North」（北）或「South」（南）。把「North De—」排进去一试，不过是片刻的事。这一试他更觉满意，因为这几个字母似乎不多不少，恰好填满所需的空当。

===Original===
While he fully realised that the evidence was by no means conclusive﻿—in fact, was but slightly removed from a guess﻿—he thought the probabilities of the last word being “Devon” were such, that it would be worth while investigating on the basis of this assumption before trying any of the other names. The next question therefore became, What, if any, places in Devon ended in “﻿—﻿—﻿rton”?

===Chinese===
他心里全明白，这推断远非确凿——实际上同瞎猜只隔一层薄纸——但他认为，末一词是「Devon」的可能性既然不小，就不妨先循着这一假定查下去，再去试其余的名字。于是下一个问题便成了：德文郡内若有以「———rton」收尾的地名，都是哪些？

===Original===
He soon saw there were a number. Tiverton, Ashburton, Silverton, Merton, Halberton, Thorverton, Yelverton, Otterton, and Staverton he picked up at once from the atlas, and he felt sure there must be others, too small to be marked. His next step must therefore be to try if a small, elderly man with a grey beard, named William Douglas, lived in —﻿—﻿— Cottage, Tiverton, Ashburton, or one of the other places he had found.

===Chinese===
他很快发现确有好些。蒂弗顿（Tiverton）、阿什伯顿（Ashburton）、西尔顿（Silverton）、默顿（Merton）、哈尔伯顿（Halberton）、索弗顿（Thorverton）、耶尔弗顿（Yelverton）、奥特顿（Otterton）和斯塔弗顿（Staverton），这些他一眼便从地图上挑了出来；他还确信必有别的，只因太小而未标出。于是下一步只能去试：在蒂弗顿、阿什伯顿或他查出的其他某一地的「———小屋」，是否住着一位名叫威廉·道格拉斯的灰胡子小个子老人。

===Original===
He took a sheet of paper and drafted a letter to the chief of the local police at each of these places, asking him to forward the information with regard to his own neighbourhood.

===Chinese===
他取过一张纸，给上述每一地的当地警察局长各起草了一封信，请其通报各自乡里的有关情况。

===Original===
Two days later he received a wire from Yelverton. “William Douglas lives at Myrtle Cottage near Yelverton Station.”

===Chinese===
两天后，他收到耶尔弗顿拍来的一封电报：「威廉·道格拉斯住在耶尔弗顿车站附近的桃金娘小屋（Myrtle Cottage）。」

===Original===
Tanner chuckled. He was getting on more rapidly than he could have hoped.

===Chinese===
坦纳暗自笑出了声。进展之快，连他自己都不敢指望。

## XII · A Stern Chase / XII · 穷追不舍

===Original===
When the 10:30 a.m. Riviera express pulled out of Paddington next morning, Inspector Tanner was occupying a corner seat in one of its first-class compartments. In his pocket was a warrant for the arrest of William Douglas, in case his investigations should indicate that such a step was desirable. He had determined that if his victim could not account satisfactorily for his actions on the night of the murder, or if his boots fitted the marks on the Cranshaw River bank, no other course would be possible.

===Chinese===
第二天上午，当 10:30 的里维埃拉快车驶出帕丁顿（Paddington）车站时，坦纳探长正坐在一节头等车厢的角落席位上。他口袋里揣着一张逮捕威廉·道格拉斯的逮捕令，只等查访结果一旦表明此着可取，便可动手。他已拿定主意：倘若那人说不清自己案发当晚的行止，或者他的靴子与克兰肖河岸上的脚印吻合，那么除了逮捕，再无第二条路可走。

===Original===
Once again the Inspector was favoured with magnificent weather for his country ramble. Indeed, like the previous days, it was too hot, and as the train slipped swiftly through the sunbaked country, he moved into the corridor so as to make the most of the draught from the open windows. Each time that he had made this journey in the past he had enjoyed it, especially the portion between Exeter and Newton Abbott﻿—down the estuary of the Exe, past Dawlish and Teignmouth with their queer spiky, red rocks, and precipitous little cliffs running out into the blue sea, then farther on inland again through the hilly, wooded country of South Devon, where one caught unexpected glimpses of tiny, nestling villages, and of narrow lanes, winding mysteriously, between mossy, flower-spangled banks under the cool shade of overhanging trees.

===Chinese===
这位探长的乡间之行，又承天公作美，得了个好天气。其实同前几日一样，这天未免太热了些。列车在骄阳炙烤的原野上飞掠而过，坦纳便挪到过道里，好充分享受敞开的车窗间对流的风。往常走这条路线，他回回都觉得赏心悦目，尤其是埃克塞特（Exeter）到牛顿阿伯特（Newton Abbott）之间那一段——沿埃克斯河河口一路下行，经过道利什（Dawlish）和廷茅斯（Teignmouth），看那些尖刺嶙峋的红色怪岩和直插碧海的小小断崖，再折回内陆，穿行南德文丘陵起伏、林木葱茏的乡野，其间不时瞥见依偎在山坳里的小小村落，还有神秘蜿蜒的窄巷，夹在青苔斑驳、繁花点缀的路坎之间，头顶浓荫匝地，一片清凉。

===Original===
He reached Plymouth﻿—the first stop since leaving Paddington﻿—shortly before three. Changing at North Road, he boarded a branch line train after a short wait. A run of a few minutes brought him to Yelverton. Here he alighted, and when the Launceston and Princetown trains had rumbled off, he accosted the stationmaster.

===Chinese===
下午三点差一点儿，他到了普利茅斯（Plymouth）——离开帕丁顿后的第一站。他在北路站（North Road）换车，稍候片刻，登上了一条支线列车。几分钟的车程，便把他带到了耶尔弗顿。他下了车，等开往朗斯顿（Launceston）和普林斯敦（Princetown）的两班火车轰隆隆驶离之后，便走向站长搭话。

===Original===
“I am looking for a Mr. William Douglas of Myrtle Cottage,” he said. “Can you tell me where that is?”

===Chinese===
「我找一位住在桃金娘小屋的威廉·道格拉斯先生，」他说，「您能告诉我那地方在哪儿吗？」

===Original===
The stationmaster could. Myrtle Cottage, it appeared, was half a mile away on the road to Dousland, and Tanner, having received directions as to his route, set off to walk.

===Chinese===
站长自然知道。原来桃金娘小屋就在通往道斯兰（Dousland）的大路边上，离车站半英里。坦纳问明了路线，便迈步走去。

===Original===
The house was small and surrounded by trees, through which the gables showed picturesquely. It was set back some little distance from the road, a path leading through a not very well kept flower garden to the door. Mr. Douglas was evidently an apiarist, for a row of wooden hives lined each side of the path, and the hum of the insects was audible even from the road. Along the side of the garden, and passing close to the gable of the house ran a lane, from which a large gate led to a yard in the rear. This gate, Tanner noticed, was standing open.

===Chinese===
那房子不大，四围树木环抱，山墙从树影里探出，景致如画。屋子离大路稍稍退后一段，一条小径穿过一座收拾得并不怎么经心的小花园，直通门前。道格拉斯先生显然是个养蜂人，小径两旁各排着一列木质蜂箱，蜜蜂的嗡嗡声隔着大路都听得见。花园一侧有条小巷贴着屋子的山墙经过，巷子里开着一扇大门，通向屋后的院子。坦纳留意到，这扇大门正敞着。

===Original===
He walked up the path and knocked at the green-painted door. For some time there was no response, but after a second and more peremptory summons he heard footsteps approaching. The door was opened by a small man with grey hair and a beard trimmed short.

===Chinese===
他沿小径走上去，敲了敲那扇漆成绿色的门。好一阵子里头没有动静，直到他又敲了一回，敲得更加不容分说，才听见里面有脚步声走近。开门的是个小个子男人，头发花白，短须修剪得整整齐齐。

===Original===
“Got him first shot,” thought Tanner, as he politely asked for Mr. William Douglas.

===Chinese===
「一枪就中。」坦纳心里说，嘴上客客气气地求见威廉·道格拉斯先生。

===Original===
The man threw the door open.

===Chinese===
那人把门整个拉开。

===Original===
“Walk in, sir,” he said. “My brother is upstairs. I’ll call him.”

===Chinese===
「请进，先生，」他说。「我兄弟在楼上，我去叫他。」

===Original===
“Your brother?” asked Tanner sharply, as he followed his guide to a rather poorly furnished sitting room.

===Chinese===
「您兄弟？」坦纳锐声问道，一边随引路人走进一间陈设相当简陋的客厅。

===Original===
“Yes. I’m John. I’ve lived here for some years, but William is just back from America.”

===Chinese===
「是啊。我叫约翰（John），在这儿住了好些年了，威廉是刚从美国回来的。」

===Original===
Tanner nodded. He recollected the hotel porter had stated that William Douglas had spoken with an American accent, whereas this man clearly hailed from the north of England. Besides, the beard was different. The porter had mentioned a goatee, but the speaker’s was cut to a tiny point.

===Chinese===
坦纳点了点头。他记得旅馆搬运工说过，威廉·道格拉斯说话带美国口音，而眼前这人一开口分明是英格兰北部腔。再说胡子也不一样：搬运工提过那人蓄一撮山羊胡，而说话这位的胡子却修成了一个小小的尖。

===Original===
“Sit down, sir,” said the man civilly. “I’ll send my brother down.”

===Chinese===
「请坐，先生，」那人和气地说。「我这就去叫兄弟下来。」

===Original===
He indicated a chair opposite the door, and Tanner took it. From where he sat he could see the foot of the staircase, and he watched John walk to it and leisurely ascend. Presently he heard him call “William!”

===Chinese===
他指了指正对房门的一把椅子，坦纳坐下了。从这个位置望得见楼梯脚，他看着约翰踱到楼梯口，慢悠悠地拾级而上。不一会儿，听见他喊了一声「威廉！」

===Original===
A nasal voice answered, but the Inspector could not hear the words. John’s voice, now more distant, mumbled something in reply, and there was a word, apparently of assent, from the other.

===Chinese===
一个带鼻音的嗓音应了一声，说的什么探长听不清。约翰的声音这时远了些，含含糊糊回了几句，那边又应了一个字，像是答应了。

===Original===
Tanner glanced round the room. Beside the easy chair in which he sat﻿—leather lined, and very old and worn﻿—there was not much that made for comfort. A deck chair stood with its back to one of the rather small windows. In front of the other window was a table on which lay a number of books, mostly dealing with bee keeping. The floor was covered by a carpet, the worn, threadbare condition of which was brought out pitilessly by the rays of the sun which struck obliquely across it. Tanner got up and began to poke about, but without taking his eye off the bottom of the stairs. William, it was evident, was in no hurry to come down.

===Chinese===
坦纳朝屋里四下打量。除了他坐的这把安乐椅——包着皮面，又旧又破——之外，称得上舒适的东西实在不多。一把帆布躺椅背对着一扇颇小的窗户立着；另一扇窗前有张桌子，桌上摊着若干书籍，大抵是讲养蜂的。地板上铺着一条地毯，日头斜斜照在上面，把它磨得露线的地方照得一清二楚，毫不留情。坦纳站起身来随意翻看，眼睛却始终没离开楼梯脚下。看样子，威廉并不急着下来。

===Original===
Suddenly there came faintly the purr of a motor engine, and in a few seconds the sounds indicated that a car had started at no great distance away. It grew louder, and Tanner moved to the window. The sitting-room was in the gable beside the lane, and as the Inspector looked out he saw a small two-seater with one occupant pass out towards the road. But this occupant was a small man, and though his collar was turned up and his cap pulled down over his eyes, Tanner could see he had a grey beard.

===Chinese===
忽然，隐隐传来一阵汽车引擎的低吼，不出几秒钟，那声响便表明一辆汽车在不远处发动了。声音越来越响，坦纳移步到窗前。客厅正在临巷的那面山墙里，探长朝外一望，只见一辆小小的双座车载着一个人朝大路驶去。可车上那人是个小个子，领子竖着，帽檐压到眼上，坦纳仍看得出他蓄着花白胡子。

===Original===
He stood for a moment wondering how John had got downstairs without having been seen. Then, as the house seemed strangely quiet, an idea flashed into his mind, and he ran to the stairs and called, “Anyone there?” There was no answer, and with a sudden feeling of foreboding, he raced up. Three rooms opened off a short landing, and the doors being open, he glanced into each in turn. They were all empty!

===Chinese===
他站了片刻，纳闷约翰是怎么下楼出去的，竟没让他瞧见。随即他发觉屋里静得出奇，一个念头闪过脑际，他奔到楼梯口喊道：「有人吗？」无人应声。他心头陡然涌起一阵不祥之感，三步并作两步冲上楼去。短短的楼梯平台上开着三个房门，门都敞着，他挨个往里扫了一眼。三个房间全空着！

===Original===
A casement window on the landing was open, and as Tanner looked out, he saw what had been done. About three feet below the sill was the roof of a low shed. Nothing could be easier than to step out of the window on to the roof, and drop to the ground. The open door of the outhouse to which led many wheel tracks showed where the motor had been kept.

===Chinese===
平台上有一扇窗开着，坦纳探头一望，便明白了是怎么回事。窗台下面约三英尺就是一间矮棚的棚顶。跨出窗子踏上棚顶、再跳到地上，再容易不过了。附属小屋的门敞着，遍地辙印通向那里，可见汽车原先就存放在那儿。

===Original===
Tanner swore savagely. Never before had he been so completely and so easily duped. It was now evident to him that William Douglas had recognised him approaching the house, and had invented a brother to enable him to hold the Inspector’s attention while he bolted. And he had played his cards skilfully! Ruefully the Inspector had to admire the trick, though he surmised it had been worked out beforehand in view of just such an emergency.

===Chinese===
坦纳狠狠地咒骂起来。他还从来没有被人这样彻头彻尾、这样轻巧地耍弄过。这下事情清楚了：威廉·道格拉斯认出了走近宅子的他，便凭空捏造出一个兄弟，好拖住探长的注意力，自己趁机脱身。而且这一手玩得真漂亮！探长虽是懊恼，也不得不佩服这招数，只是他揣测，这一手只怕是那人预先想好、专备这种万一的。

===Original===
“He’ll not get far,” the angry man growled, as he prepared to follow. But, thinking a moment or two would now make little difference, he turned his steps instead to the kitchen. There on a shelf, as he had expected, were three or four pairs of boots. Drawing from his pocket a tracing of the marks on the Cranshaw River bank, he eagerly compared it with the soles. Those of the first pair he took up corresponded! Here was proof, if proof were required. William Douglas had been at the Luce Manor boathouse on the night of the murder!

===Chinese===
「他跑不远。」怒气冲冲的探长低吼一声，准备追出去。可转念一想，此刻多耽搁一两分钟已无关紧要，便迈步进了厨房。架子上果然如他所料摆着三四双靴子。他从口袋里掏出克兰肖河岸脚印的描样，急切地同靴底一一比对。拿起来的头一双就对上了！铁证，如果要铁证的话，这就是了。案发当晚，威廉·道格拉斯到过卢斯庄园的船屋！

===Original===
Seizing a small handbag he had noticed in the sitting room, the Inspector packed the boots, then, after closing the windows, and locking the yard gates and the house doors, he hurried back along the road towards Yelverton. Inquiring for the local telephone call office, he rang up the Plymouth police authorities, describing, so far as he was able, the man and the car, and asking them to have a ring formed round the locality. Hastening on to the Yelverton police station, he told the sergeant what had occurred, and handing him the keys of the cottage, instructed him to take charge, and to make a thorough search of the premises.

===Chinese===
探长抓起他在客厅里留意到的一只小提包，把靴子装了进去，然后关好窗户，锁上院门和屋门，匆匆沿大路赶回耶尔弗顿。他打听出本地电话营业处的所在，挂电话给普利茅斯警方，把那人和汽车的模样尽力描述了一番，请他们调人把那一带围起来。接着又赶到耶尔弗顿警察所，把经过告诉警佐，把小屋的钥匙交到他手里，吩咐他接管此处，并对宅子作彻底搜查。

===Original===
He learned that a train left for Plymouth in a few minutes, and travelling by it, he soon reached the police headquarters of that city. Here he was met by a superintendent, and the two men discussed the affair in detail.

===Chinese===
他打听到几分钟后就有一班去普利茅斯的火车，坐上这班车，不多时便到了该市的警察总部。一位警司（superintendent）迎住他，两人把这件事详细商量了一遍。

===Original===
“I have done, I think, everything possible,” the Superintendent concluded. “All the stations at a radius of about twenty miles or more have been advised, and the roads will be watched from Looe and Liskeard round by Launceston, Okehampton, and Moreton Hamstead, to Newton Abbott. All trains and steamers, as far as possible, will be examined before departure, and the railway people at the smaller stations will be advised. I don’t think he’ll make for Cornwall, you know. It’s too much of a dead end. He will either go east in his car, or come to Plymouth and try the trains, or even more likely, the steamers.”

===Chinese===
「能做的，我想我都做了，」警司最后总结道。「方圆大约二十英里开外的所有车站都通知到了，各条大路也有人监视，从卢港（Looe）、利斯卡德（Liskeard）一路绕经朗斯顿、奥克汉普顿（Okehampton）、莫顿汉普斯特德（Moreton Hamstead），直到牛顿阿伯特。所有火车和轮船，凡来得及的，出发前一律检查，沿线小站的铁路人员也会得到通知。依我看，他不会往康沃尔（Cornwall）跑——那地方太像条死胡同了。他要么开汽车往东去，要么来普利茅斯碰火车，再不然——这个可能性更大——碰轮船。」

===Original===
“That is my own view,” Tanner returned. “I suppose there’s nothing to be done now but wait for information?”

===Chinese===
「我也是这么看的，」坦纳答道。「眼下除了等消息，恐怕没有别的事可做了吧？」

===Original===
“I think we’ll hear something before long. If you haven’t had a meal, I should get it while you have the chance. The Dartmoor Arms, a few doors away, is quite good, and I’ll send for you if there is news.”

===Chinese===
「我想用不了多久就会有信儿。您要是还没用饭，趁这机会赶紧去吃。「达特穆尔纹章」（Dartmoor Arms）就在几步开外，挺不错，一有消息我立刻派人去请您。」

===Original===
As this seemed sound advice, Tanner followed it. But he had not finished his hastily served dinner when he was sent for. News had come in.

===Chinese===
这话说得在理，坦纳照办了。可他那顿匆匆端上来的晚饭还没吃完，就有人来请他了。消息来了。

===Original===
“I have a wire from the Tavistock men,” the Superintendent explained. “A car answering your description has just been found abandoned in a lane about quarter of a mile on the Yelverton side of Tavistock. Evidently your man wouldn’t risk taking it through the town.”

===Chinese===
「塔维斯托克（Tavistock）方面来了电报，」警司解释道。「一辆同您描述相符的汽车，刚被人发现丢弃在塔维斯托克靠耶尔弗顿一侧约四分之一英里的一条小巷里。看来您那位先生不敢冒险把车开进镇子。」

===Original===
“Then he must be there himself.”

===Chinese===
「那么他本人准在镇上。」

===Original===
“Unless he got away by rail. What time did you say he left Yelverton?”

===Chinese===
「除非他坐火车走了。您说他几点离开耶尔弗顿的？」

===Original===
“About quarter-past four, or slightly later.”

===Chinese===
「大约四点一刻，兴许还稍晚一点。」

===Original===
“From Yelverton to Tavistock is not more than about five miles. He would do it easily in fifteen minutes. Say he would reach Tavistock between half past four and quarter to five.” The Superintendent picked up a *Bradshaw*. “Here we are. By the Great Western there’s a 5:27 for Plymouth and a 6:02 for Launceston. Now for the South-Western. There’s a 5:22, and a 7:50 for Exeter. He’s gone either by that 5:27 to Plymouth or the 5:22 to Exeter, and I should say the latter.”

===Chinese===
「耶尔弗顿到塔维斯托克不过五英里上下，他一刻钟便能开到。就算他四点半到四点三刻之间到塔维斯托克吧。」警司拿起一本《布拉德肖》（Bradshaw）时刻表。「有了。大西方铁路（Great Western）有一班 5:27 去普利茅斯，一班 6:02 去朗斯顿。再看西南铁路（South-Western）：有一班 5:22，还有一班 7:50 去埃克塞特。他不是坐那班 5:27 去了普利茅斯，就是坐 5:22 去了埃克塞特——要我说，是后者。」

===Original===
“It seems likely. Would your men have reached the stations before those trains left?”

===Chinese===
「看来是这样。您的人来得及在那两班车开走之前赶到车站吗？」

===Original===
The Superintendent shook his head.

===Chinese===
警司摇了摇头。

===Original===
“It’s just possible,” he answered, “but I hardly think so. Your phone was received at”﻿—he referred to a paper﻿—“4:42. Orders were issued immediately, but considering telegraphic delays, they were probably not received at Tavistock till five or slightly after. The men would then have to be collected and instructed. They might have seen those trains out, but it’s unlikely.”

===Chinese===
「也许碰巧来得及，」他答道，「不过我看悬。您的电话——」他看了一眼纸条——「是 4:42 接到的。命令随即发出，可算上电报周转的工夫，塔维斯托克那边多半要到五点或五点过后才收到。然后还得把人集合起来交代任务。他们或许能赶上那两班车出站，但可能性不大。」

===Original===
“Well, I’ll go on to Tavistock now anyway,” Tanner decided. “I presume you will have those trains searched?”

===Chinese===
「好，那我现在就去塔维斯托克，」坦纳当机立断。「那两班车，您会安排搜查吧？」

===Original===
“Of course. I issued a new set of orders immediately. Both trains will be carefully examined, and the country all about Tavistock will be scoured. We are well accustomed to that,” the Superintendent added with a grim smile.

===Chinese===
「当然。我已经又发了一套命令。两班车都要仔细检查，塔维斯托克四乡也要梳篦一遍。这种事我们是熟门熟路了。」警司补了一句，脸上带着冷冷的笑。

===Original===
“The Princetown convicts? I suppose you are,” answered Tanner, as with a brief word of farewell he withdrew.

===Chinese===
「普林斯敦的越狱犯？难怪你们是行家，」坦纳应道，简短道了声别，便退了出去。

===Original===
There being no train by either line for some little time, Tanner took a car. As they climbed the long, slow incline to Yelverton, out of the relaxing, enervating Plymouth air, he felt himself growing fresher and more energetic. He was grimly determined not to rest till he had laid his hands on the man who had duped him. From merely professional, the matter had become personal. Tanner’s pride was involved. No one, he swore, should play him such a trick and get off with it.

===Chinese===
两条线一时半会儿都没有车，坦纳便雇了辆汽车。车子爬上通往耶尔弗顿的那道漫长而徐缓的坡道，摆脱了普利茅斯那种让人筋酥骨软的空气，他觉得自己渐渐精神抖擞起来。他横下一条心：不把那个耍了他的人抓到手，决不罢休。这件事已不再单纯是公务，而成了他个人的事。坦纳的自尊心给牵动了。他发誓，谁敢这样耍他，就休想这么算了。

===Original===
They slipped quietly through the fifteen or sixteen miles of charmingly wooded country, dropping into Tavistock as the shadows began to lengthen across the road. The sergeant had been advised of Tanner’s arrival, and was expecting him. Together they ran back and examined the abandoned car. Though they found nothing directly helpful, Tanner felt sure it was the one he had seen from the sitting room at Myrtle Cottage.

===Chinese===
汽车悄然穿过十五六英里林木宜人的乡野，等影子开始在路面上拉长时，便滑进了塔维斯托克。警佐已得到坦纳要来的通知，正候着他。两人赶回去查看了那辆弃置的汽车。虽然没发现什么直接有用的东西，坦纳却认定，这正是他从桃金娘小屋客厅窗口望见的那一辆。

===Original===
He turned to his companion.

===Chinese===
他转向同伴。

===Original===
“Did you hear about this in time to examine the Plymouth and Exeter trains at 5:27 and 5:22?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「这件事你们得知时，来得及检查 5:27 的普利茅斯车和 5:22 的埃克塞特车吗？」他问。

===Original===
The other shook his head.

===Chinese===
对方摇了摇头。

===Original===
“No, sir, I’m sorry to say we did not. But I have since made inquiries. No one with a grey beard was seen at either station. At the Great Western Station four persons booked, all third single to Plymouth, but the clerk remembers one of these was a young sailor and the others women. At the South-Western Station two tickets were issued to Exeter, one a first to Major Reading, who lives here, the other a third single to a little, elderly, clean-shaven man. Our men were there within ten minutes of the train’s departure, so that’s how the clerks remembered﻿—between that and there being so few bookings.”

===Chinese===
「没有，先生，很遗憾没来得及。不过我事后查问过了。两个车站都没人见过蓄灰胡子的。大西方车站有四个人买票，全是去普利茅斯的三等单程，但售票员记得其中一个是年轻水兵，其余是女人。西南车站卖出去两张去埃克塞特的票：一张头等，是给住在本地的里丁（Reading）少校的；另一张三等单程，买主是个小个子、上了年纪、胡子刮得干干净净的人。车开之后十分钟内我们的人就到了站上，售票员就是这么记起来的——再加上那天卖出的票本来就少。」

===Original===
“A small, elderly, clean-shaven man, sergeant? Let us go round the Tavistock barbers.”

===Chinese===
「小个子、上了年纪、胡子刮得干干净净，警佐？咱们去把塔维斯托克的理发店兜一圈吧。」

===Original===
The sergeant looked up sharply.

===Chinese===
警佐猛地抬起头来。

===Original===
“By Jove! sir, a likely enough ruse,” he cried. “It won’t take long to find out﻿—there are only three.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，先生，这花招十有八九是这么回事！」他叫道。「查清楚用不了多久——统共才三家店。」

===Original===
They ran back to the little town, and at the first barber’s learned that a small, elderly man with a short grey beard and moustache had called at a few minutes before five, and had had his beard and moustache shaved off.

===Chinese===
两人赶回小镇，在第一家理发店就打听到：五点差几分的时候，有个小个子、上了年纪的人来过，蓄着花白的短髭短须，当场就把胡子和髭都刮掉了。

===Original===
“Now to the telegraph office. We’ll have him before long.”

===Chinese===
「这就去电报局。用不了多久就能抓住他了。」

===Original===
The Inspector sent messages to Plymouth, to Exeter, and to some of the principal stations beyond, explaining that the bearded man of the previous wires had had himself shaved. Then he looked at his watch.

===Chinese===
探长向普利茅斯、埃克塞特以及再往前去的几个大站拍发电报，说明前电所述的蓄须男子已经剃了须。然后他看了看表。

===Original===
“Quarter to eight. Can I catch the 7:50? Phone to hold it while I run across.”

===Chinese===
「八点差一刻。7:50 那班赶得上吗？打个电话让他们等一下，我马上跑过去。」

===Original===
He jumped into the car and drove to the South-Western Station. There he caught the train for Exeter with a minute to spare.

===Chinese===
他跳上汽车，驶到西南车站，赶上去埃克塞特的火车时，还富余一分钟。

===Original===
He leaned back in the corner of a first-class compartment, and slowly drew out and lit a cigar, while he turned over in his mind the next step to be taken. He thought that at all events he should go on to Exeter. The 5:22 from Tavistock, by which Douglas had travelled, reached that city before his wire about the shaving had been sent out. Therefore it was hardly likely that the man would have been detained en route. Tanner, of course, recognised that a freshly shaven chin was unmistakable, but he did not think a village constable would have the sharpness to deduce what Douglas might have done, and act accordingly. But from Exeter in what direction would the quarry head?

===Chinese===
他往头等车厢的角落里一靠，慢条斯理地掏出一支雪茄点上，一边在心里盘算下一步棋。他想，无论如何总得赶到埃克塞特去。道格拉斯坐的那班 5:22 从塔维斯托克开出的车，在剃须电报发出之前就到了埃克塞特，所以此人在半道被截下的可能性微乎其微。当然，坦纳明白，一张刚刮过的下巴是一眼便认得出的，可他不认为哪个乡村警员有那么灵的头脑，能推想出道格拉斯可能干了什么并照此处置。只是，到了埃克塞特，这只猎物又会往哪个方向去呢？

===Original===
There seemed two possibilities. Probably he would try either to reach London, or to get abroad. London, as Tanner knew, was perhaps the safest place in the world for a criminal to lie hidden. But many ill-doers had an overwhelming desire to put as great a distance as possible between themselves and the scene of their misdeeds. If Douglas were of this class he would try to get out of the country, and if, as the hotel porter had stated, he spoke like an American, would he not be likely to try to reach the country in which he might most easily pass for a native? There was, of course, no means of knowing, but at least it was clear that the approaches to London as well as the ports should be closely watched.

===Chinese===
看来有两种可能。他多半是要么设法去伦敦，要么设法出国。据坦纳所知，伦敦也许是世界上罪犯藏身的最佳去处。但也有许多作恶之人生怕躲得不够远，恨不得离作案地点越远越好。倘若道格拉斯属于这一类，他就会设法逃出国境；而如果真像旅馆搬运工说的那样，他一口美国腔，那他岂不是多半要奔那个最容易冒充本地人的国家去？这当然无从断定，但至少有一点是明白的：进伦敦的各条路口和各个口岸都得严加监视。

===Original===
In any case, whatever the fugitive’s goal, he would be almost certain to pass through Exeter. It was true he could double back to Plymouth, but the probabilities were he would keep away from the district in which he was known. As Tanner’s train ran into St. David’s Station, Exeter, he felt sure his victim was not far before him.

===Chinese===
无论如何，不管逃犯的目标是哪儿，他几乎必定要经过埃克塞特。当然他也可能折回普利茅斯，但按情理推断，他会躲开自己容易被人认出的那一带。当坦纳乘坐的火车驶入埃克塞特的圣大卫车站（St. David's）时，他确信他的猎物就在前方不远。

===Original===
A tall efficient looking sergeant of police was waiting on the platform. This man, sharply scrutinising the alighting travellers, promptly fixed on Tanner.

===Chinese===
站台上等着一位身材高大、看上去很干练的警佐。此人目光锐利地审视着下车旅客，一眼便盯住了坦纳。

===Original===
“Inspector Tanner, sir?” he questioned, and as the other nodded, continued, “they phoned us from Plymouth you were coming through on this train. We have inquiries in hand both here and at Queen Street, the other station. So far we have heard nothing of your man.”

===Chinese===
「是坦纳探长吗，先生？」他问。见对方点头，便接着说：「普利茅斯方面打电话来，说您搭这班车过来。我们这边和另一座车站女王街（Queen Street）都在查访。到目前为止，还没有您那位的音信。」

===Original===
“What exactly are you doing?”

===Chinese===
「你们具体都在做些什么？」

===Original===
“We have a man at each station working the staffs﻿—booking-clerks, ticket collectors, porters, refreshment rooms﻿—the usual thing. Another man is going round the hotels, another the restaurants open at that hour, and another the garages, in case he might have gone on by car. Is there any other line you would wish taken up?”

===Chinese===
「每座车站各派一人，把站内员工都过一遍——售票员、收票员、搬运工、小卖部——照例行事。另有一人正挨家跑旅馆，一人跑那个钟点还开门的饭馆，再有一人跑各处车库，以防他改乘汽车走了。您还有什么别的线索要查？」

===Original===
“Why no, sergeant. I think you have covered all the ground. Have you advised your men that the fellow got shaved?”

===Chinese===
「没有了，警佐。我看方方面面你们都顾到了。你们通知到弟兄们那家伙刮了胡子吗？」

===Original===
“Some of them, sir; some of them we couldn’t get hold of. We advise them as we can get in touch with them.”

===Chinese===
「通知了一部分，先生；有几个一时抓不着人。能联络上的，随联络随通知。」

===Original===
Tanner nodded again.

===Chinese===
坦纳又点了点头。

===Original===
“Well, we had better go to headquarters and wait for news.”

===Chinese===
「那么，我们最好去总部等消息。」

===Original===
For a considerable time Tanner remained, chafing and impatient, until, just as eleven was booming from the town clocks, a constable appeared accompanied by a tall, fair-haired young man in a leather coat and breeches, and a peaked cap. The latter explained that he was a taxi owner, driving his own vehicle, and he believed he knew something that might be of value.

===Chinese===
坦纳焦躁难耐地等了好一阵子，直到镇上的时钟当当敲响十一点，一名警员领着一个人进来——那是个高个子金发青年，穿皮上衣皮马裤，戴一顶鸭舌帽。来人自称是出租汽车车主，开的是自己的车，还说相信自己知道一件也许要紧的事。

===Original===
It appeared that he had been at St. David’s Station when Douglas’s train had come in. He was engaged by a small, elderly, clean-shaven man with grey hair, dressed in a tweed overcoat and a cloth cap. The man seemed nervous and excited, and told him to drive to any ready-made clothes store which would be open at that hour. He took him to a shop in the poorer part of the town. The man went in, returning in a few minutes dressed in a soft, grey felt hat and a khaki coloured waterproof, and carrying a bundle. He reentered the taxi and told the driver “Queen Street Station as quick as you can.” He drove there, and the man paid him and hurried into the station, and that was all he knew.

===Chinese===
据他说，道格拉斯那班车进圣大卫车站时，他正在站上候生意。一个上了年纪的小个子雇了他，灰白头发，胡子刮得干干净净，穿粗花呢大衣、戴布帽。那人神色紧张而兴奋，叫他开到任何一家那个钟点还开门的成衣店去。他把那人拉到了镇上穷街的一家铺子。那人进去，几分钟后出来，头上换了一顶灰色软毡帽，身上换了件卡其色雨衣，手里还提着个包袱。他重新钻进汽车，吩咐司机：「去女王街车站，越快越好。」他开到那里，那人付了钱，急匆匆进了车站——他知道的就是这么多。

===Original===
“What time did you reach Queen Street?” asked Tanner.

===Chinese===
「你们到女王街是几点？」坦纳问。

===Original===
“Going on to half-past seven.”

===Chinese===
「七点半还差一点儿。」

===Original===
“We’d better go to Queen Street and find out what trains leave about that hour.”

===Chinese===
「咱们最好去女王街一趟，查查那个钟点前后有些什么车开出。」

===Original===
Their visitor’s car was waiting outside, and engaging it, they drove rapidly off.

===Chinese===
来客的汽车就在外面候着，两人雇了它，风驰电掣地去了。

===Original===
For those who do not know Exeter, it may be explained that the Great Western and London and South-Western Railways, both running from London to Plymouth, form a gigantic figure 8, the centre where the lines cross being St. David’s Station, Exeter. In the same town, but a mile nearer London on the South-Western, is Queen Street Station. While therefore St. David’s is joint between the two Companies, Queen Street is South-Western only, and these facts seemed to indicate to Tanner the probability that Douglas was going for a South-Western train bound Londonwards.

===Chinese===
给不熟悉埃克塞特的人交代一句：大西方铁路和伦敦与西南铁路（London and South-Western）都从伦敦通到普利茅斯，两线恰巧围成一个巨大的 8 字，线路交叉的中心点，就是埃克塞特的圣大卫车站。同在一城的女王街车站在西南线上，只是朝伦敦方向近了一英里。因此圣大卫站归两公司共用，女王街站却纯属西南铁路一家——在坦纳看来，这些情形表明：道格拉斯多半要赶西南铁路开往伦敦方向的车。

===Original===
A glance at the time table at Queen Street supported this view. A train left for London at 7:32.

===Chinese===
到女王街朝时刻表上一扫，这个推断便坐实了：7:32 有一班开往伦敦的车。

===Original===
“Your constable saw the booking-clerk, I suppose?” Tanner asked.

===Chinese===
「你们那位警员问过售票员了吧？」坦纳问。

===Original===
“Yes, sir. But of course he gave the wrong description. He did not know the man had changed his cap and coat.”

===Chinese===
「问过了，先生。可他报的模样自然不对。他不知道那人已经换了帽子和上衣。」

===Original===
“That’s true,” Tanner assented. “We had better see him again.”

===Chinese===
「这倒是，」坦纳附和。「我们最好再见他一面。」

===Original===
The booking office was closed and the clerk had gone home. With considerable difficulty they obtained his address from a watchman. Then stepping into their waiting taxi, they were driven to it.

===Chinese===
售票处已经落闸，售票员回家了。他们费了好大劲，才从一个守夜人嘴里问出住址，随即钻进候着的出租汽车，直驰而去。

===Original===
The house was in darkness, but their third thunderous knock produced a sleepy and indignant householder. Tanner, who was a past-master in the art, soothed his ruffled feelings, and he brought them in and civilly asked their business.

===Chinese===
那宅子黑着灯，可他们擂到第三通门，擂出了一个睡意未醒、满肚子不痛快的户主。坦纳是抚慰人心的老手，三言两语就把他的火气捋顺了。他把两人让进屋，客客气气地问有何贵干。

===Original===
“You have been troubled about this before, I’m afraid,” the Inspector began. “I shall explain the affair in a word and you’ll see its importance. A murder has been committed, and we have traced the suspected man to Queen Street Station. He drove up in a great hurry just before half-past seven this evening, and we imagine he must have travelled by the 7:32. Now you will see why we want your help. If you can recall the man and recollect where he booked to, it would be of material assistance to us.”

===Chinese===
「这事怕是已经打扰过您一回了，」探长开口道。「我用一句话就能把事情讲明，您就知道它干系重大。出了一桩命案，我们已经把嫌疑犯追到了女王街车站。今晚七点半前，他火急火燎赶到那里，我们料定他坐的是 7:32 那班车。这下您明白我们为什么要请您帮忙了。您若能记起那个人、想起他把票买到哪里，对我们就是实实在在的大助。」

===Original===
“A clean-shaven man in a brownish cap and coat?” the clerk replied. “But I have already answered that. I saw no one so dressed.”

===Chinese===
「一个刮了胡子、戴棕色帽子穿棕色上衣的人？」售票员答道。「可这个我已经答复过了。我没见过这样穿着的人。」

===Original===
“We have just discovered that he had bought a waterproof and a grey felt hat. Can you recall him now?”

===Chinese===
「我们刚刚查明，他买了一身雨衣和一顶灰色毡帽。这会儿想得起来了吗？」

===Original===
The clerk made a sudden gesture.

===Chinese===
售票员猛然做了个手势。

===Original===
“Why yes, I can,” he cried excitedly, “I remarked him because he was in such a fuss, and I told him he was time enough. I should have thought of it when the constable asked me, but the description put me off.”

===Chinese===
「哎，对，想起来了！」他兴奋地叫道。「这个人我留意过，就因为他慌成那样，我还劝他说时间尽够。警员来问的时候我本该想起他来的，可那副描述把我带偏了。」

===Original===
“Quite naturally,” Tanner assured him smoothly, “but now if you can tell us where he booked to, you’ll do us a very great service.”

===Chinese===
「那是自然，」坦纳话音圆熟地宽慰他，「不过这会儿您要是能告诉我们他票买到哪里，就是帮了我们大忙。」

===Original===
“I can do so. His excitement drew my attention to him. He took a third single to Southampton.”

===Chinese===
「说得上来。他那副慌张劲儿把我的注意引了去。他买了一张去南安普敦（Southampton）的三等单程票。」

===Original===
“Southampton! Just as I expected,” exclaimed Tanner. “Making for the ships!”

===Chinese===
「南安普敦！果然不出我所料，」坦纳失声道。「奔船去了！」

===Original===
The other nodded and Tanner went on:

===Chinese===
对方点头，坦纳接着说：

===Original===
“Where would he get to from there? Would he catch the night boat for Havre?”

===Chinese===
「他从那儿再往哪儿去？会不会赶阿弗尔（Havre）的夜班船？」

===Original===
“No,” answered the clerk as he fetched a time table and rapidly turned over the leaves. “The 7:32 gets to Salisbury at 10:52, and there’s a train on to Southampton Town at 11:00. It doesn’t go to the Harbour. But the connection at Eastleigh is bad, and you don’t get to Southampton till 12:30. The Havre boat leaves at 11:30.”

===Chinese===
「不会，」售票员答道，一边取来一本时刻表飞快地翻着。「7:32 那班 10:52 到索尔兹伯里（Salisbury），11:00 有车续开南安普敦城站，不进码头。可伊斯特利（Eastleigh）的接续很糟，到南安普敦要拖到 12:30。去阿弗尔的船 11:30 开。」

===Original===
“And what time do you get to Eastleigh?”

===Chinese===
「那到伊斯特利是几点？」

===Original===
“11:37.”

===Chinese===
「11:37。」

===Original===
“And from there to Southampton is how far?”

===Chinese===
「从那儿到南安普敦有多远？」

===Original===
“About seven miles to the docks.”

===Chinese===
「到码头约莫七英里。」

===Original===
“So that if he had taken a motor at Eastleigh he could have been there by midnight?”

===Chinese===
「这么说，他要是在伊斯特利上了汽车，午夜前就能赶到那儿了？」

===Original===
“Yes, I should say about that.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，我看差不多。」

===Original===
Tanner looked at his watch. It was five minutes to twelve. In from five to thirty-five minutes Douglas would probably reach Southampton. Would there be time to intercept him there?

===Chinese===
坦纳看了看表：十二点差五分。再过五分钟到三十五分钟，道格拉斯多半就到南安普敦了。还来得及在那里截住他吗？

===Original===
Hastily thanking the clerk, the two men jumped once more into their taxi and drove to the police station. There the Inspector hurried to the telephone to call up the Southampton police. But there was a delay in getting through. For thirty minutes he fumed and fretted. Then at half-past twelve he got his connection.

===Chinese===
两人匆匆谢过售票员，又一头跳上出租汽车，直驶警察所。探长抢步奔向电话，接南安普敦警方。偏偏线路耽搁，他急火攻心，足足熬了三十分钟，到十二点半才接通。

===Original===
“I’m afraid the train will be in,” replied the distant voice, “but if it’s late we’ll get your man if he’s on it. If we miss him there, we’ll go on to the Docks. There’s a Union Castle liner due out at five o’clock. He may be going for that. What about the warrant?”

===Chinese===
「恐怕火车已经进站了，」远方的声音答道，「不过要是车晚点，只要人在车上，我们就能截住。万一站上漏了，我们就赶到码头去。有一班联盟城堡公司（Union Castle）的班轮定于五点开出，他多半是奔那条船去的。逮捕令怎么办？」

===Original===
“Hold on a minute,” said Tanner, then turning to the sergeant, he spoke rapidly:

===Chinese===
「请稍等片刻，」坦纳说，随即转向警佐，急促地问：

===Original===
“A liner leaves Southampton at five for South Africa. Can I get there with a good car? There are no trains, of course?”

===Chinese===
「五点有一班轮船从南安普敦开往南非。坐一辆好汽车赶得到吗？火车当然是没有的？」

===Original===
“None, sir. It’s about a hundred miles and you should do thirty miles an hour﻿—say three and a half hours. If you left here at 1:00, you should be there by 4:30.”

===Chinese===
「没有，先生。大约一百英里，照一小时三十英里算——就算三个半钟头吧。您 1:00 从这儿动身，4:30 前就能到。」

===Original===
“I’ll do that.” Then turning back to the telephone: “I’m leaving here now by road for Southampton. You may expect me at the Union Castle berth about 4:30. I’ll have the warrant.”

===Chinese===
「就这么办。」说罢又转回电话：「我现在就由公路赶南安普敦。大约 4:30 到联盟城堡公司的泊位。逮捕令我随身带着。」

===Original===
The taxi-driver they had been employing being unfamiliar with the surrounding country, they drove to the nearest garage and after some difficulty succeeded in knocking up a sleepy manager and hiring a powerful car and a man who knew the road, at least as far as Salisbury. But there were delays in getting away, and though the manager did his best, it was nearly half-past one when the big vehicle swung out of Exeter, eastward bound.

===Chinese===
先前雇的那位出租司机不熟悉四乡道路，他们便开到最近的一家汽车行，费了不少周折才敲醒一位睡眼惺忪的经理，雇到一辆大马力汽车和一个识路的司机——至少认得到索尔兹伯里的路。可是动身又耽搁了一阵，尽管经理尽力张罗，等这辆大车拐出埃克塞特、向东驶去时，已快到一点半了。

===Original===
The night was fine but dark. As they purred swiftly along the smooth road, Tanner lay back on the comfortable cushions and let the cool air blow in on his heated forehead, while he took stock of the position.

===Chinese===
夜色晴好，只是很黑。汽车在平整的路面上低鸣疾驰，坦纳靠在舒适的坐垫上，任凉风吹拂滚烫的额头，一边掂量着眼下的局面。

===Original===
He was perfectly aware that he might be on a wild-goose chase. The taking of the ticket to Southampton might have been a blind, and Douglas might not have done the obvious thing in making a bolt to the most convenient port. After the ruse the man had employed at Myrtle Cottage, Tanner felt he would not do the obvious thing unless he was impelled to it by some strong consideration. But such a consideration existed. There was the element of time. The man would realise that on such a journey he must inevitably be traced, but he would hardly imagine he could be traced in time. Before his pursuers could reach Southampton he would count on having been able to adopt a new personality, and put hundreds of miles of sea between himself and them. The more Tanner thought over this possibility, the more likely it seemed. If he were in Douglas’s position it was the view he himself would have taken.

===Chinese===
他心里雪亮：这一趟很可能是白追一场。买那张去南安普敦的票也许是虚晃一枪，道格拉斯未必会循着人人想得到的路数，直奔最方便的口岸。自打见识了那人在桃金娘小屋使的花招，坦纳认定此人若没有压倒一切的理由，决不会走人人想得到的路。可这样的理由偏偏存在——时间就是一切。那人心里明白，这样一趟旅程，事后必定会被追查出来，但他料想追查决计赶不上他的脚步。等追兵到南安普敦时，他满以为自己早已改头换面，同追捕者之间隔着几百英里的海路了。坦纳越琢磨这个可能，越觉得像那么回事。他若处在道格拉斯的位置，打的也会是同样的算盘。

===Original===
They were running well. Tanner watched the whirling hedges, lit up by the strong headlights, and blurred by the speed into quivering smudges, and judged they must be doing well on to forty miles an hour. It was, of course, breaking the law; moreover, it was by no means safe, but Tanner did not let such considerations weigh against the chance of checkmating the man who had duped him. He had informed the chauffeur he would be responsible if there was trouble.

===Chinese===
车跑得很顺。坦纳望着车窗外旋卷而过的树篱，被强烈的头灯照亮，又被车速搅成一团团颤动的模糊影子，估摸着时速准将近四十英里。这自然是在违犯法律，况且也决说不上安全，可是一想到能有把握堵死那个耍了他的人，坦纳便不容这种顾虑压倒机会。他已向司机言明，出了岔子一概由他担待。

===Original===
He fell to reckoning distances. He was not very well up in the geography of the district, but he knew there were two roads, north through Yeovil and Salisbury, and south through Dorchester and Poole. He imagined neither of these was quite direct, but he did not know if there was a good road lying between them.

===Chinese===
他又盘算起路程来。这一带的地理他不很在行，只知道有两条路：北边一条穿约维尔（Yeovil）、索尔兹伯里，南边一条穿多切斯特（Dorchester）、普尔（Poole）。他估摸着哪条都不算直路，至于两路之间还有没有一条好道，他就不得而知了。

===Original===
In about half an hour they slackened for a town, after which the road rose for some miles. Then in half an hour more it fell again and they ran through another town, whose name appeared on several buildings﻿—Chard. “The Salisbury Road,” thought Tanner. Forty minutes later they left Yeovil behind and at 4:10, nearly three hours after leaving Exeter, they turned out of Salisbury on the Southampton road.

===Chinese===
约莫半个钟头后，车子为过一座镇子放慢了速度，随后一路上坡了好几英里。又过半小时，路再度下坡，他们穿过另一座镇子，好几幢房子上都标着镇名——查德（Chard）。「走的是索尔兹伯里大道。」坦纳心想。四十分钟后，他们把约维尔甩在身后；到 4:10，离开埃克塞特将近三个小时，他们拐出索尔兹伯里，上了去南安普敦的路。

===Original===
“Not bad going,” thought Tanner. “If we can keep it up we should be at the boat at twenty to five.”

===Chinese===
「跑得不算慢，」坦纳想。「照这个速度撑下去，五点差二十就该到船边了。」

===Original===
But alas! the driver’s knowledge of the road which had served them so well up to Salisbury, now failed them. They had to reduce speed at crossroads and run more cautiously. Fortunately, it was now fairly light, or their progress would have been still slower.

===Chinese===
可惜！司机的认路本领到索尔兹伯里就到了头，先前帮了大忙，如今不管用了。到十字路口不得不减速，跑得更加小心。幸而这会儿天色已颇亮，不然还要更慢。

===Original===
Tanner, was getting nervous. It was going to be a near thing. He held his watch in his hand and counted the mileposts as one after another they dropped behind. Now it was half-past four, and still they had nine miles to go.

===Chinese===
